Three universes answer it three ways. |
| CH20 | **Transmutation Practice** | STANDARD | Early Modern | CH19 (Substance-Elemental validated) + CH01 (Alchemy) | **Grants MATTER_THEORY — the Elemental Codex**, as Five-Phase Theory (CH22) does for Process worlds; every prerequisite naming the Periodic Table (CH06) reads this token. **Prima Materia**ⁿ — a new *stockpiled* resource (formless temperament; `Stockpiled`, never placed on the map). **Transmutation Hall**ⁿ [**national wonder** — one per civilisation; 300; maintenance 2; ⟨Provides [6] [Prima Materia]⟩; Scientist ×1 — no production at all: the Hall makes matter, not goods, and its two Gold of upkeep are the alchemists who are not at the forge. One Hall, not one per city: transmutation is a capital industry, not an empire-wide income — the physics calls it ruinous by hand, and the mine-head Vats, the Athanors and Dispersal are how it scales. (Verified in `TurnManager.kt`: each turn a civilisation gains its resource *supply* of every stockpiled resource, and `Provides` counts toward supply — so a building yields a stockpile directly, with no yield to convert. A negative production stat would also work — the engine sums building stats without a floor — but upkeep says the same thing more legibly, and specialists cannot yield stockpiles natively, so an Alchemist slot would need a further extension.) The loss the physics demands sits in the *re-locking*.] **Where matter comes from, and how much:** the Hall (6 a turn, one per civilisation); Dispersal, only in the Hall's city (2 Production → 1); the Reserve Vats (CH27), one per city, half of every worked mine's base yield; and the Philosopher's Stone (CH21), 6 a turn and a lump of 150 for the one civilisation that builds it. For an eight-city empire from the Hall's Early Modern arrival to a late-game finish, that is roughly Hall ~1,100, Vats ~800, Dispersal ~900 (at a steep cost in production), and ~850 more with the Stone — some 2,800 matter without the Stone and 3,700 with it. A three-city tall civilisation lands within a few per cent of that: its capital's larger output makes Dispersal its stronger lever, while its fewer Vats bank less. Wide leans slightly ahead, as intended; tall is not crippled. There is no Great Person of matter — the Substance world's one new mechanic is the resource itself. (Preliminary estimates; the Hall's 6 and the Stone's 6 are the dials.) **Dispersal**ⁿ — a perpetual process in the build queue, like Wealth: ⟨Enables conversion of city production to [Prima Materia]⟩ at **2 Production → 1 Prima Materia**, ⟨Only available <in cities with a [Transmutation Hall]>⟩ — a city with a Hall may turn its *whole* surplus to formless matter for as long as it chooses. This is true production banking: what is not needed this decade is dispersed and re-locked when it is. (Fork extension: Unciv's `PerpetualStatConversion` targets a `Stat`; it is extended to accept a stockpiled resource — the `[stockpile]` parameter type already exists — see §7.0.) **Re-locking into the land.** Matter is re-tempered into a mineral *lode* on a tile of your own territory. **Mineral Crucible**ⁿ [unit, Civilian; ⟨Unbuildable⟩; ⟨Can be purchased for [1] [Gold] [in all cities]⟩ + ⟨Costs [240] [Prima Materia]⟩; ⟨Can move immediately once bought⟩; mv 2; ⟨Can instantly construct a [Transmuted Iron Lode] improvement⟩ `<by consuming this unit>`, and the same unique for the Coal, Oil, Aluminum and Rare Earths lodes; ⟨Only available <in cities with a [Transmutation Hall]>⟩] and **Noble Crucible**ⁿ [the same, but ⟨Costs [400] [Prima Materia]⟩ and building a Transmuted Gold, Silver or Gem Lode]. **Transmuted Lodes** — eight improvements, the **Transmuted Iron Lode**ⁿ, **Transmuted Coal Lode**ⁿ, **Transmuted Oil Lode**ⁿ, **Transmuted Aluminum Lode**ⁿ, **Transmuted Rare Earths Lode**ⁿ, **Transmuted Gold Lode**ⁿ, **Transmuted Silver Lode**ⁿ and **Transmuted Gem Lode**ⁿ ( `terrainsCanBeBuiltOn: [Land]`; no `turnsToBuild`, so no Worker can ever build one — Unciv lets only instant construction place an improvement without a build time (`MapUnit.canBuildImprovement`) — though a Worker may repair one that has been pillaged; strategic lodes ⟨Provides [2] [Iron]⟩ and likewise [Coal], [Oil], [Aluminum], [Rare Earths]; noble lodes ⟨Provides [1] [Gold Ore]⟩, [Silver] or [Gems]; each lode's `techRequired` is the breakthrough that reveals its resource — Iron, Gold and Gems Ore Identification (S04), Silver Deep Shaft Mining (MI05), Coal and Oil — the fuels — once the civilisation knows the fuel by either route, discovering its seams or making it: ⟨Only available <after discovering [non-[{non-[Coal Discovery]} {non-[Blast Furnace]}]]>⟩ and ⟨Only available <after discovering [non-[{non-[Petroleum Discovery]} {non-[Internal Combustion Engine]}]]>⟩ in place of a single `techRequired` (improvements honour `Only available`: `TileImprovementFunctions`), Aluminum Aluminium Extraction (ME13), Rare Earths Rare Earth Extraction (MI11) — so a Crucible's Create action stays disabled until you know the form exists (instant construction is enabled only when `canBuildImprovement` finds no problem, and `MissingTech` is one: `UnitActionsFromUniques`, `TileImprovementFunctions`); ⟨Will not be replaced by automated units⟩; ⟨Pillaging this improvement yields approximately [+20 Gold]⟩). Transmutation is therefore **repeatable and limited by land, not by city count**: every lode displaces whatever the tile was doing, stands in your territory (Unciv's `OutsideBorders` building problem), and stops providing the moment it is pillaged — an improvement's `Provides` is counted over every owned tile, unpillaged ones only (`CityResources.getResourceFromUniqueImprovedTiles`) — until a Worker repairs it. The noble lodes are gold, silver and gems rather than the made luxuries of earlier drafts: a lode is mineral, and lead into gold is the alchemist's defining act. Re-locking into coin — or into friendship: **Transmuted Bullion**ⁿ [unit, Civilian; ⟨Unbuildable⟩; ⟨Can be purchased for [1] [Gold] [in all cities]⟩ + ⟨Costs [40] [Prima Materia]⟩ — buy-only, like a Missionary bought with Faith, the coin a token and the matter the price; ⟨Can move immediately once bought⟩ — without this a purchased unit has no movement that turn (`BaseUnit.construct`) and the consume action is gated on movement (`UnitActionsFromUniques`); mv 2; ⟨Instantly gain [30] [Gold]⟩ `<by consuming this unit>` — four matter for three coin, the alchemist's loss made a rate; ⟨Only available <in cities with a [Transmutation Hall]>⟩]. Stockpile costs are charged on purchase (`CityConstructions.purchaseConstruction`), there is no per-turn purchase limit, and each cart is placed on the nearest free tile, so a city may buy and cash several a turn without stacking — the queue is never touched. Civ unique carried by this breakthrough: ⟨Gain [6] Influence with a [Transmuted Bullion] gift to a City-State⟩ — a Global template, which is how Unciv's gifting code (`UnitActions.getGiftActions`, `checkCivInfoUniques = true`) looks it up; it admits a civilian to the gift action. Why six: Unciv offers gold gifts only in 250, 500 and 1000 (`CityStateDiplomacyTable`), paying ~25 Influence for 250 early and 5 late (`influenceGainedByGift`), so a 30-Gold cart already buys Influence in a denomination gold cannot, at a better rate per coin; the sixth point is the premium for driving it there, so that cashing the cart and gifting the coin is never simply the better move. Higher would let Dispersal-fed carts hold alliances from a single city. So a Substance civilisation keeps a **second treasury** of matter: spend it on the strategic resource you lack, cash it at three-quarters value, or drive it to a City-State for alliance. **Pricing anchor:** every re-locking is priced in the gold Bullion would have returned — a permanent +2 of a strategic resource costs 240 matter (≈180 Gold), a permanent luxury 400 (≈300 Gold, about what the AI charges for one luxury for thirty turns) — permanent, that is, until pillaged; against the budget above, a whole game's matter buys about a dozen strategic re-lockings, or half as many luxuries, or some two thousand Gold. **Balance sheet, from Unciv's own formulas:** rush-buying costs `(30 × production)^0.75` Gold — 2.5 to 4.6 Gold per point — and the universal Wealth process converts production to Gold at ¼. Banked through Dispersal and cashed as Bullion, production becomes Gold at ⅜ — half again better than Wealth, which is the Substance civilisation's edge, and it keeps the option of spending the matter on a resource instead. Banked and then *rush-bought back*, production returns about a tenth of itself: a loss so steep that no one will use matter as a substitute for building, only as a reserve for the turn a unit is needed anywhere, now. Preliminary, but the *ratios* are the point: gold must never be the weak use of matter, and matter must never out-produce production. Any form into any other, at a loss, with no new interface. In a FOSSIL_FUELS_ABSENT world the fuel lodes open through the charcoal and alcohol roads, so a Substance civilisation there can re-temper matter into the seams its world never laid down. *Substance only.* | Only in Substance-Elemental worlds (FQ9). The alchemists' dream, industrialised. |
| CH21 | **Philosopher's Stone** | STANDARD | Modern | CH20 + PH04 (Scientific Method) | ⟨[Great Merchant] is earned [+15]% faster⟩ — every adept's gold, if not the Stone itself. **Philosopher's Stone**ⁿ [world wonder; 400; +3 Culture, +2 Happiness; Scientist ×1; Merchant ×1; Great Merchant points 2; ⟨Instantly provides [150] [Prima Materia]⟩ on completion; ⟨Provides [6] [Prima Materia]⟩ — a second furnace that never goes out]. Transmutation at scale: the civilisation that holds the Stone keeps the largest second treasury in the world, and strategic resources become effectively fungible for it. (Earlier drafts had the Stone turn half of every Market's gold into matter; the converter reads only a building's own yield — the Market's 2 Gold — so that was one matter a turn. A flat flow says what was meant.) There is no route from gold back into matter, so Bullion's exchange is one-way and cannot pump. *Substance only.* | The crown of the substance-elemental chain. Every court alchemist vindicated. |
| CH22 | **Five-Phase Theory** | STANDARD | Early Modern | CH19 (Process-Elemental validated) + CH01 (Alchemy) | Grants MATTER_THEORY; *reveals* the Generative Cycle Engine (CH23) and Phase-Destruction Warfare (CH24). *Process only.* | Only in Process-Elemental worlds (FQ9). Transformation, not composition, is the deep truth. |
| CH23 | **Generative Cycle Engine** | STANDARD | Early Modern | CH22 | **Cycle Forge**ⁿ [190/2; +2 Production; Sage ×1; Great Sage points 2] — where the cycle is studied; its gain is its Sages. **Sage**ⁿ (new specialist, `Specialists.json`: +1 Food, +1 Culture; Great Sage points 3 — slots in the Forge, the Calorimetry Hall and The Great Wheel). **Great Sage**ⁿ (new Great Person by the standard recipe: Civilian; `Is part of Great Person group [Great Person]`; `Unbuildable`; `Uncapturable`). A Great Sage's life's work is one of **the Five Turnings**ⁿ — Great Improvements on the Manufactory's pattern (`Great Improvement`; `Will not be replaced by automated units`; no `turnsToBuild`, so no Worker can build one — `MapUnit.canBuildImprovement`), each built by ⟨Can instantly construct a [Turning of Wood] improvement⟩ `<by consuming this unit>` (five such uniques on the Great Sage, one per Turning), and each only on its own element's land — which Unciv enforces without new code, since instant construction offers only improvements with no permanent building problem on the tile (`UnitActionsFromUniques.getImprovementConstructionActionsFromGeneralUnique`). **On its tile**, each Turning is a plain Great Improvement: **Turning of Wood**ⁿ (Forest, Jungle — built on the feature, which stays, like a Lumber mill) ⟨[+3 Food]⟩; **Turning of Fire**ⁿ (Desert, Plains, Volcanic) ⟨[+3 Production]⟩; **Turning of Earth**ⁿ (Grassland, Flood plains) ⟨[+3 Gold]⟩; **Turning of Metal**ⁿ (Hill, Tundra, Snow) ⟨[+3 Science]⟩; **Turning of Water**ⁿ (any land ⟨Can only be built on [non-[{non-[Fresh Water]} {non-[Coastal]}]] tiles⟩ — beside a river, lake, oasis or the sea; Unciv filters have AND and NOT but no OR, and several placement uniques are ANDed (`TileImprovementFunctions`), so the OR is written by De Morgan's law) ⟨[+3 Culture]⟩. **Fed, each grants its phase to the whole realm.** A Turning is *fed* when a Turning of the element before it in the generative cycle is there to feed it — **one feeder to one beneficiary**, so the number of fed Wood Turnings is the smaller of the Wood and Water counts. Carried by this breakthrough, one unique per phase, stacking for every fed Turning: **Wood** (spring, growth) ⟨[+15]% growth [in all cities] <for every [min([Owned [Turning of Wood] Tiles], [Owned [Turning of Water] Tiles] * (1 + [[The Great Wheel] Buildings]))]>⟩ — water nourishes wood; **Fire** (summer, flourishing) ⟨[+15]% Production when constructing [All] buildings [in all cities] <for every [min([Owned [Turning of Fire] Tiles], [Owned [Turning of Wood] Tiles] * (1 + [[The Great Wheel] Buildings]))]>⟩ — wood feeds fire; **Earth** (the centre, ground) ⟨[-20]% Culture cost of natural border growth [in all cities] <for every [min([Owned [Turning of Earth] Tiles], [Owned [Turning of Fire] Tiles] * (1 + [[The Great Wheel] Buildings]))]>⟩ — fire makes earth, and the realm's ground settles outward; **Metal** (autumn, the blade) ⟨[+10]% Strength <for [Military] units> <for every [min([Owned [Turning of Metal] Tiles], [Owned [Turning of Earth] Tiles] * (1 + [[The Great Wheel] Buildings]))]>⟩ — earth bears metal; **Water** (winter, wisdom) ⟨[+15]% Great Person generation [in all cities] <for every [min([Owned [Turning of Water] Tiles], [Owned [Turning of Metal] Tiles] * (1 + [[The Great Wheel] Buildings]))]>⟩ — metal gathers water. The `(1 + [[The Great Wheel] Buildings])` term is The Great Wheel's rule — *each Turning feeds two* — folded into the same count, since Unciv counts a civilisation's buildings by name; without the wonder it is 1 and feeding is strictly one-to-one. **How it is counted:** `Owned [tileFilter] Tiles` matches an unpillaged improvement by name (`Tile.matchesSingleFilter`); `<for every [countable]>` multiplies any unique aggregated through `UniqueMap.forEachMatchingUnique` (`forEachMultiplied`); and countable expressions support `min()` and arithmetic on nested countables (`Unique-parameters.md`). No marker resource and no stockpile. **What follows:** one complete cycle — five Great Sages — returns fifteen tile yields and five policy-sized effects; beyond it, progress is incremental rather than all-or-nothing, since any extra Turning placed behind an extra of its feeder adds one more fed effect (a wheel plus a second Water and a second Wood holds six); a pillaged Turning drops out of every count, so raiding an enemy's Wood starves its Fire; and the terrain sets are chosen so that almost any start holds all five. The one loop to watch: fed Water raises Great Person generation, which yields more Great Sages — bounded, since each Water must itself be fed by a Metal. Civ unique: ⟨Gain [40] Influence with a [Great Sage] gift to a City-State⟩ — the gifting code destroys a Great Person given to a City-State but pays the Influence first (`UnitActions.getGiftActions`), so a Great Sage may instead be sent to teach a neighbour. *Process only.* | The wu xing answer to industry: the turning cycle feeds itself. |
| CH24 | **Phase-Destruction Warfare** | STANDARD | Modern | CH22 + WA-any (any military breakthrough) | **Phase Disruptor**ⁿ [unit, Siege; cost 300; mv 2; str 22; ranged str 35; range 2; ⟨[+25]% Strength <vs cities>⟩; ⟨[+25]% Strength <when attacking>⟩]; **Elemental Ward**ⁿ [150/1; cityStrength +6]. The controlling cycle weaponised. *Process only.* | The five phases turned to war. |
| CH25 | **Organic Synthesis Attempt** | CORE | Modern | CH07 (Organic Chemistry) | **Synthesis Laboratory**ⁿ [national wonder; 300/0; +2 Science; on completion ⟨Triggers a [Organic Synthesis Attempt] event⟩ and `Reveals` the path: fully abiotic synthesis (Polymer Science, CH11) in Mechanist worlds (~70%), Vital Essence Isolation (GN12) in Vitalist worlds (~30%)]. **The decisive experiment for FQ11** — urea from dead matter, as Wöhler did; and whether it works decides what life is. | The experiment that quietly killed vitalism in our world — and vindicated it in others, without closing the refinery. |
| CH26 | **Closed-Loop Calorimetry** | STANDARD | Early Modern | CH22 (Five-Phase Theory) + TH01 (Laws of Thermodynamics) | **Calorimetry Hall**ⁿ [125/1; Sage ×1; ⟨[+1 Production] from all [Cycle Forge] buildings⟩ — better-tuned loops]; *reveals* the Generative Cycle Engine (CH23). The paddle-wheel of the wu xing: the sealed loop warms a little more than it should. *Process only.* | The paddle-wheel of the wu xing: the water warms a little more than it should. |
| CH27 | **Formless Reserve** | STANDARD | Modern | CH20 (Transmutation Practice) + TH02 (Refrigeration) | **Reserve Vats**ⁿ [500/3; +2 Gold; Merchant ×1; ⟨[50]% of [Production] from every [Mine] in the city added to [Prima Materia]⟩ — half of what every worked mine yields, echoed as matter at the mine-head]. One per city, so this is the source that leans wide: the converter reads a Mine's own +1 Production, not the tile's total, and truncates per city — eight cities working three mines each bank eight a turn, a tall capital working eight mines banks four. (It sums before truncating, `CityResources.kt`, so many mines add up where a fraction of one would vanish; a Quarry, with no base production, would yield nothing.) *Substance only.* | Nothing is wasted that can be bottled. |
| CH28 | **Helium Extraction** | STANDARD | Modern | CH06 (Periodic Table) + OP08 (Spectroscopy) | A lifting gas that does not burn. **Ends the Airship Disaster** for this civilisation — every airship's risk carries `<before discovering [Helium Extraction]>` — and a civilisation that holds it is unshaken by reports of other nations' disasters, since it knows the danger was the gas, not the airship. It does not restore confidence already lost. Liquid helium also opened low-temperature physics: Refrigeration (TH02, which now holds Cryogenics) and Superconductivity (EL11) cost ×0.8 for a civilisation that has it (a multiplier, §1.2). | Found first in the Sun's spectrum (1868) and named for it, which holds in any world; superconductivity was discovered in 1911 in mercury cooled by liquid helium. |

### FOCUS: ELECTRICITY ⚡
*Available from: O06 (Natural Philosophy) + CH04 (Distillation — acids)*

**Theory-agnostic:** electricity is a *phenomenon* — amber sparks, piles produce current, coils turn shafts — so the whole EL chain works identically in every world. What differs is the *account*: an atomic world explains current as electron flow; an elemental world treats it as a manifestation of a fundamental element or phase (a "fluid" or an aspect of the fire/metal phases), and unifies it with the rest of physics through Aetheric/Great-Harmony Field Theory (QM-E04) rather than atomic electromagnetism. No EL breakthrough requires atomic theory; EL03 (Electromagnetism) is the empirical field theory and stands in both kinds of world. Downstream nodes that need the *deep* unification read the HIGH_ENERGY_PHYSICS token.

Prerequisite for the Telegraph (EL04), Cold Fusion (NP10) and Electric Aviation (AE11). ⟨[+1 Culture] from all [Museum] buildings⟩ — galvanic demonstrations draw the crowds: Galvani's frogs and Volta's pile were one line of experiment (Static Electricity, merged here in v3.105). Foundation of electrical science. | |

Prerequisite for Radio (EN11) and the Internet (CP04). **Telephone Exchange**ⁿ [375/2; +2 Gold; ⟨[+1 Gold] from each Trade Route⟩] — Bell and Marconi: telecommunication by wire and without (Telephone, merged here in v3.103). | |

| EL12 | **Room Temperature Superconductor** | RARE | Atomic | EL11 + MS02 (Nanotechnology) | ⟨[+2 Production] [in all cities connected to capital]⟩; ⟨[+1] Movement <for [Civilian] units>⟩ (maglev). **Frontier Question FQ3** validates here. *Also reachable through Electronics or Materials Science.* |  |
| EL14 | **Direct-Current Stations** | STANDARD | Modern | EL06 (Electric Generator) + EL02 (Voltaic Pile) | **Generating Station**ⁿ [a grid plant: ⟨Power Plant⟩ (tag); 310/2; requires a **Factory** in this city; +2 Production; ⟨[+5]% [Production] [in this city]⟩; ⟨Remove [{Power Plant} {non-[Generating Station]}] [in this city]⟩ — a neighbourhood station, built in any city, connected or not]; ⟨[+2 Production] from all [Generating Station] buildings <after discovering [Aluminium Extraction]>⟩ (direct current is what aluminium smelting runs on — the Hall–Héroult cell is electrolysis). **Competing Solution** (§1.2): the alternative to the Power Grid (EL07), detour margin ×1.5; if the grid is discovered first this is never developed, and if it arrives later this *persists* — it works where the network does not reach. The trade: a Generating Station is a cheap, weak plant in the city's plant slot, so a connected city that builds one gives up AC's free grid power; a disconnected city has nothing to give up. **The War of the Currents** (an easter egg). *Thomas Edison* and *Nikola Tesla* are already in Unciv's canonical Engineer name group (`UnitNameGroups.json`), so the egg needs no new data: it turns on which civilisation's Great Engineers they become. Unciv never reuses a Great Person's name within a game (`gameInfo.unitNamesTaken`), so at most one civilisation ever receives each — the war can be fought between two rivals. The fork records the Great Person names each civilisation receives (one line where Unciv assigns the name, `UniqueTriggerActivation`), and while the race is still open the discovery roll reads that record: a civilisation that has had **Edison**, DC's champion, finds Direct-Current Stations first — the Power Grid cannot fire until they have, and their detour margin is dropped; one that has had **Tesla**, whose induction motor was the heart of the AC system, never develops DC at all — Direct-Current Stations are locked out, a Path Not Taken. The effects are not mirror images because lock-out runs one way: a primary is never locked out, so Edison's AC still arrives in time, and DC persists beside it. | Edison's Pearl Street station, 1882: power had to be made within a mile or two of where it was used. |

**THE FISSION DEEP PATH:**

The deep fission path represents what happens when a civilisation pushes fission technology to its limits rather than pivoting to fusion. NP-F01 is the gateway — the alternative in a Competing Solutions group whose primary is Nuclear Fusion (NP04). Discover fusion first and the deep fission path is never developed; reach advanced fuel cycles first and the chain opens and *persists* — its micro-reactors and endurance remain worth having after fusion arrives. But a civilisation that invests heavily in breeder reactors and thorium before fusion arrives may discover advanced fuel cycles — and from there, the rest of the chain follows. In FQ2-Impossible worlds Nuclear Fusion dead-ends — a dead-ended breakthrough obsoletes nothing — so the deep fission path can never be obsoleted.

The no-fusion road to the stars. | W18 — Pool. The no-fusion path to deep space. |

### FOCUS: QUINTESSIC PHYSICS ✦
*Available from: CH19 (Substance-Elemental or Process-Elemental validated) + MA05 — the elemental-world niche equivalent of Nuclear Physics*

In Elemental worlds (FQ9 ≠ Corpuscular), matter is not made of atoms, so Nuclear Physics (NP), Quantum Physics (QP), and the semiconductor route to Computing never cohere — those focuses stay Unknown and unenterable. Quintessic Physics fills the same structural niche: the deep theory of matter, the high-energy endgame, and an alternative computation substrate. **It serves both elemental traditions.** Each breakthrough below has two framings that share identical mechanics: a **Substance** reading (Greek/Aristotelian — a fifth element, the aether, binds and transmutes the four) and a **Process** reading (Chinese *wu xing* — the generative and destructive cycles are the fundamental forces, and "energy" means driving those cycles). The building and unit names shown are Substance-world; Process worlds use the parenthetical cycle-names. It reconnects to shared frontier physics (FTL, orbital engineering) by the late Space era per Deep Reconciliation.

**Shared spine, mirrored frontier, divergent branches.** The seven core nodes (QM-E01–07) are load-bearing — dual-framed, shared by both traditions, and required for the deep-physics tokens and the endgame. Around them, a **mirrored frontier** gives the elemental world a node for every atomic experience that is a phenomenon rather than a quantum: Limiting Fineness (QM-E14) where atomic worlds have Wave-Particle Duality, Aetheric Electrodynamics (QM-E15) for QED, the Boundary Survey (QM-E16) for Radioactivity and Uranium, the Complete Catalogue (QM-E17) for the Standard Model, Plenum Gravitation (QM-E27) for Quantum Gravity, the Quintessic Warhead (QM-E20) for Nuclear Weapons, the Compact Re-Tempering Reactor (QM-E21) for compact fusion — and, where the plenum offers what vacuum cannot, Aether Telegraphy (QM-E18), the Celestial Current Survey (QM-E19) and the Current Sail (QM-E23). Shared late craft keeps its familiar names in every world — the Programmable Computer, Integrated Circuit, Vacuum Tube, Solar Panel and Radiation Shielding are named for what they are and do. Only a name that asserts a theory false in that world (the Nuclear Submarine, the Atomic Bomb, Semiconductor Mastery) gives way, to the name that world's own science would give; the machine is the same, the switch inside it differs. Beyond all this the two elemental worlds pull in opposite strategic directions. **Substance rewards depth and self-sufficiency:** transmutation (CH20/CH21) turns any resource into any other, the Formless Reserve (CH27) catches what transmutation wastes, Incorruptible Matter (QM-E09) perfects what you hold, Dissonance Weaponry (QM-E26) shatters what others hold, and Temperament Medicine (QM-E10) crowns a flourishing, self-contained civilisation — a substance civilisation can synthesise its way out of scarcity and prosper tall and isolated. **Process rewards breadth and balance:** Closed-Loop Calorimetry (CH26) proves the wheel gives back, the Generative Cycle (CH23) grows the economy by keeping resources flowing through the five phases at a gain, Phase Exchange (TR16) pays for routes that complete a cycle, Self-Renewing Structures (QM-E24) achieve permanence by renewal rather than perfection, Full-Cycle Coupling (QM-E11) pays out for holding all five phase-classes at once, and Counter-Cycle Reversal (QM-E25) lets a rich realm pay the ruinous price of turning the wheel backwards — so a process civilisation wants wide, diverse territory and many trade partners. The Boundary Furnace / Cycle-Limit Observatory (QM-E08) is the deep-science instrument both traditions build; Plenum Gravitation (QM-E27) carries the elemental routes to the FTL frontier, with Exotic Matter (QM-E06), Higher Strata Theory (QM-E12), the Aetheric Vacuum (QM-E13) and the Celestial Current Survey (QM-E19) (see §4). Every node here is derived from the two physics models (*Elemental Physics, Models I and II*); none exists in an atomic world, and no atomic-only node exists here. The Great Cycle of the Process world has no terrestrial rhythm — no seasons, no ages — so that nothing betrays the world before its decisive experiment.

### FOCUS: RELATIVITY & COSMOLOGY 🌌
*Available from: MA08 + EL03*

**Theory-agnostic (dual-framed).** Relativity is phenomenon-level — clocks slow with speed, light bends near mass, E=mc² holds — so the whole RC chain works in every world; only the *account* differs. Atomic worlds read these as the geometry of curved spacetime. Elemental worlds read them through **Lorentzian aether-mechanics**: the same equations and the same predictions, reinterpreted as effects of motion *through* the plenum (the aether / the medium of the great cycle) rather than of curvature. The aether that an atomic world's experiments rule out is, in an elemental world, simply real — a preferred medium whose density gradients do the work curvature does elsewhere. No RC breakthrough forks on this; RC01/RC02 stand in both kinds of world, and nodes needing the deep account read the malleable-space primitive (QM-E27 elemental, QP08 atomic). Black Hole Thermodynamics, Dark Energy and Closed Timelike Curves each carry an elemental account — the dark star, the medium's tension at cosmic scale, closed circuits of the medium. See *FTL in Elemental Worlds* (§4).

Michell's dark star of 1783, read through Lorentzian aether-mechanics; the report speaks of a fold in the medium a vessel might pass. | |

### FOCUS: ROCKETRY 🚀
*Available from: CH13 (Rocket Propellant)*

**Elemental-world equivalence:** the propulsion breakthroughs below that name nuclear physics (RK08, RK10, RK13, RK14) have straight elemental counterparts with identical mechanics, gated by the Quintessic chain (QM-E) instead of NP/ES. The Quintessic Reactor (QM-E02) substitutes for a fission core, the Re-Tempering Reactor (QM-E05) for a fusion core. Only the names and flavour change: a *Nuclear Thermal Rocket* is a *Quintessic Thermal Rocket*, an *Orion Nuclear Pulse* is an *Alchemical Pulse Drive*, and so on. The HIGH_ENERGY_PHYSICS token resolves the prerequisite in either kind of world.

### FOCUS: MATERIALS SCIENCE 🔬
*Available from: CH11 (Polymer Science) + ME12 (Bessemer)*

*Also reachable in this focus (dual-path; home focus in parentheses): Room Temperature Superconductor (EL12, from Electricity).*

**Non-atomic worlds:** Materials Science sits largely in the Deep-Reconciliation convergence zone — by the late Atomic era, elemental and atomic worlds have both arrived at a working account of fine structure (atoms, or the deep quintessic/phase structure of the elements), so most of this focus is shared. Two nodes name atomic/molecular mechanism explicitly and read the HIGH_ENERGY_PHYSICS token instead of a specific theory: MS02 (Nanotechnology, with Graphene merged in) is reached via NP02→QP02 in atomic worlds or QM-E01→QM-E04 in elemental worlds. The "molecular manufacturing" of MS02 is, in an elemental world, *fine elemental structuring* — same mechanics, different account of what is being arranged.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| MS02 | **Nanotechnology / Fine Structuring** | CORE | Atomic | HIGH_ENERGY_PHYSICS (QP02 atomic, or QM-E04 elemental) + CH07 | ⟨[+1 Happiness] [in all cities]⟩ (targeted medicine). In elemental worlds the craft is *fine structuring*, and the building is the Fine-Structure Forge — nothing there is built atom by atom. ⟨[+5]% [Production] [in all cities]⟩ — the strongest cable ever spun (Graphene Production, merged here in v3.101). | Same mechanics; the account of *what* is being arranged depends on FQ9. |

(Cloaking land units has no fair Unciv mechanism and is dropped.) |  |
| MS05 | **Self-Healing Materials** | UNCOMMON | Atomic | MS02 + CH07 | ⟨[-25]% maintenance cost for [All] buildings [in all cities]⟩; ⟨All newly-trained [Military] units [in all cities] receive the [Self-Healing] promotion⟩ (**Self-Healing**ⁿ: ⟨May heal outside of friendly territory⟩). The atomic counterpart of Incorruptible Matter and Self-Renewing Structures. |  |

RARE. | W18 — Capstone B, rare. |
| MS08 | **Exotic Matter Engineering** | STANDARD | Space | (QP11 Exotic Matter Synthesis, validated, or QM-E06 Exotic Matter, validated) + MS02 | **Exotic Matter Foundry**ⁿ [1000/3; ⟨[+15]% Production when constructing [SS FTL Drive] units [in this city]⟩] — practical fabrication; prerequisite for Warp and Wormhole FTL. *Elemental:* the same practical fabrication, of exotic Aether made in quantity — Aether held heavier or lighter than its rest density (Substance; Model I, §7) — or of broken-cycle matter held past its own closure (Process; Model II, §7). (Named apart from that Forge under the filter discipline of §7.0.) It needs exotic matter that holds: where none can be sustained, there is nothing to fabricate. *Exists only where exotic matter is validated (QP11 / QM-E06).* Hosts *the bubble is stable* (Warp) and *the throat is stable* (Wormhole), both merged into those builds (W14). | W18 — Pool; Wormhole's and Warp's engineering, wherever exotic matter is validated; hosts two conditions (W14), so priced as a decider. |
| MS17 | **Transformation Media** | STANDARD | Space | MS08 + (QP12 or QM-E13) — **cost ×4** | **Shockproof Walls**ⁿ [building; cost set at spec; ⟨[+50]% Strength for cities <vs [Siege] units> <when defending> <in this city>⟩ — the walls route a bombardment's shock waves around their own city's structures; `<in this city>` makes the unique local, so each building strengthens only its own city and the bonuses never accumulate across cities]. Bomb Shelters (SE03 Radiation Shielding) become 50% more effective: the Bomb Shelter itself gains ⟨Population loss from nuclear attacks [-50]% [in this city] <after discovering [Transformation Media]>⟩ and ⟨Damage to garrison from nuclear attacks [-50]% [in this city] <after discovering [Transformation Media]>⟩, so a sheltered city loses 12.5% of the normal amount instead of 25% (fork engine work: `City.getAggregateModifier` in `Nuke.kt` must use the defending city's own context, not an empty one, for the conditional to apply). A nuke's damage to city health is not reducible. Distinct from Hardened Shelters (NP13), which protects every city. Designing a space by its shape: choose the geometry, then build the medium that produces it — as Morris and Thorne designed traversable wormholes, and as transformation optics steers light (Pendry, Schurig and Smith; Leonhardt, 2006); analogue wormholes have been built in metamaterials (a magnetic one, 2015), and seismic metamaterials tested at full scale (2014). On the wormhole path it is the discipline of shaping exotic matter into a designed throat, tried first on analogue throats. Substance: graded substance that makes waves travel as if the Aether were folded; Process: a pattern grown to channel the flow's course. | W18/balance round two — Wormhole's engineering, outside its tests; the Space-era successor to MS03 Metamaterials, without needing it. |

### FOCUS: SPACE ENGINEERING 🛰️
*Available from: RK03 (Multi-Stage Rocket) + MS01 (Composite Materials)*

All Space Engineering breakthroughs represent orbital and deep-space projects managed from Earth. They provide Earth-based bonuses (buildings, resource income, strategic advantages) and serve as prerequisites for the FTL/sub-light victory conditions. No new playable maps are opened.

Radiation is named for what it does, and stellar radiation exists in every world; the warm ores taught both worlds what to shield against. | Radiation is named for what it does, and stellar radiation exists in every world; the warm ores taught both worlds what to shield against. |

A national wonder is destroyed when its city is captured (`CityConquestFunctions`), so losing the launch site stops the clock until it is rebuilt — at the cost of another citizen. A deliberately slow challenge beside the intended FTL victory. |  |
| SE13 | **Planetary Defense** | STANDARD | Space | RK05 + (WA20 Drones, or NP05 Nuclear Weapons, or QM-E20 Quintessic Warhead) | **Planetary Defense Network**ⁿ [national wonder; 750/0; cityStrength +10; cityHealth +100; ⟨[+15]% Strength for cities⟩; ⟨Cost increases by [180] per owned city⟩] — one network for the whole planet, hardest at its centre; ⟨[+15]% Strength for cities⟩ — every city under the orbital umbrella. (Asteroid events have no Unciv mechanism.) |  |

### FOCUS: ENERGY SYSTEMS ⚡
*Available from: WM01 (Canal) + MC03 (Gearing), or TH01 (Laws of Thermodynamics) + EL03 (Electromagnetism)*

**One grid plant per city — and never locked into the first.** Every grid plant carries the tag unique ⟨Power Plant⟩. Unciv files every unique a building has, recognised or not, under its text (`UniqueMap.addUnique`), and `Building.matchesFilter` matches a building by any exact unique it has, so `[Power Plant]` names the whole group. Each plant also carries `Remove [{Power Plant} {non-[its own name]}] [in this city]`, which fires when it is completed (`CityConstructions.addBuilding` → `triggerNewBuildingUniques`): building a new plant retires the old one, which keeps the city powered until its replacement is ready. So a city holds one plant at a time, as in Civ V, but a Generating Station never bars the way to a Fusion Plant. The canonical Nuclear and Solar Plants join the group through the ruleset derivation, generalising their pairwise exclusion; the Hydro Plant stays outside it, as canonically it stands beside one. (Until v3.76 the group was a placeholder building that every plant declared it `replaces`, with `Only available <in cities without a [Power Plant]>` — which blocked every later plant, and which Unciv's capture swap, following `replaces`, would have turned into the stat-less placeholder.)

*Also reachable in this focus (dual-path; home focus in parentheses): Water Mill (WM05, from Water Management); Windmill (MC04, from Mechanics); Stirling Engine Mastery (TH08, from Thermodynamics). The Water Mill is the first mechanical power source beyond muscle.*

## 4. ENDGAME: FTL RESEARCH

*Rewritten in v3.112 from the FTL endgame redesign (the direction document's W1–W19). Two agreed rules that belong to the discovery model — Grand Endeavours priced at 2× as insurance, and proximity revelation (a 10% chance each turn that an eligible idea which fails to occur is glimpsed) — are written into §1.2 and §1.5 with that model, a deferred task; this section relies on them.*

### The FTL Problem

To win a Science Victory a civilisation builds and launches a ship with an FTL Drive. **Exactly one way of outrunning light works in any world**, chosen secretly at world creation from the five its ontology offers (*FTL in Elemental Worlds*, below). The player finds out which by investigation: each candidate is a **hypothesis** whose **conditions** Space-era experiments confirm or rule out, and the Drive can be built only from a successful **prototype** of the viable method.

**Every report is true** (W3). There are no false positives: a gamble's risk comes only from what the player has not yet tested, never from evidence that lies. **Certainty is information, not a gate** (W2): a civilisation may commit to a prototype before it is certain — the Hail Mary — and the finale is decided by judgement, never by dice.

The evidence comes only from experiments humanity has not yet done (W1): every condition is a Space-era result, while Atomic-era rows keep their real content and find what ours found.

### Hypotheses and the FTL Screen

- **The puzzle has its own screen** (W6), separate from the research screen: the hypotheses, a running list of results, hints of where to look, what has been disproved, and commitment to a prototype.
- **The puzzle opens with RC01 Special Relativity**, which establishes the light barrier; before it, going faster than light is no special achievement, and nothing is proposed. A hypothesis whose proposing row comes first waits for it.
- **Each hypothesis is proposed by a CORE theory row**, as each was in real history long before anything could test it: Warp and Wormhole by RC02 General Relativity, Hyperspace by MA13 Topology, Tachyonic by RC01 Special Relativity, Tunnelling by QP02 Quantum Mechanics Formalism (atomic worlds), Plenum by QM-E04 Unified Field Theory (elemental worlds). Each world proposes exactly its own five. **Proposing a hypothesis Reveals it** — the point from which commitment is possible.
- **Hypotheses are named for what they would do**, not in physics terms (the player-facing names are spec work). The game may say how many there are and name them; it never says which is true.
- **The unified theory closes the list** — QP08 Quantum Gravity Theory, or QM-E27 Plenum Gravitation, at the start of the Space era: every consistent completion of the theory admits exactly one way to outrun light, the candidates are these five, and which holds depends on properties not yet measured. Before it, the board makes no claim that any proposal is true or that the list is complete. Each unified theory is built on its ontology's great instrument (QP04 Particle Accelerator / QM-E08 Boundary Furnace, both CORE), so it arrives with its first tests nearly ready (W17); MA13 Topology is among QM-E27's prerequisites, as it is in QP08's chain, so every proposal arrives before the list is closed.
- **Every change to the screen is announced.** A discovery that changes anything on the FTL screen — a proposal, a condition's result, a build need met, the closing statement, a hint moving — carries a line in its notification, such as *"This bears on the question of how to exceed the speed of light."* It is computed from the screen's state before and after the discovery, and says only that something changed, never what it means.

**What the screen shows, and what stays hidden** (W10, W11):

- **A checklist of questions and needs, deliberately.** Each hypothesis lists its open questions and its build needs in plain words; the puzzle lies in their order and in when to commit (W12), not in finding them.
- **Every hint points to where progress is possible now.** For each open question or build need, the screen follows the answering breakthrough's prerequisites back to the first missing step whose own prerequisites are met, and names that step's focus — each focus where several steps can be worked on at once, each route's foci where there are alternatives. As research advances the hint moves, so following a hint in good faith is never a dead end. The research screen complements it: each focus says, vaguely, what it tends to help with (W19; mechanism and wording open).
- **The breakthroughs, and how far away each answer lies, stay hidden.** No breakthrough is named, nor its prerequisites, until it is Revealed or discovered in the normal way; a hint naming an unexpected focus shows only that the answer is more than one step away, never how many. The one exception is an instrument (*Conditions and Their Tests*): once a test's breakthrough is discovered, the screen names the building that will settle its question.
- **No odds, and no reasoning beyond one step.** The screen applies only direct consequences: a failed condition disproves every hypothesis that needs it; a hypothesis with all its conditions confirmed is confirmed; the last one standing is marked as the only one left.

**The guidance principles.** (1) *Show facts, not advice*: the screen and the reports state what is known and its direct consequences, and where each open question can be investigated; they never rate likelihood, recommend a test, or say which hypothesis to pursue — standing is factual, never "promising" or "likely". (2) Use the player's vocabulary. (3) Negative results are clearer than positive ones: a dead end is signposted plainly. (4) Several signals are needed to be sure — met by the conditions' structure, not by noise in reports. (5) No outside knowledge is required: a thoughtful player who follows the screen can always reach a victory.

### Conditions and Their Tests

A method is viable only if **all its conditions hold**, each a Space-era result (W14). A single condition can hold in worlds where its method fails, so a positive raises that method's standing without settling it; any failed condition rules the method out. Suggestive evidence (W5) and the need for several signals come from this structure, not from randomness in reports. Conditions marked *merged* are ingredients of the method's own build, tested by building them.

| Method | Atomic conditions | Elemental conditions |
|---|---|---|
| Warp | Exotic matter holds (merged); extra dimensions exist; the bubble is stable (merged) | Exotic matter holds (merged); higher strata or a higher-order cycle; the bubble of shaped Aether holds (merged) |
| Wormhole | Exotic matter holds (merged); closed timelike curves are stable; the throat is stable (merged) | Exotic matter holds (merged); closed circuits of the medium are stable; the throat is stable (merged) |
| Hyperspace | Extra dimensions exist; gravitational leakage shows they are open; dark matter is a hidden sector | Higher strata or a higher-order cycle; leakage into the strata shows they are open; dark matter is a hidden sector |
| Tunnelling | Macro-scale coherence (merged); spacetime is discrete; tunnelling carries information faster than light — the Hartman effect (merged) | — |
| Tachyonic | Dark energy is a tachyonic field; closed timelike curves are stable; a tachyonic sector exists | The medium carries a superluminal mode; closed circuits of the medium are stable; the catalogue contains a superluminal form |
| Plenum | — | The currents run faster than light (merged); the medium carries a superluminal mode; the currents can be ridden (merged) |

**Hosts.** Exotic matter — QP11 Exotic Matter Synthesis / QM-E06 Exotic Matter (Elemental); extra dimensions or strata — QP09 String Theory Validation / QM-E12 Higher Strata Theory; discreteness — QP10 Loop Quantum Gravity, which excludes extra dimensions; closed curves or circuits — RC07 Closed Timelike Curves; leakage — RC11 Gravitational Leakage Survey, which can hold only where extra dimensions or strata exist; dark matter — RC05 Dark Matter Identification; the tachyonic field or superluminal mode — RC06 Dark Energy Understanding; coherence — CP21 Macroscopic Quantum Control, or GN10 Bio-Quantum Coherence only where FQ6 (Biological Depth) makes the biological route real; the currents' speed — QM-E19 Celestial Current Survey; bubble and throat stability — MS08 Exotic Matter Engineering (only where exotic matter exists); the Hartman effect — QP14 Quantum Communication; a tachyonic sector or superluminal form — QP15 Beyond the Standard Model, or QM-E28 Beyond the Catalogue in elemental worlds; riding the currents — AE20 Current Rigging. QP04 Particle Accelerator and QM-E08 Boundary Furnace settle no condition: they are the great instruments the unified theories are built on.

**Why each answer is still unknown today.** Each deciding row's prerequisites include the missing piece that explains why the question is open in our world, which keeps every decider beyond our present for a real reason and makes the order of testing a real choice. The missing pieces are of two kinds:

- **Theoretical at root — the missing piece is the unified theory**, which gates them: RC07 Closed Timelike Curves (whether they survive depends on quantum effects in curved spacetime — chronology protection); QP11 Exotic Matter Synthesis (the Casimir effect gives tiny negative energy densities, and quantum inequalities constrain anything more; it also needs QP12 Casimir Effect Amplification); QP09 String Theory Validation and QP10 Loop Quantum Gravity (neither theory has yet made a prediction we can reach). For these, the breakthrough is the answer.
- **Blocked by measurement — the missing piece is an instrument.** The breakthrough is the design; the construction is the measurement. Each such test unlocks its instrument, a national wonder every civilisation may build for itself (construction about 3–6 turns), and the answer arrives when it is completed:

| Test (what it settles) | Instrument it unlocks | Real-world model |
|---|---|---|
| CP21 Macroscopic Quantum Control (coherence survives at large scales) | Orbital Quantum Laboratory — massive superpositions in free fall | ESA's MAQRO proposal |
| RC11 Gravitational Leakage Survey (extra dimensions are open) | Space Interferometer — a gravitational-wave detector in orbit | LISA, planned for the 2030s |
| RC05 Dark Matter Identification (dark matter is a hidden sector) | Survey Telescope — lensing maps and the expansion history | Euclid, which measures both |
| RC06 Dark Energy Understanding (dark energy is a tachyonic field; the medium carries a superluminal mode) | the same Survey Telescope | DESI; Euclid |
| QP15 Beyond the Standard Model (a tachyonic sector exists); elemental QM-E28 Beyond the Catalogue (a superluminal form) | Very Large Particle Collider — a next-generation collider; elemental: the Very Large Boundary Furnace (+6 Science, 2 Scientist slots each) | the proposed Future Circular Collider |

Either RC05 or RC06 unlocks the Survey Telescope, and once built it answers each of the two questions whose breakthrough the civilisation holds; RC05 and RC06 are researched alongside each other, not one behind the other. Once a test's breakthrough is discovered, while its question is open and the instrument not yet built, the FTL screen says so — *"To settle this unanswered question, we'll need to build a Space Interferometer"* — naming only the building the civilisation has just learned: the puzzle stays suggestive about what to research, but is explicit about what to build, since building is a deliberate act rather than a discovery. Instruments are visible, so rivals can see what a civilisation is testing; a civilisation that cannot build its instrument cannot settle that question.

**Evidence travels with its breakthrough.** For tests that are theoretical or merged into a build, the answer comes with the breakthrough however it arrives — discovery, diffusion, trade or theft. For measurement tests, the receiver must still build its own instrument to learn the answer. Knowledge diffusion thus acts as catch-up for laggards and never helps the leader.

**Prices.** The deciding rows cost 1× their era's price, so theory is the cheaper phase; so do the build rows that host a condition (CP21, MS08, AE20). Engineering rows in the signature fields cost 2.5× and their capstones 4× (the multipliers are marked on each row, §1.2); the Space era's base price is set in §1.2.

### Building and Proving

A method's **build list** is the theory and engineering to construct it if it is real; its **prove list** is its conditions outside the build (W4). Some ingredients are their own tests (merged). Balance is counted in Space-era rows, since earlier rows are already researched when the investigation begins.

| Method | Build (atomic / elemental) | Prove | Space-era rows |
|---|---|---|---|
| Warp | RC02 General Relativity; QP08 / QM-E27; QP11 / QM-E06 (merged); MS08 Exotic Matter Engineering (merged); power; ES18 Superconducting Energy Storage (after EL11 Superconductivity and EL07 Power Grid) | QP09 / QM-E12 | 6 |
| Wormhole | As Warp without power or ES18, plus RC04 Black Hole Thermodynamics, QP12 Casimir Effect Amplification / QM-E13 Aetheric Vacuum, MA13 Topology, and MS17 Transformation Media | RC07 | 6 |
| Hyperspace | QP08 / QM-E27; MA13 Topology; power; MA19 Higher-Dimensional Geometry (after MA15 Optimisation Theory) | QP09 / QM-E12; RC11; RC05 | 6 |
| Tunnelling (atomic) | QP02 Quantum Mechanics Formalism; CP10 Quantum Computing (after CP16 Planetary Data Networks); MS02 Nanotechnology or GN05 Synthetic Biology; QP14 Quantum Communication (merged; needs QP07 or GN10); CP21 Macroscopic Quantum Control (merged) | QP10 | 6 |
| Tachyonic | QP06 / QM-E17; QP08 / QM-E27; QM-E05 in elemental worlds; SE02 Orbital Assembly; power; NP15 Superconducting Storage Rings in atomic worlds (after EL11 and a power plant), TH20 Boundary Ring / Cycle-Limit Ring in elemental ones | RC06; RC07; QP15 / QM-E28 | 5 atomic / 6 elemental |
| Plenum (elemental) | QM-E27; MS02 Fine Structuring; QM-E19 Celestial Current Survey (merged); QM-E23 Current Sail (after AE14 Aerospace Industry); AE20 Current Rigging (merged) | RC06 | 6 |

A fully biological route to Tunnelling stays open but rare: QP14 accepts GN10 Bio-Quantum Coherence, MS02 may be replaced by GN05, and CP10 accepts GN10 — through GN08 Bio-Computing, a RARE gateway, and only where FQ6 allows.

**Power** (W9). Where a method needs a great deal of power the game measures it, without a new resource. Only the brute-force methods — Warp, Hyperspace and Tachyonic — need it ("this requires considerable amounts of energy"): the prototype needs generating capacity worth **one advanced fission plant**, counted from the grid plants the civilisation owns, weighted by era through Unciv's existing countables, and shown only on the FTL screen. Modern-era plants count 1 point; Atomic-era plants (ES06 Nuclear Fission Plant, ES07 Solar Panel, ES15 Quintessic Plant) 3; ES14 Advanced Fission 8 — the requirement; ES08 Fusion Reactor, QM-E05 Re-Tempering / Self-Feeding Cycle Reactor and the RARE compact and cascade reactors 10. Power is flavour, not a balance lever.

**Signature fields** (W18). Each method's engineering lies in one field's Space-era pool: Warp in Energy Systems, Wormhole in Materials Science, Hyperspace in Mathematics, Tunnelling in Computing, Tachyonic in Nuclear Physics (atomic worlds) or Thermodynamics (elemental), Plenum in Aeronautics. The pools are optional content: a science player meets the FTL requirements, which stay light, and is then free to explore any field; incidental discoveries along the way are part of the race, and the pools stay open during it. The evidence fields (Quantum Physics or Quintessic Physics, and Relativity & Cosmology) and the ship fields (Rocketry, Space Engineering) are shared by every method.

**The rules each method's shape obeys** (W15): (1) no single result settles a method; (2) every method has at least one condition outside its build list, so a Hail Mary is always possible; (3) conditions are shared wherever the physics allows, so choosing a test is a real decision; (4) shapes vary, and match each method's lane (W13: the Engineering, Renaissance, Theorist's, Information Age, Big Science and Elemental paths); (5) total time to victory is balanced across methods and ontologies, through the build and prove lists — to the standard that no method gives an advantage or disadvantage larger than ordinary game-to-game randomness, so a world whose method is Warp does not make it markedly harder to beat a diplomatic or cultural player; the prototype's cost is flat, and power is flavour; (6) confirming is the normal path — waiting to confirm is the better bet when no rival is close, while a right gamble skips enough confirmation to win a close race; (7) the theory phase is judged by how it plays — how soon the first answer comes, whether the narrowing is steady, how long the silences are — not by a length target.

**Bad luck never blocks a science victory.** A STANDARD idea whose moment passed comes back when something waits on it: a breakthrough it alone blocks, an FTL prototype, or an open question on the FTL screen (rediscovery on demand, §1.2). Whoever needs a row gets it, without the row being CORE. An idea nothing waits on stays lost, and UNCOMMON, RARE and UNIQUE alternatives keep their odds. Grand Endeavours (2×) are the deliberate route to any revealed goal, its idea occurred or not. Knowledge diffusion recovers old rows long before the race begins. With rediscovery every measured game finishes, isolated civilisations included. *(W16, which held a Space-era row's window open while its focus was funded, was dropped in v3.120: with rediscovery it added only speed, about 8 turns for the expert, and its written form did nothing measurable for the simulated players.)*

### The Prototype and the Hail Mary

Every science victory needs **a working prototype drive** (W2): a Grand Endeavour on the FTL screen, committable once its hypothesis is Revealed and its build prerequisites are discovered, whether or not the method has been verified. Committing before verification is the Hail Mary, a risk a player chooses — typically one who expects to lose the race otherwise.

- **It costs 5× the Space era's price** (about 4 turns on a good economy, 5 on an average one) — the same for every method — and **runs its full length**, then reports whether it works. A failed prototype proves its method invalid.
- **It costs more than any experiment that could disprove its method**, so a prototype is never the cheaper way to test a hypothesis — only a bet on finishing sooner.
- **The ship's FTL Drive requires a successful prototype**: one condition, the viable method's prototype researched. No failed prototype can satisfy it.
- **Six prototype Endeavours exist**, one per method (Tunnelling's and Plenum's in their own worlds only), and live on the FTL screen, not in the research tree.
- **The active/spread split holds.** A prototype takes the active focus's share (80% once SO05 Specialization of Labor is discovered, 50% before), and the spread still funds every other focus with work under study, so evidence keeps arriving during a build — possibly the experiment that disproves its method. Committing early gives up directed testing, not all testing.
- **A failed prototype returns nothing** but its proof: a refund would erode the rule that failure costs more than the disproving experiment.
- **A prototype halts if an experiment disproves its method mid-build**, and its progress is lost — being overtaken by evidence is part of the gamble's risk. **A disproved hypothesis cannot be committed.**
- **Completing the prototype, and later the SS FTL Drive, is announced to every civilisation**, as base Civ V announces spaceship parts; committing to a prototype stays private.
- **The AI commits when certain, and gambles only as a Hail Mary**: when a rival's ship looks close to launch and its best hypothesis has decent odds. (The AI's detailed policy belongs to the AI-automation task.)
- **The finale is decided by judgement**: wait, and win by commitment; commit early, and take a risk of your own choosing — never dice.

### Generation Rules

At world creation (W14): (1) the viable method is chosen uniformly from the ontology's five. (2) Its conditions hold; every other condition holds or fails at even odds, subject to extra dimensions and discreteness being exclusive, leakage holding only where extra dimensions or higher strata exist, and every other method failing at least one of its conditions — in practice, a uniform draw from the valid world truths. (3) The guarantee, checked as a battery: exactly one viable method; no method's conditions inside another's; and the viable method's build and prove rows always reachable, whatever the world's other truths — QP12 and QM-E13 always work as science (FQ4 is read at ES12 and QM-E07); fusion-free worlds keep advanced fission; GN10 is only ever an alternative; no Removal Truth touches these rows. MS08's two results exist only where exotic matter does, which matters only to methods exotic matter has already ruled out.

Reliability: MS02 Nanotechnology / Fine Structuring is CORE, QM-E06 accepts MS02 as well as its chemistry routes, and the rows that host a condition inside a build cost 1×, so a lost idea rarely leaves a world unwinnable; Grand Endeavours and proximity revelation recover the rest (§1.2, §1.5).

### FTL in Elemental Worlds

Strip the tech names from the six methods and each is a claim about what space and matter fundamentally *are*. Five of those claims are **ontology-neutral** — they hold or fail regardless of whether matter is atomic or elemental — and one on each side is **ontology-locked**:

- **Malleable space** (Warp), **foldable space** (Wormhole), **higher strata of space** (Hyperspace), and **a superluminal mode of matter** (Tachyonic) need only a deep theory of space and matter, which every world reaches. They are **universal**.
- **Discreteness** — a thing here becoming a thing there without crossing between (Quantum Tunnelling) — exists only where matter is quantised: **atomic-only**.
- **A continuous rideable medium** (the Plenum Drive) exists only where space is a plenum: **elemental-only**.

So each world offers **five candidate methods** — the four universal ones plus its matter-axis method (Tunnelling in atomic worlds, Plenum in elemental ones) — of which exactly one is viable; **six methods across all worlds**. Tunnelling and Plenum are not an awkward asymmetry: they are the same axis that forks the whole physics tree, expressing itself one last time at the finale.

The universal methods reach the same **capability primitives** by two accounts, so an elemental civilisation pursuing (say) the Warp-equivalent invests in the *same kind* of research an atomic one does:

| Capability primitive | Atomic account | Elemental account | Method(s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Malleable space | spacetime geometry (QP08 + RC02) | the plenum's gravitation — the Aether's rarefaction, or the world-eddy's inward draw (QM-E27 + RC02) | Warp, Wormhole, Hyperspace; Plenum |
| Exotic matter | negative-energy matter (QP11), fabricated (MS08) | Aether made heavy or light; broken-cycle matter (QM-E06), fabricated (MS08) | Warp, Wormhole |
| Traversable geometry | black-hole horizons and closed timelike curves (RC04, RC07) | the dark star and closed circuits of the medium (RC04, RC07, read through Lorentzian aether-mechanics) | Wormhole |
| Medium energy | Casimir / zero-point (QP12) | aetheric vacuum / void-cycle tap (QM-E13) | Wormhole |
| Higher strata | extra dimensions (QP09) | strata of purer Aether, or a cycle of higher order than the five (QM-E12) | Hyperspace |
| Superluminal matter | a tachyonic sector beyond the Standard Model (QP15), and dark energy as a tachyonic field (RC06) | a superluminal form beyond the catalogue (QM-E28), and a superluminal mode of the medium (RC06) | Tachyonic (the medium's mode also Plenum) |
| Rideable medium | — (vacuum) | the celestial currents, charted from orbit (QM-E19) | Plenum Drive |
| Deep high-energy theory | QP05 | Unified Field Theory (QM-E04), which grants HIGH_ENERGY_PHYSICS | Tachyonic; shared prereq |
| Endgame power (W9) | ES14 Advanced Fission or ES08 Fusion Reactor | QM-E05 Re-Tempering / Self-Feeding Cycle Reactor | Warp, Hyperspace, Tachyonic |

Because the primitives line up, the **strategic lane of each method is preserved across worlds**: Wormhole is still the widest-investment "Renaissance path" whether you fold spacetime or fold the aether; Hyperspace is still the lean theorist's path whether it proves extra dimensions or charts the medium's higher strata; Tachyonic still centres on cosmology and a converter ring (NP15, or its mirror TH20 in elemental worlds). Relativity itself is dual-framed by Lorentzian aether-mechanics (see the Relativity & Cosmology note), so the RC nodes stand in both kinds of world.

The guidance reports follow the same shapes in elemental terms, and every method can be ruled out in both kinds of world: see *Conditions and Their Tests* above, which gives every condition in both ontologies.

*(Coherent light is itself dual-framed — see OP07: atomic worlds reach it by stimulated emission, elemental worlds by resonant aetheric/phase emission drawing on QM-E03 — so lasers, and the laser-dependent RC03 gravitational-wave route, exist in both kinds of world.)*

### How the Victories Are Built

Unciv's victories are data (`VictoryTypes.json`): each is a list of milestones — `Build [building]`, `Add all [spaceship parts] in capital`, `Have at least [amount] [countable]` and a few others — plus the spaceship parts it requires. Two space victories exist.

- **Scientific Victory — the FTL ship.** The intended path, built on the canonical space race so the familiar world keeps its familiar flow: `Build [Apollo Program]`, then add SS Booster ×3, SS Cockpit and the **SS FTL Drive**ⁿ to the capital. The Drive replaces the canonical SS Engine and is the only new part: a Civilian on the canonical recipe (`Spaceship part`; `Cannot be purchased`; `Only available <if [Apollo Program] is constructed>`; `Uncapturable`; `Can be added to [The Spaceship] in the Capital`; requires **Aluminum**; 1,500 production), whose one further requirement is written at world creation by the World-Truth ruleset derivation (§7.0): `Only available <when number of [Researched [P] Technologies] is more than [0]>`, where P is the prototype of the single method this world permits (§4, *The Prototype and the Hail Mary*) — so no failed prototype can satisfy it, and the puzzle this section describes is settled in one native countable. 4,500 production in all — exactly base Civ V's six parts at 750, the Drive taking the Engine's and Stasis Chamber's 1,500 — so the Spaceship Factory and Hubble keep their meaning. The canonical SS Engine and SS Stasis Chamber are removed.
- **Building the ship.** Parts are built in any city and added to the ship in the capital, and each of the jobs of building a starship counts once, because Unciv adds every percentage bonus to a construction into one sum.
  - *Fabrication* is canonical: a Spaceship Factory (+50%) in each city that builds parts, and Hubble (+25%) in its own.
  - *Lift* is the capital's launch complex — the Space Port (+25%), Mass Driver (+33%) or Gravitic Platform (+50%), each replacing the last — or the Space Elevator (+50%), which retires it and suspends every complex's bonus while it is held. *Assembly* is the Orbital Shipyard (+25%).
  - Every part must reach the capital to be launched, so lift and assembly are built only there and help every city. The capital's production is best spent on that infrastructure while other cities build the parts: a race decided by which work lies on the critical path. The infrastructure consists of ordinary buildings, destroyed if the capital falls, and — unlike the parts — it can be bought with Gold, which is how a one-city civilisation keeps pace.
  - *Exotic components* (the Exotic Matter Lab or Forge, the Exotic Matter Foundry, the Current Chart: +15% each) speed only the FTL Drive, and *propulsion* only the Boosters, the ship's sub-light stages: +10% for each breakthrough tagged ⟨Propulsion⟩, up to three.
  - The best city reaches +125% (+150% with the Elevator or in a gravitic world), against base Civ V's +75%. Counting the spaceship bonuses alone, it would build the whole ship there for about 1,650–1,900 production, against base Civ V's 2,570; other part-building cities reach +100%, so parallel building is faster in the same proportion.
- **Announcements.** Completing the prototype and the SS FTL Drive is announced to every civilisation, as base Civ V announces spaceship parts; committing to a prototype stays private.
- **Generation Ship Victory — the challenge.** `Build [Generation Ship]` and `Have at least [150] [Voyage]` (on Standard speed; the derivation scales the number to game speed). The Generation Ship (SE11) is a national wonder of 1,500 production that pauses its city's growth like a Settler, costs a citizen on completion, and `Provides [1] [Voyage]` — a stockpiled resource that counts the turns since launch, read by the milestone through `getResourceAmount`, which returns a stockpile's amount. Losing the launch city destroys the wonder and stops the clock. Unciv can offer a victory only as a checkbox that starts ticked, or hide it entirely (`Will not be chosen for new games`), so this one is offered and ticked — but at 150 turns from launch it wins only games that no one else is winning.

Guarantee Rule 2 holds through the FTL Drive (the viable method's build and prove rows are CORE or STANDARD, and its prototype is a Grand Endeavour), and the Generation Ship is reachable through STANDARD breakthroughs alone.

---

## 5. WORLD TRUTHS — VARIABLE LAWS OF PHYSICS

Each game generates a set of **World Truths** at creation: the governing laws of that particular universe, which must be *discovered* through play rather than assumed. Players are never told which Truths are set — they infer them from what works and what doesn't. This is the single system behind every kind of world variation in the game; the older "World Flag" vocabulary used in earlier drafts is retained only as the identifier names of individual Truths (e.g. `GUNPOWDER_ABSENT`).

The design problem is combinatorial explosion: naive per-breakthrough randomisation would create thousands of world configurations, each needing bespoke content and balance. The World Truths system instead produces enormous variety from bounded content through six design principles (below), yielding some fifteen thousand distinct physics signatures from a few dozen authored outcomes.

The section proceeds from the general principle (what varies and what doesn't), through the taxonomy of Truth families, to the specific axes in each family — including worked examples of the Geological and Historical families, which were the first worlds designed — and finishes with the anti-explosion principles and how the player learns their universe's Truths.

### What Varies and What Doesn't: Phenomena, Mechanisms, Frontiers

**Phenomena and craft are fixed.** Fire burns, metals smelt, crops grow, disease spreads, steam expands — in every game. The practical tree (tools, engines, agriculture, medicine-as-practice) depends on phenomena, not on why they happen, which is historically true: steam engines preceded thermodynamics, smelting preceded chemistry, quarantine preceded germ theory. Craft is craft everywhere.

**Discovery reveals reality; it never rewrites it.** A World Truth is a fact about the universe that holds from turn one, whether or not anyone has noticed. Discovering it changes what the civilisation *knows and can exploit* — never the underlying world. This has a strict authoring consequence: a breakthrough's effects must be framed as new *knowledge, capability, or infrastructure*, not as a change to terrain, resources, or the map that "switches on" at discovery. The clearest case is a resource reveal: the ore was always in the ground; Ore Identification lets you *see and mine* it, it does not place the ore. The same governs every Truth fork — caloric's conserved fluid, the vital force, and elemental transmutation were all true before their decisive experiments; the experiment only tells you *which* world you live in and lets you build on it. A subtle corollary the disease axis (FQ10) shows: a discovery must not become a *diagnostic tell* for the world's hidden Truth either. Because learning miasma theory only grants a new counter (drainage, ventilation) rather than changing how marshes behave, and because every disease world faces the same underlying pressure, a player cannot read the world's Truth off the map before the decisive experiment. When writing any breakthrough, prefer verbs like *reveals, enables, lets you build, counters, exploits* over *makes, becomes, turns X dangerous/valuable* — and check that its effects don't leak the value of an unresolved axis.

**Mechanisms are sampled — and no mechanism is privileged.** *Why* things happen — the deep explanation — varies per world, and becomes discoverable the moment a civilisation starts asking why (Natural Philosophy, Classical era). Is heat motion, or a fluid? Are elements emergent, or fundamental? Is disease carried by germs, by bad air, or by imbalance? These **Foundational Questions** resolve in the Classical through Early Modern eras and reshape the middle and late game.

**The Symmetry Principle.** Our own reality's answers — matter is atomic, heat is molecular motion, life is chemistry, disease is microbial — are **not** the game's default. They are simply the *highest-probability value* on each axis, and they are as contingent as any rival. This has a consequence the earlier drafts missed: **every Truth value, including the orthodox one, gates its own exclusive cluster of breakthroughs and forecloses its rivals'.** Atomic theory is not "the normal tree that alternatives fall away from"; it is one fork among several, and it *only exists in atomic worlds*. The Nuclear→Quantum spine, and with it the silicon age of Electronics and Computing, is predicated on matter being atomic — in an Elemental world that spine never forms, and a structurally equivalent one grows in its place (see *Niche Equivalents*, below); the shared craft of electronics and computing reaches it by other roads. A vitalist world lacks synthetic organic chemistry the way an atomic world lacks vital-essence engineering: each is blind to the other's cluster.

**"Forks," not holes.** On a foundational axis the losing *theory* dead-ends — its breakthrough yields its consolation and its report (§1.2) — but the civilisation does not: the decisive experiment routes it onto a genuinely different chain occupying the *same structural niche* (an energy pillar, a materials pillar, a computation substrate, a medicine paradigm). A genuine hole is reserved for *frontier* axes, where the honest answer is sometimes simply "this is impossible in your universe" (fusion won't ignite; exotic matter won't hold). Foundational axes fork; frontier axes may leave a hole. Both are real knowledge, and Guarantee Rule 2 ensures no hole ever blocks victory.

**Frontiers are sampled.** The questions even a Modern-era civilisation cannot settle — fusion, superconductivity ceilings, vacuum energy, FTL — resolve in the Atomic and Space eras. Here dead ends are legitimate: a frontier that closes teaches you what your universe forbids.

*The uncertainty the player feels at every era's edge is the same uncertainty real natural philosophers felt at theirs — and there is no "correct" tech tree hiding behind the variation, only the tree this universe permits.*

### Niche Equivalents: Filling Truth-Dependent Pillars

When a Truth forecloses an entire structural pillar, an equivalent pillar must grow in its place so that no world is crippled and every world reaches the endgame. A **niche equivalent** occupies the same functional slot (early-energy, high-energy, advanced-materials, computation, mass-medicine) through the mechanism its Truth permits. Niche equivalents are *disjoint clusters* (Principle 1): a world runs exactly one fork per niche, so total authored content grows additively, not multiplicatively.

| Niche (structural role) | Orthodox fork (our reality) | Alternative fork(s) | Governing axis |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Deep theory of matter** | NP02 Atomic Model → Nuclear + Quantum Physics | *Elemental:* Quintessic Theory (QM-E chain) — the fifth element/aether as substrate | FQ9 |
| **High-energy / endgame power** | Nuclear Fission & Fusion (NP03/NP04) | *Elemental:* Quintessic Reactor (forced elemental transmutation releases bound energy) | FQ9 (+FQ2) |
| **Computation substrate** | Transistor → semiconductor → silicon (EN02+) | *Elemental:* Resonance Computing (harmonic elemental states); *Process-Elemental:* Cycle-Logic (wu xing state machines) | FQ9 |
| **The chemistry of life** | Abiotic synthesis — Organic Chemistry (CH07) making life's compounds from dead matter | *Vitalist:* vital-essence engineering (GN12 Essence Reservoir → GN13 Vital Automaton → GN14 Animate Intelligence), while Organic Chemistry keeps refining but cannot create life's chemistry from dead matter | FQ11 |
| **Energy storage & transfer** | Kinetic thermodynamics + batteries | *Caloric:* caloric reservoirs & heat-fluid transfer (TH11/TH12) | FQ12 |
| **Mass medicine / disease control** | Germ theory → antibiotics, vaccines (MD05/MD06) | *Miasma:* environmental purification (MD16); *Humoral:* humoral pharmacology (MD18) | FQ10 |
| **Strategic deterrent** | Nuclear Weapons (NP05) | *Elemental:* Quintessic Warhead (QM-E20) — bound-energy release made portable | FQ9 |
| **Deep-space signalling** | Quantum Communication (QP14) | *Elemental:* Aether Telegraphy / Flow Signalling (QM-E18) — modulating the plenum; long-ranged but not unhackable | FQ9 |

The orthodox fork is not written as "the tree"; it is one column of this table, chosen ~55–70% of the time per axis. The alternative columns are the new content this revision adds or formalises. Deep Reconciliation (Principle 6) still applies: by the late Atomic/Space eras the forks *converge* on shared frontier physics (FTL, orbital engineering, the victory conditions), so the divergence is an era-spanning experience, not a set of permanent parallel games.

### Truth Axes

Each open question is an **axis** with 2–5 mutually exclusive values, sampled independently at world creation (subject to consistency rules below). Four families — Geological, Historical, **Foundational** (Classical–Early Modern mechanisms, detailed above), and Frontier:

**Geological Truths** (Removal family): did this planet lay down fossil fuels? Does gunpowder chemistry work? These change what exists — a fossil-free world's geology is data (no seams, no wells), and breakthroughs whose premise it falsifies are absent (§1.2) — and they shape the whole industrial arc. Their worked examples are the No-Gunpowder and No-Fossil-Fuels worlds (below).

**Historical Contingencies** (Enabling family): did the Antikythera tradition flourish? Are mainsprings extraordinary? These remove nothing — they are different *histories*, made likelier by easing a RARE gateway to UNCOMMON difficulty. Their worked examples are the Early Steam, Enhanced Springs, and Antikythera worlds (below).

**Frontier Physics**: the sampled answers to the questions even a Modern-era civilisation cannot settle. Every affected breakthrough *exists in every game*; what varies is whether it **validates** or **dead-ends**:

| Axis | Values (weights) | Content hooks |
|---|---|---|
| **FQ1: FTL Method** | One of the five candidates valid for this world's Matter value (FQ9), equal weights — *the master axis*; six methods exist in all | Section 4. Sampled *after* FQ9: Tunnelling is a candidate only in Corpuscular worlds, the Plenum Drive only in Elemental worlds. Sets derived truths — the deciding rows of §4's evidence map: the viable method's conditions hold, and every other condition holds or fails at even odds, subject to the exclusions and the guarantee in §4, *Generation Rules*. Every deciding row validates where its method is the world's, and dead-ends with a clear negative report everywhere else. |
| **FQ2: Fusion Practicality** | Attainable (60%) / Marginal (15%) / Impossible (25% → legacy FUSION_ABSENT) | Marginal: NP04 works but ES08 underperforms — deep fission (NP-F chain) stays competitive without removal. **Moot in Elemental worlds** (there are no nuclei to fuse). |
| **FQ3: Superconductivity Ceiling** | Room-temperature (35%) / Cryogenic-only (65%) | EL12 validates or dead-ends. Cryogenic-only worlds pay more for maglev, fusion containment, railguns. |
| **FQ4: Vacuum Energy** | Extractable (20%) / Trace-only (80%) | ES12 (Zero-Point Energy) validates or dead-ends — in elemental worlds QM-E07 (Aether Tension Engine) does the same; QP12 (Casimir Effect Amplification) and QM-E13 (Aetheric Vacuum) always work as science either way. |
| **FQ5: Quantum Foundation** | Orthodox (70%) / Deterministic (30%) | QP13 validates in Deterministic worlds — its Determinist Institute reveals each breakthrough as soon as the civilisation becomes eligible for it. |
| **FQ6: Biological Depth** | Quantum-coherent (40%) / Classical-only (60%) | GN10 validates or dead-ends; Classical-only worlds cap bio-computing at GN09 — no biological path to quantum computing or to Tunnelling. **Moot in Elemental worlds** (no quantum coherence exists to be biological); sampled only when FQ9 = Corpuscular. |
| **FQ7: Gravitic Engineering** | Possible (15%) / Impossible (85%) | RC08 (Gravitational Coupling Experiment) validates → opens RC09/RC10 chain; dead-ends otherwise. |

### Foundational Questions: Variable Laws Before the Frontier

Four Foundational Axes govern the deep mechanisms of the pre-modern and industrial world.
