Each is resolved by a **decisive experiment** — a breakthrough that exists in every game, modelled on the experiment that settled the question in our own history. Until that experiment, *rival* theories coexist, each grants partial benefits, and each drives research — exactly as caloric theory productively drove real physics in our own history — Carnot built the theory of the heat engine on it.

**What qualifies as a World Truth.** A World Truth must be a fact that eventually makes different predictions — never a choice of description. Phlogiston was cut on this test (v3.71). A consistent phlogiston world is our own world described through missing oxygen: phlogiston behaves as a *hole* in oxygen, exactly as a semiconductor's positive charge-carriers are holes in its electrons. Its negative mass is the mass of the oxygen that is not there, so every weighing agrees with ours, and the account breaks only where it treats the hole as a thing — trying to isolate it, or mistaking it for hydrogen. Each remaining axis makes a claim some laboratory can decide (in our world, Joule's paddle-wheel, Snow's pump and Wöhler's urea decided three of them), so each is a genuine truth. The reasoning is kept in *Combustion Physics* (Model III).

| Axis | Values (weights) | Decisive experiment | If an alternative is true |
|---|---|---|---|
| **FQ9: Matter** | Corpuscular (55%) / **Substance-Elemental** (25%) / **Process-Elemental** (20%) | CH19 (Systematic Element Analysis) | *Substance-Elemental (Greek/Aristotelian):* the four elements are fundamental and interconvertible — **transmutation by recombination works**. Opens CH20 (Transmutation Practice), CH21 (Philosopher's Stone). *Process-Elemental (Chinese wu xing):* the five phases and their generative/destructive cycles are real forces — **transformation, not composition, is fundamental**. Opens CH22 (Five-Phase Theory), CH23 (Generative Cycle Engine), CH24 (Phase-Destruction Warfare). **Either elemental value forecloses the atomic spine** (Nuclear Physics, Quantum Physics, semiconductor Computing never cohere) and instead opens the **Quintessic Physics** focus (QM-E chain) as the niche equivalent — deep matter theory, high-energy endgame power, and a resonance/cycle-logic computation substrate. CH06 (Periodic Table) dead-ends; Transmutation Practice (the Elemental Codex) or Five-Phase Theory (the Phase Codex) grants the MATTER_THEORY token instead. |
| **FQ10: Disease** | Germ (60%) / **Miasma** (20%) / **Humoral** (20%) | MD15 (Contagion Experiments — Pasteur's swan-neck flask) | All worlds suffer the *same* disease pressure (plague events, growth penalties in large/dense cities) — the phenomenon is fixed and gives nothing away early. What differs is only which **counter** works, and that isn't revealed until the decisive experiment (MD15). *Germ:* microbe control (MD05 → antibiotics). *Miasma:* MD16 (Miasma Engineering) — drainage and ventilation; only here does draining marsh/fen tiles yield a happiness bonus, and only here can foul air be weaponised. *Humoral:* MD17/MD18 — humour-balancing pharmacology and civic temperament. Because the counters differ only in *form* (all deliver comparable disease control) and none produces a distinctive early terrain effect, an experienced player cannot read the world's disease Truth from the map before running MD15. MD05 (Germ Theory) dead-ends in non-germ worlds; the others dead-end in germ worlds. |
| **FQ11: Nature of Life** | Mechanist (70%) / **Vitalist** (30%) | CH25 (Organic Synthesis Attempt — Wöhler's urea) | *Vitalist:* a measurable vital force separates living from dead matter, and synthesis-from-inorganic *fails*. Opens GN12 (Vital Essence Isolation — the Essence Reservoir), GN13 (Animating Principle — the Vital Automaton) and GN14 (Animate Intelligence). Organic Chemistry (CH07) still stands and still refines, but cannot create life's chemistry from dead matter; everything downstream reads LIFE_THEORY, which GN12 supplies. |
| **FQ12: Nature of Heat** | Kinetic (70%) / **Caloric** (30%) | TH10 (Mechanical Equivalent of Heat — Joule's paddle-wheel, Rumford's cannon) | *Caloric:* heat is a real conserved fluid. Opens TH11 (Caloric Storage — heat banked as a substance in Reservoirs and spent on Heat Exchanges) and TH12 (Caloric Projection — the Caloric Lance and Thermal Ward, fielded from banked heat). TH01's kinetic account dead-ends; the caloric model grants THERMO_THEORY instead. |

**Understanding Tokens.** Downstream prerequisites never name a specific theory — they name the *understanding* it provides, so the practical tree is theory-agnostic. MATTER_THEORY comes from CH06, the Elemental Codex, or the Phase Codex; DISEASE_THEORY from MD05, MD16, or MD17; MASS_MEDICINE (empire-wide disease control) from MD06 (germ antibiotics), MD16 (miasma purification), or MD18 (humoral pharmacology); LIFE_THEORY from CH07 (mechanist) or GN12 (vitalist); THERMO_THEORY from TH01 (kinetic) or TH11 (caloric). Every prerequisite that names CH06, MD05, CH07, or TH01 is read as its token. The steam engine runs in a caloric world — its engineers simply understand it differently, and correctly *for their universe*, as Carnot did in ours.

**The deep-physics tokens (Symmetry Principle).** Two further tokens let the late tree accept either the atomic or the elemental foundation without duplicating every downstream row:
- **HIGH_ENERGY_PHYSICS** — granted by NP02 (Atomic Model) in atomic worlds or QM-E01 (Quintessence Theory) in elemental worlds. Any breakthrough naming NP02, QP01, QP05, or QP08 as a prerequisite reads the token (the deep-high-energy and malleable-space primitives); in elemental worlds the Quintessic chain (QM-E01/QM-E04) satisfies it.
- **EXOTIC_MATTER** — granted by QP11 (atomic route) or QM-E06 (aetheric route). Any FTL or frontier node naming QP11 reads the token. Exotic matter is one of several FTL capability primitives that elemental worlds reach by their own account — see *FTL in Elemental Worlds* (§4) for the full primitive-by-primitive mapping and the atomic-only/elemental-only method pair (D/F).
Frontier focuses that are atomic-specific (Nuclear Physics, Quantum Physics) and their elemental mirror (Quintessic Physics) are mutually exclusive per world, selected by FQ9 — a civilisation enters one or the other, never both.

**The Deep Reconciliation principle.** By the Atomic era, deeper investigation reconnects every world to the shared late tree: the classical elements reveal inner structure; caloric is unmasked as molecular motion after all; the vital force proves to be chemistry of unusual subtlety — *or, in these universes, the reverse reduction holds and our physics is the special case.* The alternative chains' unlocks persist forever (the Stone keeps transmuting, the Turnings keep turning, the caloric reservoirs stay charged), but late-game content converges. This is not a retreat — it is exactly what happened in our own history when chemistry reduced to physics without invalidating a single reaction. Wrongness at one level is compatible with truth at another, and this principle is what keeps four foundational axes from cascading into a dozen incompatible late-game trees.

**Candidate future axes** (the framework extends cleanly; each would multiply variety again): Nature of Light (corpuscular / wave / dual), Luminiferous Aether (real / absent — would derive from the FTL master axis to avoid contradiction), Cosmological Structure (the path to heliocentrism varies even where the destination doesn't), Spontaneous Generation (does life arise from non-life?). Each must first pass the test above — a fact that eventually makes different predictions, not a choice of description — and then needs only a decisive experiment, two or three authored outcomes, a token, and a reconciliation clause.

### Worked Examples: The First Worlds

These worlds were designed first and remain the clearest illustrations of the Geological (Removal) and Historical (Enabling) families, together with the Impossible value of the Fusion frontier (FQ2). Each is a specific value on a Truth axis, described narratively.

#### The No-Gunpowder World
*World Truth: GUNPOWDER_ABSENT (Removal, ~15% of games)*
- WA10 (Gunpowder) does not exist here (a Removal Truth, §1.2). **MI08 (Saltpeter) still exists** — a preservative, and a fertiliser: it feeds Nitrate Fertiliser (FA16) with no musket competing for it.
- **Repeating Crossbow** (WA17) dominant ranged unit through Medieval/Early Modern
- **Pneumatic weaponry** (WA18) silent alternative ranged path
- **Chemical explosives** (CH08) still arrive in Modern era for mining/rocketry — different chemistry, not dependent on gunpowder
- **Railguns** (WA19) become the long-term ranged solution
- Armor and fortification remain dominant longer; cavalry tactics have extended strategic relevance
- Naval warfare shifts toward ramming, fire ships, and boarding actions far longer than in our history
- Cannons (WA22) do not exist either, so medieval fortifications remain viable into the Modern era

#### The No-Fusion World
*World Truth: FQ2 (Fusion Practicality) = Impossible — legacy name FUSION_ABSENT, ~25% of games*
- NP04 (Nuclear Fusion) never validates: its dependents (NP06, RK10, RK14, ES08) dead-end. (In the softer *Marginal* value of FQ2, ~15% of games, NP04 works but ES08 underperforms — the deep fission chain stays competitive rather than mandatory.)
- **Competing Solutions.** Advanced Fuel Cycles (NP-F01) and Advanced Fission (ES14) are the alternatives whose primaries — Nuclear Fusion and the Fusion Reactor — dead-end here, so they lose their detour margin and can never be locked out (§1.2):
  - the deep fission chain (NP-F01 to NP-F04) becomes the primary advanced energy path
  - Advanced Fission approaches fusion output through refinement
  - **Micro-fission reactors** (NP-F02): miniaturisation advantage fusion civs never develop
  - **Fission fragment propulsion** (NP-F03): comparable to fusion drives with superior endurance
- Renewables, geothermal, and solar get more investment out of necessity; energy scarcity is a strategic factor
- Thermonuclear weapons (NP06) dead-end — fission weapons only, limiting the destructive ceiling
- The Fusion Drive (RK10) and Bussard Ramjet (RK14) dead-end on their atomic route; fission propulsion and ion drives must suffice. (In elemental worlds fusion is moot — there are no nuclei — and these drives run on the Re-Tempering Reactor instead.)

#### The No-Fossil-Fuels World
*World Truth: FOSSIL_FUELS_ABSENT (Removal, ~10% of games)*

**The model.** This world's forests never became coal and its seas never became oil. A real — and contested — hypothesis holds that coal formation in our own world slowed once white-rot fungi evolved the enzymes that digest lignin; here they evolved first, so the great forests rotted where they fell. Nor did its seas bury their plankton in the stagnant basins that cook oil, so there is no oil, no gas and no bitumen. Everything a layman sees is unchanged except what is missing — the forests, farms, winds and rivers are the same — and because decay needs air, waterlogged bogs still lay down peat.

**What is removed.** Coal and Oil *deposits*: the Removal Truth gives both resources ⟨Doesn't generate naturally⟩ — the world's geology, expressed as data, which the map generator honours. With no seams or wells there is nothing for Coal Discovery (MI06) or Petroleum Discovery (MI07) to find, so they do not exist here, and neither do the breakthroughs that need them — the coke Blast Furnace, the Internal Combustion Engine, Petrochemistry, and the Coal and Oil Power Plants. That is the ordinary absence of §1.2 — a breakthrough whose premise is false in this world — not a rule that works differently. A Substance civilisation can still transmute Coal and Oil lodes once it knows the fuels by the charcoal and alcohol roads (CH20): its fungibility at its most valuable.

**Nothing else works differently.** The Coal and Oil *resources* still exist, and every building and unit that needs them needs them here as everywhere: the Factory and the Ironclad burn Coal; tanks, battleships and aircraft burn Oil. Only the source of the fuel changes — and the sources are the same technologies, with the same costs, that a coal-poor civilisation in any world could develop. They are the Competing Solutions whose primaries this world lacks, so each loses its detour margin, takes its primary's tier and can never be locked out (§1.2):
- **Coal is charcoal.** A Charcoal Kiln (ME08) provides 1 Coal while its city works three fuel tiles — forest, jungle or a Peat Cutting — at −10% Production.
- **Oil is grown.** A Distillery Fuel Depot (MC09) provides 1 Oil while its city works three Farms, at −20% growth.
- **Engines.** The Landship and Tank follow either the internal combustion or the alcohol engine.

In a world with coal and oil these are outclassed by any seam or well, and most civilisations never develop them; here they are how industry happens.

**The strategic experience.** Energy is never solved the way coal and oil solve it: woods and bogs become coal, farms become oil, and the same land must feed people. Industry is smaller — a kiln-fed Factory is worth its flat +4, never its percentage, and fuel caps at one kiln and one depot per city. Hydro, geothermal, tidal and solar power matter more, and nuclear power feels like liberation when it arrives. **Location:** fuel oil comes from farms, which every civilisation has; solid fuel needs three worked woodland or bog tiles, which are far commoner than coal seams, and charcoal trades like any Coal — so a fossil-free world is less unequal than a coal world, not more. **In a Caloric world** the Caloric Plant is a fuel-free grid plant running on banked heat. **Unique advantage:** no coal or oil smoke — each such plant costs a point of happiness elsewhere — so fossil-free cities run happier.

#### The Early Steam World
*World Truth: AEOLIPILE_VIABLE (Enabling, ~15% of games)*

- Hero's Aeolipile (MC07) is RARE in every world; in this one it is UNCOMMON. It unlocks the Thermodynamics focus centuries early, with the Steam Temple.
- **Early steam is atmospheric.** Pressure steam needs cylinders bored true, boilers that hold, and standardised parts — none of which exist before the industrial era. Vacuum steam needs none of them: the steam condenses and the air does the work. So the chain it seeds is The Weight of the Air (TH13, Classical) and the Atmospheric Engine (TH14, Medieval), each priced for its own era, which gives the early focus something it can afford.
- **Steam's one pre-industrial job is moving water:** fountains and town water first, then drained mines and mills away from the river. Nothing moves under its own power, and no factory runs on it; that waits for the Steam Engine, which the Atmospheric Engine supersedes. An Engine House is then converted rather than scrapped, as many Newcomen engines were refitted with Watt's condenser: a Steam Works goes up twice as fast in its city and retires it.
- **The head start is real but modest.** The Weight of the Air makes every Thermodynamics breakthrough ×0.9 for its holder — the field it has explored longest — and the Atmospheric Engine halves the Steam Engine's cost, since Watt's condenser began as a repair to a Newcomen model.

#### The Enhanced Springs World
*World Truth: ENHANCED_SPRINGS (Enabling, ~15% of games)*

- Mainspring Energy Storage (MC15) is UNCOMMON instead of RARE, and opens the Clockwork chain: the Clockwork Automaton (MC16), a worker that needs no pay; Spring-Powered Vehicles (MC17), which here rival early steam and need no fuel at all; and, with the Babbage engine, the Clockwork Analytical Engine (MC18), which opens Computing with no electrical prerequisite.
- A clockwork civilisation industrialises and computes in brass and steel, without coal or electricity; it will want both eventually, but the path buys it an early industrial era no one else can copy. **With No Fossil Fuels** (about 1.5% of games) it becomes a true clockpunk civilisation — spring-wound industry, clockwork computing and alcohol-fuelled chemistry, without a lump of coal.

#### The Antikythera Tradition
*World Truth: ANTIKYTHERA_TRADITION (Enabling, ~20% of games)*

- The Antikythera-Style Mechanism (O10) is UNCOMMON here, and seeds its chain: the Mechanical Ephemeris (O13), the Tide Computing Engine (O14) and the Mechanical Weather Predictor (O15). Each is useful in itself, at sea or on the farm, and any of them halves the cost of the Babbage engine (MC14).
- **With Enhanced Springs** (about 3% of games) the two traditions meet: mechanical general-purpose computing can arrive in the Early Modern era, bypassing the electrical spine altogether. A player who finds O10 early should ask what kind of world this is.

### The Six Anti-Explosion Principles

1. **Disjoint clusters.** Each axis touches its own set of breakthroughs. Combinations need no bespoke content — variety emerges from independent clusters interacting through the shared tree.
2. **Shared content, variant outcomes — forks and dead ends.** Foundational axes **fork**: each value routes the civilisation onto an exclusive cluster occupying a shared structural niche (Niche Equivalents, above). No value is a "consolation" — the orthodox and alternative forks are equally real, equally complete paths to the endgame. Frontier axes may instead **dead-end**: a breakthrough that *validates* grants full effects and may reveal follow-ups; one that *dead-ends* grants consolation Insight and a **clear negative Science Advisor report** (negative signals are always unambiguous). The distinction matters: a foundational fork never leaves a hole (the alternative pillar fills it), whereas a frontier dead end legitimately closes a door — and Guarantee Rule 2 ensures no closed door ever blocks victory. Either way, knowing what is true or impossible in *this* universe is real knowledge, never a wasted turn.
3. **Master-axis derivation and cross-axis implication.** Where clusters would overlap or where one Truth bears on another, axes are sampled in dependency order and earlier results *derive* or *bias* later ones, so no contradictory world is ever generated. Established links:
   - **Matter (FQ9) is sampled first, then FTL (FQ1) as the master axis for the frontier.** Because Tunnelling requires discreteness and the Plenum Drive requires a continuous plenum, the FTL candidate set depends on the Matter value: A/B/C/D/E in Corpuscular worlds, A/B/C/E/F in Elemental worlds. FQ1 is then drawn from those five and derives the rest: the viable method's conditions are made to hold and the rest are drawn at even odds, subject to the guarantee (§4, *Generation Rules*). An Elemental Matter value also renders FQ6 (Biological Depth) moot — Bio-Quantum Coherence (GN10) requires quantum coherence, which elemental worlds lack — so FQ6 is sampled only in Corpuscular worlds.
   - **Elemental matter → computation & quantum substrate (FQ9).** Elemental worlds never form the atomic→quantum spine; their Quintessic Theory instead biases FQ5 (Quantum Foundation) toward the *Deterministic* value (a continuous elemental substrate is naturally hidden-variable), and their computation niche is filled by Resonance/Cycle-Logic rather than semiconductors.
   - **Caloric → energy frontiers (FQ12 → FQ4).** A conserved-heat-fluid universe treats energy as a manipulable substance, so caloric worlds bias vacuum energy (FQ4) toward extractable.
   - **Vitalist → biological frontiers (FQ11 → FQ6).** A universe with a real vital force is one where biology reaches deeper: vitalist worlds bias FQ6 (Biological Depth) toward quantum-coherent, feeding the biological FTL route (Tunnelling via GN10).
   No contradictory world (e.g. a Warp world where exotic matter fails) can be sampled.
4. **Bounded axis count.** Eleven axes (7 Frontier + 4 Foundational), ~2.6 values each ≈ **15,000+ distinct physics signatures**. The Symmetry Principle adds authored content for the alternative forks (niche equivalents) but *additively*: because forks are disjoint clusters filling shared niches, a world instantiates exactly one fork per niche. That is additive content producing multiplicative variety. Each future axis multiplies the signature count again.
5. **Constraint-checked sampling.** After sampling, the generator verifies all Guaranteed Path Integrity rules (Section 6); violating combinations are re-sampled. Victory is possible in every world that can exist.
6. **Deep Reconciliation.** Foundational alternatives never fork the late game: every mechanism, probed deeply enough, reconnects to shared Atomic-era physics while its exclusive unlocks persist. Divergence is an era-long experience, not a permanent tree split — which is why four foundational axes cost ~30 authored outcomes rather than four full alternate trees.

### Frontier Questions: How the Player Learns the Truth

The FTL Science Advisor reports (Section 4) generalise to *every* axis. Each axis is a **Frontier Question** the game silently poses; specific experiment breakthroughs answer it:

- **Foundational axes** answer earlier, through their decisive experiments: FQ9 upon CH19 (matter decomposes into corpuscles, four elements, or five phases), FQ10 upon MD15 (the flask stays clear, the air sickens, or the humours imbalance), FQ11 upon CH25 (urea forms from inorganics — or stubbornly refuses), FQ12 upon TH10 (friction yields limitless heat, or a conserved fluid)
- FQ2 answers upon NP04 research maturity ("ignition sustained!" / "confinement losses persist beyond every prediction")
- FQ3 answers upon EL12 attempts ("resistance vanished at room temperature" / "the ceiling appears fundamental")
- FQ4 upon ES12; FQ5 upon QP13; FQ6 upon GN10; FQ7 upon RC08
- FQ1 answers through the accumulating FTL evidence system (Section 4)

The investigation now spans the *whole game*, not just the endgame: an Early Modern civilisation analysing its first elements, or boiling broth in a swan-necked flask, is already discovering the laws of its universe, and the answer reshapes its next ten eras.

The mid-to-late game thereby becomes a research *investigation*: the player is not just climbing a tree but interrogating their universe. Two games with identical starts diverge because the universes themselves differ — and the player who probes efficiently (cheap experiments first, reading negative signals correctly) gains real strategic advantage over one who assumes this world resembles the last one. Axis-specific reports must be worded distinctly enough that (for example) a gravitic-positive world is not mistaken for Warp-FTL evidence; mild thematic overlap is acceptable and even desirable, but *definitive* signals are always axis-specific.

---

## 6. GAME BALANCE RULES

### World Truths
Each game generates a set of World Truths at creation (Section 5). Breakthroughs are absent from a game in two ways: the **Removal Truths** (historically called Removal Flags) delete them, and the **foundational axes** leave no room for the branches belonging to the values a world did not draw; the frontier axes, and the rival theories a decisive experiment settles, leave breakthroughs present but decide whether they *validate* or *dead-end* (Section 1.2 sets out all three). Removal Truths and the RARE-easing **Enabling Truths** (Enabling Flags) are summarised here; the Foundational and Frontier axes are detailed in Section 5.

**Removal Truths** remove specific breakthroughs. Their key interaction is with Competing Solutions: an alternative whose primary is removed loses its detour margin, takes the primary's tier and can never be locked out — it becomes the way.

- **GUNPOWDER_ABSENT**: ~15% of games. WA10 (Gunpowder) and all gunpowder-dependent breakthroughs removed. No UNCOMMON alternative — the alternative weapons (crossbow, pneumatic, railgun) are entirely different branches.
- **FOSSIL_FUELS_ABSENT**: ~10% of games. MI06 (Coal) and MI07 (Petroleum) are removed, and with them Petrochemistry (CH09), which needs Petroleum. The Blast Furnace (ME08) and the Internal Combustion Engine (MC09) open without them — by charcoal and by distilled alcohol — and their Charcoal Kilns and Distillery Fuel Depots make the Coal and Oil, which the Coal and Oil Power Plants then burn. Bio-Organic Synthesis (CH09-W) can never be locked out there, its primary being absent, which makes it the primary chemistry path; the Biomass and Bio-Fuel Plants remain Competing Solutions, as in every world.

Note that fusion is **not** a Removal Truth in the current model: it is governed by the Frontier axis **FQ2 (Fusion Practicality)**, whose *Impossible* value (~25% of games) reproduces the old FUSION_ABSENT experience — NP04 and its dependents never validate, and the deep fission chain (NP-F01+) can never be obsoleted. The *Marginal* value (~15%) is a softer outcome the old flag couldn't express: fusion works but underperforms, keeping deep fission competitive. Legacy "FUSION_ABSENT" references elsewhere in the document denote the FQ2-Impossible world.

**Enabling Truths** don't remove anything. Instead, they make certain RARE gateway breakthroughs significantly easier to discover — effectively lowering them to UNCOMMON difficulty while set.

- **AEOLIPILE_VIABLE**: ~15% of games. MC07 (Hero's Aeolipile) treated as UNCOMMON difficulty instead of RARE. Early steam becomes a realistic possibility in the Classical era.
- **ENHANCED_SPRINGS**: ~15% of games. MC15 (Mainspring Energy Storage) treated as UNCOMMON difficulty instead of RARE. The entire Clockwork path becomes dramatically more viable.
- **ANTIKYTHERA_TRADITION**: ~20% of games. O10 (Antikythera-Style Mechanism) treated as UNCOMMON difficulty instead of RARE. The analog computation chain becomes accessible.

Truths combine: ~2% chance of Enhanced Springs + No Fossil Fuels ("clockpunk"), ~3% chance of Antikythera + Enhanced Springs ("mechanical computing"), etc. All twelve axes (Section 5) are sampled together, subject to master-axis derivation and constraint-checking. FTL method selection (FQ1) is *not* independent of the other axes: it is sampled after the Matter axis (FQ9), from the five methods valid for that world (D atomic-only, F elemental-only), and then derives the frontier truths as Principle 3 (Section 5) describes.

### Guaranteed Path Integrity
1. At least one complete path from Primitive to Interstellar using only CORE breakthroughs, regardless of which World Truths are set. No terrain condition stands on it (v3.117–v3.118): its only environmental conditions are iron in territory, which trade or conquest can supply (§1.2), and a city of five (Specialization of Labor)
2. Exactly one FTL method viable per game, its build and prove rows always CORE or STANDARD *in that world*. Six methods exist; Tunnelling is atomic-only and Plenum elemental-only (a matter-axis mirror pair), so each world offers five candidates. World creation draws the viable method and the world's conditions by §4's *Generation Rules*, whose guarantee keeps the viable method's build and prove rows reachable whatever the world's other truths; the universal methods' atomic rows resolve to their Quintessic equivalents per *FTL in Elemental Worlds* (§4). Power for the brute-force methods (W9) is always reachable: three ES06 Nuclear Fission Plants (CORE) match one advanced fission plant, as do ES14 Advanced Fission or ES08 Fusion alone; in elemental worlds, ES15 Quintessic Plants or QM-E05 (Re-Tempering Reactor).
3. At least one energy path to Space-era power levels — the Nuclear Fission Plant (ES06, CORE) in Corpuscular worlds, the Quintessic Power Plant (ES15) fed by Bound-Energy Release (QM-E02, CORE) in Elemental worlds
4. Writing (SO04) or alternative (SO09) always available
5. At least one metal (copper or iron) always available via S04 + F04. Strategic resource trading allows access without local deposits.
6. Scientific Method (PH04) always available
7. At least one ranged weapon path reaches Modern era (crossbow is STANDARD and always discoverable)
8. Mass iron/steel production always achievable: the Blast Furnace (ME08) is CORE and opens with either fuel, Coal Discovery or Charcoal Production, and with either mill, the Water Mill or the Windmill, so it exists in every world and needs no river. Where there are no coal seams, its Charcoal Kilns make Coal from worked fuel tiles — Forest, Jungle or Peat Cutting — and trade can supply the rest (§1.2).
9. Vehicles/motorisation always achievable: the Internal Combustion Engine (MC09) is CORE and opens with either fuel — petroleum, or distilled alcohol — so it exists in every world. Where there are no wells, its Distillery Fuel Depots make Oil from worked Farms, and trade can supply the rest (§1.2).
10. Polymer/materials science path always reachable (CH09-W bio-organic synthesis, a STANDARD Competing Solution; in FOSSIL_FUELS_ABSENT worlds its primary is absent, so it has no detour margin and can never be locked out). Its prerequisites are CORE and STANDARD only: Mycological Knowledge speeds it but is not required.
11. **World Truth sampling is constraint-checked**: after axes are sampled (Section 5), the generator verifies rules 1–10 hold; violating combinations are re-sampled. The FTL master axis derives dependent truths so contradictory worlds cannot exist.
12. **Victory is never dice-locked**: every breakthrough required for the viable FTL method is CORE or STANDARD, all of which can be Revealed through theory or proximity, and any Revealed breakthrough with Discovered prerequisites can be completed deterministically as a Grand Endeavour (Section 1.5), whether or not its idea has occurred. A STANDARD idea the victory waits on comes back by itself if its moment passes (rediscovery on demand, §1.2, v3.120). A player who pursues victory methodically cannot be stopped by chance — only slowed by it.

### Knowledge Diffusion
See Section 1.2 for full details. Summary:
- After the Printing Press: CORE and STANDARD breakthroughs held by half a civilisation's contacts arrive free 10 turns later
- After the Internet: 5 turns, UNCOMMON included; RARE never diffuse, and what the old roll bonuses become is proposed, not decided (§1.2)
- UNIQUE breakthroughs are always exempt from diffusion

### The "Almost Discovered" Mechanic
When a breakthrough under study passes four-fifths of its meter: "*Your scholars report promising developments in [Focus Area]...*" (the focus, never the breakthrough: meters stay hidden).

### Technology Trading & Espionage
- **Trading**: Breakthrough can be traded between civs. Recipient gains 75% effectiveness until they accumulate matching Insight through their own research (representing full local understanding).
- **Espionage**: Stolen breakthroughs operate at 50% effectiveness.
- **Reverse Engineering**: Chance from captured units/cities.
- **UNIQUE breakthroughs**: Cannot be traded or stolen. The special buildings, units, and abilities from UNIQUEs are exclusive to the discovering civilisation. Other civs can see the effects but cannot replicate them. This is the primary mechanism for civilisation differentiation in the mid-to-late game.
- **Strategic Resources**: Active trade agreements importing a resource count as "in territory" for prerequisite purposes. Losing the agreement means losing access.

### Competing Solutions Reference

Every Competing Solutions group (§1.2). Each alternative is STANDARD with a detour margin of ×1.5 while its primary exists in this world; discovering the primary first locks the alternative out for that civilisation.

| Goal | Primary | Alternative | If the primary arrives later |
|---|---|---|---|
| Spanning a roof | Arch Construction (CO03) | Corbel Arch/Vault (CO05) | superseded |
| Mounted shock troops | Cavalry Tactics (WA07) | Chariot Warfare (WA08) | superseded |
| Bulk inland transport | Railway Network (CO23) | Industrial Canal Network (WM11) | superseded |
| A prime mover from fire | Steam Engine (MC08) | Atmospheric Engine (TH14) | superseded — no new Engine Houses; existing ones keep pumping until converted into a Steam Works |
| Industrial chemistry | Petrochemistry (CH09) | Bio-Organic Synthesis (CH09-W) | persists — it needs no Oil |
| Thermal power | Coal Power Plant (ES03) | Biomass Power Plant (ES03-W) | persists — it needs no Coal and makes no smoke |
| Flexible power | Oil Power Plant (ES05) | Alcohol/Biogas Power Plant (ES05-W) | persists — it needs no Oil |
| Power beyond basic fission | Nuclear Fusion (NP04) | Advanced Fuel Cycles (NP-F01) | persists — the deep fission chain it opens keeps its micro-reactors and endurance |
| The endgame plant | Fusion Reactor (ES08) | Advanced Fission (ES14) | persists — it is part of the deep fission chain |
| Fission fuel | Nuclear Fission Plant (ES06) | Thorium Reactor (NP08) | persists — it needs no Uranium and cannot melt down |
| A compact prime mover | Internal Combustion Engine (MC09) | Stirling Engine Mastery (TH08) | persists — it burns anything |
| Calculation | Programmable Computer (CP01) | Analog Computing Mastery (CP13) | superseded — but the perceptron road it opens persists |
| Machine intelligence | Artificial Intelligence (Narrow) (CP07) | Perceptron Machines (CP14) | persists — a city chooses a Perceptron Laboratory or an AI Lab |
| Treating infection | Antibiotics (MD06) | Phage Therapy (MD20) | persists — a city chooses a Phage Institute or a Medical Lab |
| Electrification | Power Grid (EL07) | Direct-Current Stations (EL14) | persists — it works where the network does not reach |
| Fixed nitrogen for the fields | Catalysis (CH12) | Nitrate Fertiliser (FA16) | superseded |

**Interaction with World Truths:** When a Removal Truth removes the primary, a foundational axis forecloses it, or a frontier axis dead-ends it (Nuclear Fusion in an FQ2-Impossible world), the alternative loses its margin, takes the primary's tier and can never be locked out — a dead-ended breakthrough locks out nothing. This is the core mechanism of the alternative industrial and energy paths: they add no breakthroughs; they *protect* existing ones from obsolescence.

### Dual-Path Discovery
Some breakthroughs can be discovered through multiple Focus Areas (noted on each breakthrough). These are discovered only once — whichever focus completes the study first claims it. This means a civilization investing in Farming might accidentally discover Catalysis (CH12) before a rival's Chemistry focus does.

---

## 7. BASE-GAME OBJECT ASSIGNMENTS (Unciv Port Mapping)

Because this mod **replaces the science tech tree** with the Focus/Insight/breakthrough system while leaving Unciv's Policy and Religion systems untouched, every wonder, building, tile improvement, and unit that Civ V/Unciv normally gates behind a *technology* must instead be gated behind a **breakthrough** — otherwise it would be available from turn one or never at all. (Policy branches need no such treatment: they unlock by **era**, and eras are reached by discovering breakthroughs, so they remain reachable automatically.)

The tables below assign every base-game object (wonders, buildings, tile improvements, and units) from the *Civ V – Gods & Kings* ruleset to the breakthrough that should unlock it. The middle column records the object's original Unciv `requiredTech` for provenance; the assignment picks the breakthrough that best matches that tech's era and theme. Where a breakthrough already names the building in its Unlocks column (e.g. CO01 Granary, WM05 Water Mill, OP03 Observatory, SF08 Harbor, MC04 Windmill, ES06 Nuclear Plant), this table simply confirms that pairing.

### 7.0 Mechanical Grounding — What a Breakthrough May Actually Grant

*Every effect in this document is being rewritten against Unciv's real capabilities, read from the repository rather than from memory. Sources: `docs/Modders/uniques.md` (the auto-generated list of all 832 unique templates and what each may be attached to), `docs/Modders/Unique-parameters.md` (the legal filter vocabularies), `docs/Modders/Mod-file-structure/*.md` (the fields a building, unit, improvement or tech may define), and the `Civ V - Gods & Kings` ruleset JSONs (`Techs`, `Buildings`, `Units`, `TileImprovements`, `TileResources`, `Terrains`, `Specialists`, `Eras`) for calibration. Version checked: repository `master`, September 2026.*

**What exists.** Seven stats — Food, Production, Gold, Science, Culture, Happiness, Faith. Buildings (with `cost`, `maintenance`, flat stats, `percentStatBonus`, `specialistSlots`, `greatPersonPoints`, `cityStrength`, `cityHealth`, `requiredResource`, `requiredBuilding`, `requiredNearbyImprovedResources`, `replaces`, and `uniques`); units (`unitType`, `cost`, `movement`, `strength`, `rangedStrength`, `range`, `requiredResource`, `promotions`, `uniques`); tile improvements (`terrainsCanBeBuiltOn`, `turnsToBuild`, stats, `uniques`); resources (Bonus/Luxury/Strategic, `revealedBy` a tech, improved by one improvement); the four specialists (Scientist +3 Science, Merchant +3 Gold, Artist +2 Culture, Engineer +2 Production — new specialist types are legal data in `Specialists.json`, each carrying its own Great Person points, and a new Great Person is a Civilian unit with `Is part of Great Person group` and consuming actions; both are used once, for the Sage and the Great Sage of Process worlds); Great People; Golden Ages; religion and Faith; city connections (which Unciv calls **Trade Routes** — roads or harbours to the capital); unit supply, XP, promotions, sight, movement, strength, healing, pillaging; embarkation; open borders, defensive pacts, research agreements, embassies; City-State influence; puppeting and annexation; nuclear weapons and fallout; an optional **espionage** mode (spies, `<when espionage is enabled>`); an **Events** system (`Events.json`) for narrative choices, which a building may fire on completion with `Triggers a [event] event`; and **stockpiled resources** — a resource carrying the `Stockpiled` unique accumulates like a treasury, a building may generate it in proportion to a building's yield (`[X]% of [stat] from every [buildingFilter] in the city added to [resource]` — which, read in `CityResources.kt`, *adds* a resource supply and does not remove the stat; it duplicates, it does not divert, and it truncates to whole units, so a percentage of a small or absent flat stat yields nothing), and buildings, units and improvements may spend it (`Costs [N] [stockpiledResource]`; Unciv's own documentation example is `Costs [3] [Mana]`). The `[stockpile]` parameter of `Instantly gain [amount] [stockpile]` accepts stat names as well as stockpiled resources, so a consumable unit (`<by consuming this unit>`, the Great Merchant's own mechanism) can turn a stockpile into Gold natively. A building may yield a stockpile directly with `Provides [amount] [resource]`, since each turn a civilisation gains its supply of every stockpiled resource (`TurnManager.kt`). Purchased units have no movement the turn they are bought unless they carry `Can move immediately once bought`, and unit actions require movement; stockpile costs are charged on purchase; there is no per-turn purchase limit; City-State gold gifts are offered only in 250, 500 and 1000. A tech may carry **Global** uniques (permanent civ-wide effects — Astronomy's `[+1] Movement <for [Embarked] units>`) and **Triggerable** uniques (fire once on discovery — Satellites' `Reveals the entire map`); nothing else.

**What does not exist, and what it converts to.** These concepts appear throughout earlier drafts and have no mechanism in Unciv; each row is being rewritten as follows.

| Concept with no mechanism | Convert to |
|---|---|
| Tourism, prestige, "soft power" | Culture (flat or `%`), Great Artist points, or City-State Influence |
| Loyalty, unrest, corruption, bureaucracy, tax efficiency | `%` Unhappiness, `%` building maintenance, Gold per population, `-% unhappiness from the number of cities` |
| Morale, terror, rout, pursuit, first strike, ambush | `[±X]% Strength` with conditionals (`<when attacking>`, `<vs [Mounted] units>`, `<in [Hill] tiles>`), `Can move after attacking`, `Withdraws before melee combat` |
| Health (as a stat), disease/plague events, lifespan | Happiness, `% growth`, `% Food carried over after population increases`, `% Great Person generation` |
| Disasters, earthquakes, floods, storms, weather, climate | Removed; where the intent was resilience, use `cityStrength`/`cityHealth`, terrain-conditional yields, or `% maintenance` |
| City size cap | `% growth` or `% Food carried over` (Civ V growth is happiness-limited, never capped) |
| Spoilage / food storage | `[X]% Food is carried over after population increases` (the Aqueduct pattern) |
| Pollution | Removed; a Production-vs-Happiness trade-off where one was intended |
| Trade route capacity or range | `[stats] from each Trade Route` or `[%] [stat] from Trade Routes` (city connections) |
| Loans, insurance, inflation, monopoly, propaganda | Gold `%`, `Happiness from each type of luxury`, Culture `%` |
| MAD, deterrence, nuclear winter, meltdown | Removed; the units stay, gated `<when nuclear weapons are enabled>` |
| Space probes with movement or range | Removed; deep-space capability is `% Production when constructing [Spaceship part] units`, Science, or the spaceship parts themselves. The three sub-light victories are alternative **spaceship-part sets** for the Scientific victory. |
| Fog of war on oceans, "never lost" | `[+X] Sight <for [Water] units>` |
| Blockade, airlift | `% Strength <vs cities>` for naval units; `May Paradrop` |
| Code-breaking, cipher, encryption, surveillance | `% spy effectiveness` inside `<when espionage is enabled>`, or removed |
| Advisors, UI reveals | UI only — no mechanical effect claimed |
| "Trade good" | `Provides [1] [Luxury]` from a building (legal, but each new luxury is a happiness source — used sparingly) |

**What the fork adds.** The Insight system is new code in any case, so a small, closed set of custom uniques rides with it and is used in this document by name: `Unlocks focus [X]`; `[+X]% Insight to [Focus]` (implemented as a Science modifier scoped to one focus); `Reveals [breakthrough]` (a Triggerable that sets a breakthrough to the Revealed state — the native `Free Technology` trigger is the precedent); obsolescence locking; the Knowledge Diffusion phase switches; World-Truth-conditional cost multipliers on breakthroughs (data of the Insight system, Section 1.2); UNIQUE-granted replacement buildings joining the holder's unique-building cache, and converting its existing base buildings, on discovery (below); a record of the Great Person names each civilisation receives (for the War of the Currents, EL14); the airship confidence ladder and its reports to contacted civilisations (§1.2, the Airship Disaster); and one extension of an existing mechanism — `Enables conversion of city production to [stockpiledResource]`, letting the Wealth-style perpetual process (`PerpetualStatConversion`) target a stockpile at a per-process rate, so that Dispersal and Banking Heat can bank a city's surplus into Prima Materia or Caloric (the Process world has no stockpile). Nothing else in the Unlocks column is invented — if it is not one of these, it is a real Unciv template.

**Power budget: build time is the invariant.** The rest of Unciv — AI, city-states, policy costs, wonders — is built against base Civ V's power level, so the fork aims at it, using the base game as a guide rather than a straitjacket. Per city, each yield from buildings should run within about 1.25× base Civ V per era; production may run a little higher (about 1.3×) provided what a city builds costs correspondingly more, because what players feel is how long things take. The measure is the time to build a city's full building set, which should stay close to base Civ V's; unit costs scale with the production ratio for the same reason. Surplus choice should come from exclusive and situational buildings, not a longer universal list. The per-city ledger that measures all of this is kept in `power_tools/`.

**Exclusion by replacement, not by blocking.** Where buildings share a slot — one grid plant, one temperament, bank or draw, or the two halves of a persisting Competing Solution — each carries `Remove [the others] [in this city]` rather than `Only available <in cities without a [the others]>`. Building one retires the rest when it completes, so a city is never locked into its first choice, and never holds two. An early version of a later building converts the same way — the Engine House into the Steam Works, the Tide Computer into the Seaport — sped up by the old building and retiring it. Shared slots are named by tag uniques; `replaces` is reserved for true equivalents — nation and UNIQUE buildings — because Unciv's capture swap follows `replaces`, and would turn a building that `replaces` a placeholder into the placeholder.

**Filter discipline.** `Building.matchesFilter` matches a building by its name, by the name it `replaces`, *and by the name of any technology it requires* — so a breakthrough must never share its name with a building that appears inside a `[buildingFilter]`, or every building that breakthrough unlocks will match. Caloric Storage (TH11) is named apart from its Caloric Reservoir for this reason; Windmill (MC04), Water Mill (WM05), the Thorium Reactor (NP08), the Re-Tempering Reactor (QM-E05) and the Mass Driver (RK15) share names with their buildings harmlessly, because each of those breakthroughs unlocks only its namesake; the verification battery checks every such share. The rule holds for every future filter. Since v3.60 every bracketed value is also validated against its parameter's vocabulary — the ruleset's own names, the keyword lists in `Unique-parameters.md`, and every object this document declares with ⁿ — so a filter can no longer name something that does not exist. (It cannot catch a valid name used with the wrong meaning: `Fresh water` and `Fresh Water` are both legal, and mean different things.)

**The familiar world is the baseline.** The atomic world is balanced to feel like base Civ V — roughly its power, and familiar to play despite the research system — and every other world is measured against it, never the reverse. An elemental world therefore *gains* its economy and *loses* what its physics forbids, and the two must weigh the same. **Gains:** Substance has Prima Materia (any form into any other, at a loss, from the Early Modern era); Process has the Five Turnings (Great Improvements built only by Great Sages, each on its own element's land, each fed by the one before it in the generative cycle). **Losses, from the models:** a continuum has no semiconductor and no photon to count (Model I §6–7), so the elemental world builds computers but never the silicon age — Miniaturisation at two and a half times the cost (so pocket electronics and Powered Armour arrive late, not never), no Photonics, a Fab that does not shrink, no Quantum Computing, no entanglement communication; and it has no fusion-fails fork, no thermonuclear tier, no fuel-cycle chain. The Information era, which base Civ V hands every player, is the elemental world's price for its Early Modern economy. **Brought down to meet it:** the Transmutation Hall is a national wonder (one per civilisation — matter comes from the Hall, the Hall city's Dispersal, the Stone and per-city Vats that lean mildly wide; there is no Great Person of matter), and the Process world has no stockpile at all: its gain is built one Great Sage at a time. **The quantitative audit (v3.45):** the ~17 atomic breakthroughs with no elemental mirror sum, for an eight-city late empire that builds all of them, to roughly +30 Science flat and +25% in two cities, +16 Production reliably (up to +56 with two RARE finds), +16 Happiness only via a RARE, +25% spaceship-part production and one missile tier — almost all Atomic-era or later. The Process gains, since v3.48, are the Forge's +4 Production and a handful of Turnings — one per Great Sage, perhaps five to eight in a game — each +3 on its tile and, when fed one-to-one by the element before it, one stacking policy-sized effect of its phase (growth, building production, border growth, military strength, Great Person generation); The Great Wheel lets each feeder feed two and adds +5% of every yield per complete wheel. One cycle is five Great People for fifteen tile yields and five such effects — GP-priced, and well below v3.44's +120 yields. The phase-effect sizes are the next levers if it is still ahead. The gateways to both elemental economies, Transmutation Practice (CH20) and Five-Phase Theory (CH22), exist only in their own worlds, so their difficulty is set directly in their prices — a multiplier would have nothing to multiply. If playtesting finds the elemental worlds ahead, the levers in order are: raise those prices, raise the re-locking prices, and last, move the gateways an era later — never a new power for the atomic world.

**Resource discipline.** A strategic resource is defined by what consumes it. The audit of every resource this design adds applied one test — at least three consumers spanning an era, and a game that is honestly poorer without it — and kept **three map-placed resources**: **Saltpeter** (the gunpowder strategic: Musketman, Cannon, Fireworks Ground; the Renaissance scarcity Civ V never had), **Rare Earths** (the high-tech strategic: Microchip Foundry, Fab, Drone, Solar Farm), and **Obsidian** (a Volcanic luxury that also gates the Obsidian Warrior and pays the volcanic cluster of UNIQUEs). It added one luxury that is made rather than mined: **Whisky**, distilled wherever a city works a Peat Cutting (CH04), which gives a bog a reason to be kept rather than drained. It kept two **stockpiles** — **Prima Materia**, the Substance economy itself, and **Caloric**, which is the caloric world's economy: heat is conserved and storable there, so heat is banked in Reservoirs and spent on Heat Exchanges, Lances, Wards and the Caloric Engine Plant (four sinks, and the branch's whole identity). It cut Sulfur and Bitumen (two more gates on eras Saltpeter and Iron already gate; both breakthroughs survive as knowledge), Rubber (nothing consumed it; the breakthrough folded into Vulcanization), and the Ritual Bronze luxury (the Foundry Temple provides Copper instead). Qi, once the Process stockpile, was cut in v3.46 — its only sinks were the Five Turnings and the Wandering Sage — and the Turnings became Great Sage improvements counted directly with `Owned [tileFilter] Tiles`, so the Process world needs no stockpile; at most a civilisation holds two (Prima Materia and Caloric, in a caloric Substance world). Any future resource must pass the same test.

**World-Truth ruleset derivation.** Before a game begins, the fork derives that game's ruleset from its sampled World Truths — the step that already has to remove absent breakthroughs and apply cost multipliers (§1.2). It does five things, all data transformations on the loaded ruleset: (1) removes breakthroughs that cannot exist in this world, with the objects only they unlock, and writes a Removal Truth's geology as data — `Doesn't generate naturally` on the resources it forbids; (2) applies world-conditional cost multipliers; (3) **remaps base-game objects to their mirrors** — an object has one `requiredTech` and a resource one `revealedBy`, so in elemental worlds the Manhattan Project and Atomic Bomb answer to the Quintessic Warhead (QM-E20) and Uranium's reveal to the Boundary Survey (QM-E16); (4) applies **world-dependent display names** — but only where the familiar name asserts a theory that is false in that world (*atomic*, *nuclear*, *semiconductor*), and then the name that world's own science would give: the Atomic Bomb is a Dissolution Bomb in a Substance world, where nothing is atomic. Everything named for what it observably is or does, or for historical reasons that hold in any world, keeps its name everywhere — Uranium is named for the planet Uranus; a computer, a vacuum tube, a solar panel and radiation are named for what they are and do. Elemental science is as rigorous as atomic science and names what it finds by the same habits; nothing is renamed for flavour; (5) applies world-conditional tier overrides — as when a Competing Solution's primary cannot exist in a world and the alternative takes its tier (§1.2). At runtime, one further tier change is per civilisation rather than per world: the airship confidence ladder (§1.2). New objects need none of this: each simply names its own breakthrough.

**Terrain the tree assumes but Civ V lacks.** "Volcanic region" becomes a new terrain feature **Volcanic** (placed near volcanic Natural Wonders and on some Hills at map generation); "flood-prone" is the existing **Flood plains** feature; "earthquake-prone" has no equivalent and those effects are re-based on Hills and Mountains.

**Layering on nation uniques.** Players still choose an Unciv nation, and every nation arrives with its own ability and one or two unique units or buildings (Mongolia's Keshik and mounted movement, Denmark's Berserker and embarked movement, Inca hill roads and Terrace farms, Polynesia's ocean-going embarkation…). UNIQUE breakthroughs are therefore a *second* layer of identity on top of a nation's, never the first — and three rules follow. (1) **A breakthrough never grants a nation's own unique object.** Where a UNIQUE breakthrough's theme matches an existing UU or UB, it grants a *differently named, differently statted* object (Mangudai rather than Keshik, Huscarl rather than Berserker, Chinampa Garden rather than Floating Gardens) so the nation keeps what is its own. (2) **Nation uniques keep their base object's gate**: Krepost unlocks when Barracks does, for Russia only, exactly as §7's "civ-uniques" rows say. (3) **Stacking is allowed and expected.** A Polynesian player who also discovers Polynesian Wayfinding stacks two naval-movement bonuses; a Mongol who finds Mongol Combined Arms stacks Great General bonuses on mounted speed. Nation abilities make some UNIQUEs *more* attractive to some players, which is intended, and the cumulative UNIQUE penalty bounds the total. Where playtesting shows a stack is too strong, the fix is to that UNIQUE's numbers, not to the layering.

**UNIQUE replacements must actually replace.** A nation's unique building blocks its base building because `Building.kt` rejects any building that a building in the civilisation's `uniqueBuildings` cache `replaces` — and that cache is built from the nation's `uniqueTo` buildings. A breakthrough-granted replacement (the Cowrie Exchange for the Market, the River Temple for the Temple) has no `uniqueTo`, so as the data stands the base building would stay buildable beside it and the two would stack. The fork therefore adds every UNIQUE-granted replacement to the holder's `uniqueBuildings` cache on discovery, **and converts the holder's existing base buildings into it** — free and in place, exactly as Unciv's own transfer does when a city changes hands (`CityConquestFunctions`: each built building becomes `getEquivalentBuilding` for the new owner). Only the new replacement's base building is converted, so nothing else is disturbed. A UNIQUE therefore takes effect in the cities you already have, and a city never holds both. This is the eighth fork extension: a line in a cache we already touch, and a call to code Unciv already has. One case is left for the redesign phase: a civilisation whose nation already replaces the same base building (Egypt's Burial Tomb and a River Temple both replace the Temple).

**Calibration rule.** The canonical tree grants roughly one to four objects per tech and almost never a civ-wide percentage; percentages come from buildings. With ~528 breakthroughs replacing 80 techs, one breakthrough's budget is about a sixth of a tech's — except that **CORE gateways** (those that unlock a focus) may carry a full tech's worth, **STANDARD** breakthroughs carry one object or one modest unique, **UNCOMMON** one distinctive object or unique, **RARE** one strong unique or a wonder, and **UNIQUE** breakthroughs mirror a Civ V civilisation: one unique building or unit plus one civ-wide unique tied to their terrain. Costs and stats of new objects are pegged to the tech column of their era (`Techs.json`: Ancient building 40–75, Classical 100, Medieval 120–160, Renaissance 200–250, Industrial 300–360, Modern+ 500, Information 750).

**Notation in the Unlocks column.** **Name** — a base-game object; **Name**ⁿ — a new object, with its JSON-ready spec in brackets [cost/maintenance; stats; uniques]; ⟨…⟩ — a Global or Triggerable unique the breakthrough itself carries, in Unciv unique syntax; *Reveals* **X** — a resource's `revealedBy`; `requires a **X** in this city` inside a spec — the building's `requiredBuilding`: X, or a building that replaces X, must stand in the city (Unciv's `containsBuildingOrEquivalent`). X always names a real building, never a slot tag, because the rejection message looks the name up. A fork building that takes a canonical building's place carries that building's requirement: a replacement copies its base's, as the Wat copies the University's; an early version copies its successor's; an alternative that removes a building copies the removed building's; a paradigm's stand-in copies the one it stands in for; and every member of the Power Plant slot requires a Factory, as the canonical Nuclear and Solar Plants do. *Unlocks* **Focus** — our own system. Insight effects are our own stat and are written as `+X% Insight to Focus`.

**Notation in the Prerequisites column.** Row IDs joined by "+" are all required; "or" offers alternatives. A cell never relies on which of the two binds tighter: whenever it uses both, each group of alternatives is in parentheses — "WA10 + (ME05 or ME08)" — and so is each alternative that needs several rows — "G02 + (G07 or (FA01 + FA02))", "CH01 or (F03 + ME04)". Inside a group, commas also separate the alternatives ("(NP03 Fission, or QM-E02 Bound-Energy Release)"). A parenthesis whose content begins with a row ID is a group; any other parenthesis is a description — "(Kiln Construction)", "(but NOT SO04)" — and so is commentary after a dash outside every parenthesis. Plain text with no ID, such as "Coastal location" or "Temperate or Tropical biome", is an environmental condition. The battery, the generator and the derivation all read prerequisites through one parser (check 18), and check 19 holds every cell to this notation.

**Status.** Complete (v3.21–v3.57): every breakthrough's Unlocks column is grounded in Unciv objects, real unique templates and the fork's enumerated extensions. Reward values are preliminary, pending playtesting.

**Requirement overrides.** The ruleset derivation changes these canonical `requiredBuilding` values. Every requirement must name a safe predecessor: one whose row is certain to come first, or a CORE row of the same or an earlier era that is reachable wherever the dependent is (battery check 13). Vanilla chains its culture and happiness buildings rung by rung, for pacing rather than realism — a museum does not need an opera house. In this tree the middle rungs sit on STANDARD rows, so a rung-by-rung chain could leave a top building unbuildable. Instead a top rung requires its line's *first* rung: cheap, always reachable, and still no way to buy only the best building. The Broadcast Tower needs nothing, because its +33% Culture multiplies whatever the city already has.

| Building | Vanilla requires | Fork requires |
|---|---|---|
| Opera House | Amphitheater | Monument |
| Ceilidh Hall | Amphitheater | Monument |
| Museum | Opera House | Monument |
| Stadium | Theatre | Colosseum |
| Broadcast Tower | Museum | — |

**Cost scaling.** Build time is the invariant: the fork's extra Production buys breadth, a longer building list, not a faster game. Counting buildings alone, a fork city has about 1.3× base Civ V's Production from the Medieval era; counting its worked tiles too, which both games share, it has only about 1.05–1.2×, and it is that whole-city ratio that sets how long anything takes. Fork building specs are priced on vanilla's scale by value: the era's typical canonical building cost (Primitive 50, Ancient 75, Classical 100, Medieval 140, Early Modern 250, Modern and Atomic 500, Space 750) × (value points ÷ 4), clamped between a quarter and one and a half times that cost and rounded to 5. Value points: 1 per flat yield, 1 per 10% bonus, 1 per specialist slot, 1 per 5 city Strength or 50 city HP, a tile or per-population unique at its typical-city yield, and 1 for any other unique. Upkeep follows vanilla's rate for the era, per 100 cost: Primitive 2.5, Ancient 1.33, Classical 1.17, Medieval 0.85, Early Modern 0.8, Modern to Space 0.55. A UNIQUE replacement costs what its base costs, as vanilla's unique buildings do. The v3.77 spaceship stack — the launch complex, the Orbital Shipyard and the exotic-component buildings — keeps its tuned costs. The ruleset derivation then multiplies every cost by its era's row below, canonical and fork alike, the era being that of the row that grants the object. Every building shares one multiplier, so none is a bargain per point. The building column is solved era by era so that, by the end of every fork era, the fork's buildings have taken about 1.1× the share of a city's Production that base Civ V's had taken by the same turn (the extra tenth for breadth). The comparison is by turn, not by era: since v3.108 the fork's eras are its own (§2, Era boundaries) and no longer line up with base Civ V's, and an era-against-era comparison would have let fork cities spend 20–55% more of their Production on buildings than base cities at the same point in the game. A city's Production here counts its worked tiles (1.5 per citizen, plus 2 from the city centre — an assumption) as well as its buildings, with percentage bonuses applied to the whole, over the era's length from the era model below. The Ancient and Classical eras share one value, because the Ancient surplus is canonical Classical buildings on historically Ancient rows, a timing shift gone by the end of the Classical era. The unit column is the era's whole-city Production ratio, rounded to 0.05, so units, wonders and spaceship parts take base Civ V's time. Unciv derives purchase prices from production cost, so Gold prices follow both columns. The Space era carried about five times base Civ V's building load at value price, since base adds almost nothing in its last era. Consolidation (v3.92) brought that to about 3.4 times — the Planetary Defense Network became a national wonder, the Orbital Depot an upgrade of the Asteroid Mining Station, and the Quantum Network a bonus of its own breakthrough — and the Space row is solved on what remains. Most of the rest is the Fusion Plant upgrade; base Civ V has no Space-era plant.

**What a city's set counts.** The Power Plant slot holds one plant at a time, so each era adds its best new plant once, as the city's upgrade. Infrastructure for one city — capital-only buildings and the spaceship stack — counts at one city's share, and a building limited per civilisation at its limit's share of the cities.

**Era lengths.** The era model divides each era's Insight, with the per-city factor, by an empire's Insight per turn: 1 Science per citizen, building Science and the Palace's +3, with percentage bonuses on the whole, for the typical city's population and an assumed 1, 3, 5, 6, 7 and then 8 cities. What the typical city does not model — specialists, trade routes, city-states, policies, wonders, Great Scientists — enters as a factor that ramps from nothing in the Primitive era to about ×2.5 by the Space era, calibrated so that base Civ V's science victory falls around turn 320 (a good player wins by 300; after about 375, someone else usually has). The calibrated base game enters the Ancient era around turn 32, the Classical around 53, the Medieval around 79, the Early Modern around 115, the Modern around 176, the Atomic around 228 and the Space era around 264. The fork's eras have their own boundaries (§2, Era boundaries), drawn so that discoveries come at an even pace up to the Early Modern era (with the opening smoothed in v3.123) and, from the Modern era, at base Civ V's own era timing (v3.122): they last about 33, 36, 28, 36, 49, 46, 36 and 56 turns for the same player. The science victory itself is held to base Civ V's on the same economy, simulated directly (§2, Era boundaries). With the residual factor recalibrated to base Civ V's strong and casual players (about 260 and 400 turns), the fork takes about 262 and 396: its extra Science scales with income, so one Insight curve matches base Civ V at every skill level. The absolute turns rest on the calibration; the comparisons between fork and base, which the table uses, are sturdier, since the same omissions apply to both.

| Era | Buildings | Units, wonders and spaceship parts |
|---|---|---|
| Primitive | ×0.72 | ×1.00 |
| Ancient | ×0.72 | ×1.05 |
| Classical | ×0.72 | ×1.00 |
| Medieval | ×0.86 | ×1.10 |
| Early Modern | ×0.64 | ×1.10 |
| Modern | ×0.52 | ×1.20 |
| Atomic | ×0.48 | ×1.15 |
| Space | ×0.45 | ×1.20 |
| Interstellar | ×0.45 | ×1.20 |

Interstellar has no base Civ V era to solve against, so it takes the Space values. The values were solved for v3.108's era timing; v3.122 moved the Modern, Atomic and Space eras (on a good economy, the Atomic era now from about turn 228 and the Space era from about 264 to 320) and v3.123 the earlier ones by a few turns (the Ancient era now ends around turn 69 rather than 62), so they are to be re-solved.

### Units

Civ-unique units are omitted: in Civ V a unique unit unlocks at the **same tech as the generic unit it replaces**, so it inherits that unit's breakthrough (e.g. Legion → Swordsman → ME04, Keshik → Knight → WA07). Only the generic ladder is mapped here. Our tree abstracts military progression more coarsely than Civ V's unit ladder, so several late units share a breakthrough or map to the nearest thematic one.

### Ungated objects (no breakthrough required)

These are produced by Great People, Great Generals, or engine mechanics rather than unlocked by tech, so they carry no breakthrough gate:

- **Great Person / General improvements:** Academy, Landmark, Manufactory, Customs house, Holy site, Citadel.
- **Worship buildings (faith-purchased via Beliefs):** Cathedral, Monastery, Mosque, Pagoda — gated by the Cathedrals / Monasteries / Mosques / Pagodas follower beliefs. (This is why our former religious-institution breakthroughs — Organised Religion, Ascetic Tradition, Ancestor Veneration — were removed: the Religion system already provides them.)
- **Great People (units):** Great Artist, Scientist, Merchant, Engineer, Prophet, General, Admiral — generated by Great Person points / faith / policies, not tech.
- **Faith-purchased religious units:** Missionary, Inquisitor — gated by the Religion system, not a breakthrough.
- **Barbarian units:** Brute, Galley — not part of any player's research.
- **Spaceship parts (SS Booster / Cockpit / Engine / Stasis Chamber):** these belong to Civ V's *Apollo/spaceship* science victory, which this design replaces with the FTL / sub-light system (Section 4). Mapped above for completeness in case a build-a-spaceship victory is retained; otherwise superseded.
- **Engine / worker actions (no gate):** Remove Fallout, Remove Road, Remove Railroad, Cancel improvement order, Repair, Ancient ruins, City ruins, City center, Barbarian encampment.

**Naming-collision note:** the base-game faith buildings **Cathedral** and **Pagoda** share names with content our tree used to grant, so CO06's building was renamed **Basilica** and CO20's building **Seismic Pagoda** to avoid clashing with the belief-purchased Cathedral/Pagoda.

## 8. SUMMARY STATISTICS

| Metric | Count |
|---|---|
| Focus Areas | 35 (5 starting + 31 unlockable) |
| Total Breakthroughs | ~544 |
| CORE Breakthroughs | ~142 |
| STANDARD Breakthroughs | ~253 |
| UNCOMMON Breakthroughs | ~56 |
| RARE Breakthroughs | ~39 |
| UNIQUE Breakthroughs | ~54 |
| World Truth Axes | 11 (4 Foundational + 7 Frontier) + 3 Geological + 3 Contingency (~15,000+ physics signatures) |
| Competing Solutions groups | 16 (10 persist, 6 are superseded) |
| Dual-Path Breakthroughs | ~22 (discoverable through multiple Focus Areas) |
| RARE Gateway Chains | 4 (Clockwork, Antikythera, Bio-Computing, Early Steam — follow-ups are STANDARD) |
| FTL Methods | 6 (D atomic-only, F elemental-only; 5 candidates per world, exactly 1 viable) |
| Eras | 9 |
| Base-game objects mapped | 124 buildings/wonders + 20 tile improvements + 64 generic units → breakthroughs (Section 7) |

**Tier design philosophy:**
- **CORE** (~146): The skeleton — guaranteed, reliable, sufficient to reach victory alone.
- **STANDARD** (~233): The flesh — what most civilisations will discover, including RARE chain follow-ups and the many theory-variant breakthroughs of the World Truths system.
- **UNCOMMON** (~60): The branching paths — where civilisations diverge. Obsolescence creates natural variation without removing anything from the global pool.
- **RARE** (~39): The surprises — exciting, powerful, never required. Gateway breakthroughs to speculative chains.
- **UNIQUE** (~54): The identity — cultural and environmental specialisations that define your civilisation's playstyle. First come, first served. Each game, 2–5 per civilisation, ~20–35 claimed, ~20–35 unclaimed.

*Version 3.13 — Mapped every base-game unit to a breakthrough (64 generic; civ-uniques inherit their base unit's gate) and fixed the fragile "Founded a Religion" prerequisite on CO21/CH15/CU10 by repointing them to CU02 (Monumental Architecture), since Civ V seeds fewer religions than players. ~507 breakthroughs, 36 focus areas.*

*Version 3.14 — Early-game pacing pass. Replaced the building/terrain passive-Insight scheme with an explicit **80/20 active/spread routing** model (spread weighted by environmental modifiers), added the **soft Specialisation-of-Labour gate** (50/50 before SO05, 80/20 after) so the opening is always steerable, front-loaded CORE difficulty, and noted a focus-queue UX affordance. Prose-only: all 507 breakthrough rows unchanged.*

*Version 3.15 — Added the **Insight Scale & Calibration** note to §1.2: Insight maps 1:1 to base-game science (inheriting Unciv's science curve as the threshold anchor), per-focus Insight stored as float to stop the 20% spread underflowing, and Civ V's per-city cost modifier retained by applying it to the breakthrough **threshold** (preserving tall-vs-wide balance). Prose-only; all 507 breakthrough rows unchanged.*

*Version 3.16 — Quintessic Physics fleshed out and given divergent elemental identities. Added four nodes (Grand Athanor/Great Observatory, Incorruptible Matter, Elixir of Life, Great Harmony), taking the focus 7→11 (~511 total). Substance now rewards depth/self-sufficiency (transmutation, perfected matter, the Elixir); process rewards breadth/balance (net-gain generative cycle, Five-Phase Balance). Fixed CH23, which assumed Civ VI districts (removed the tile-adjacency; kept non-spatial conversion-at-a-gain). Deferred to later passes: the FTL elemental-guidance fix (incl. Method F "Plenum Drive", elemental-only, mirror of the atomic-only Quantum-Tunneling method) and the "Phase Ascendancy" tempo mechanic.*

*Version 3.17 — FTL pass for elemental worlds, built ontology-first. Reframed the six methods as claims about reality reaching shared **capability primitives** (malleable space, exotic matter, medium energy, higher realms, superluminal matter) by atomic or elemental accounts, so each universal method (A/B/C/E) keeps the same research lane in both kinds of world. Formalised Method D (Quantum Tunneling) as atomic-only and added Method F (Plenum Drive) as its elemental-only mirror — the matter axis grants each world one exclusive method. Added QM-E12 (Supernal Spheres) and QM-E13 (Aetheric Vacuum) as the higher-realms and medium-energy primitives (~513 total); made Relativity & Cosmology dual-framed via Lorentzian aether-mechanics; wired elemental guidance reports; fixed the EXOTIC_MATTER token overclaim and extended HIGH_ENERGY_PHYSICS to QP08; updated Guarantee Rule 2. (Laser/OP07-in-elemental-worlds parked separately.)*

*Version 3.18 — Laser resolution. OP07 reframed as theory-agnostic coherent light: atomic worlds by stimulated emission (QP02), elemental worlds by resonant aetheric/phase emission (QM-E03). Unblocks laser-dependent tech (incl. the RC03 gravitational-wave route) in elemental worlds. No change to breakthrough counts. Closes the last parked FTL-adjacent dependency; "Phase Ascendancy" remains the sole open design item.*

*Version 3.19 — Cleanup pass. Lockout window defined as UNCOMMON obsolescence (CORE/STANDARD never lock out); FTL sampling order corrected to Matter-then-FTL with D/F candidate gating; Rule 3, never-tell list and Periodic Table weight corrected; ten prerequisite labels fixed (incl. WA02→WA06 in F15/WA24); Water Wheel merged into Water Mill (~512). Elemental-world prerequisite unblocks deferred to the physics-derived pass.*

*Version 3.20 — Elemental worlds derived from the two physics models. Sixty-three blocked breakthroughs rewired (Computer, Integrated Circuit, Substrate Mastery, Solar, and nine more read an elemental face); alternate elemental names on shared late craft; sixteen new breakthroughs — ten shared elemental (Limiting Fineness, Aetheric Electrodynamics, Boundary Survey, Complete Catalogue, Aether Telegraphy, Celestial Current Survey, Quintessic Warhead, Compact Athanor, Medium Reservoir, Current Sail), two Substance (Formless Reserve, Dissonance Weaponry), four Process (Closed-Loop Calorimetry, Self-Renewing Structures, Counter-Cycle Reversal, Phase Exchange). The sparse Communications focus is gone; its nine original breakthroughs rehomed to Trade, Optics, Electricity, Electronics, Rocketry, Quantum Physics, Space Engineering, Religion & Culture, and Warfare (IDs and prerequisite chains preserved), and the five v3.6 filler techs removed. ~520 breakthroughs, 37 focus areas.*

*Version 3.7 — Disease forks, cross-listing index & changelog relocation. Antibiotics made Germ-exclusive; humoral medicine line filled out (MD18 Humoral Pharmacology, MD19 Temperament Engineering) plus a vitalist medicine layer (MD-V01); MASS_MEDICINE token keeps late medicine world-agnostic. Vitalist AI route (GN14) dual-listed with Computing. Every focus now lists the dual-path breakthroughs reachable through it. Changelog moved to the end. ~525 breakthroughs, 38 focus areas.*

*Version 3.6 — Non-Atomic Coherence Pass. The alternative-physics forks made to hang together across the whole tree: Aeronautics reworked for phlogiston airship worlds, Rocketry given elemental equivalents, Quintessic Physics dual-framed for both Greek and Chinese elemental traditions, vitalism reconciled with petrochemistry and made to block silicon AGI, Energy Systems given elemental/caloric/phlogiston plants, Materials Science and Electricity checked for non-atomic compatibility, Communications thickened into a steady chain. ~522 breakthroughs, 38 focus areas.*

*Version 3.5 — The Symmetry Principle. No privileged reality: every Truth value, ours included, forks into an exclusive cluster and forecloses its rivals'. Foundational axes fork into niche-equivalent pillars (added Quintessic Physics, the elemental mirror of Nuclear Physics); only frontier axes truly dead-end. Far-flung cross-axis implications formalised (phlogiston reaches exotic-matter FTL an era early). Deep-physics tokens keep the late tree foundation-agnostic. Redundancies removed (Ekranoplan, Factory, Zero-Point Generator, Laboratory). ~513 breakthroughs, 38 focus areas.*

*Version 3.4 — Structural merge & coherence pass. World variation unified under a single Section 5 (World Truths); the six original narrative worlds are now worked examples of the Geological and Historical families. "World Flag" retired as a system name in favour of "World Truth." No breakthrough content altered.*

*Version 3.3 — Discovery States, Grand Endeavours & World Truths. ~511 breakthroughs across 37 Focus Areas. Unknown → Revealed → Discovered per civilisation, with the One-Step Horizon preserving genuine unknowability forever. Grand Endeavours model directed research — serendipity for the surprises, engineering for the visible mountains. Variable laws of physics on three layers: fixed phenomena, sampled mechanisms (phlogiston, Greek and Chinese elemental truth, miasma, humours, vitalism, caloric — the great rival theories, sometimes true), and sampled frontiers (fusion, FTL, gravitics). Twelve Truth Axes yield ~30,000 physics signatures from ~75 authored outcomes, held together by Understanding Tokens and Deep Reconciliation. Every universe must be discovered from the inside — from the first weighed calx to the final FTL launch.*

---

## Changelog from v2.0 — Game Design Pass

### v3.123 — Parity with Base Civ V's Science Victory; a Smoother Opening

- **Parity with base Civ V's science victory, the design's hardest constraint** (Tamsin, 15 October): a science victory must take as long as base Civ V's for the same player on the same economy, within five turns either way, because domination, cultural and diplomatic victories are unchanged and balanced against base Civ V's game length. The reference is base Civ V's own tree run to a launched spaceship on the simulator's income (Unciv's Gods & Kings techs, researching only what the ship needs, the Apollo Program and the six parts built from a typical city's Production): turn 305 on a good economy and 380 on an average one. The era model's 320 is base Civ V's whole-tree research time, not its victory. §2 now states the constraint.
- **The late prices back to their v3.108 values** (§2): 951 per discovery in the Modern era, 1,369 in the Atomic and 1,399 in the Space era, against v3.122's ×1.25, ×1.25 and ×2. With the SS FTL Drive built from Production rather than pencilled in at 4 turns, v3.122 measures 327 / 332 / 412 (expert / novice / average), not the 323 / 328 / 407 its changelog gave: 22–32 turns longer than base Civ V's. v3.122's era timing stays. The prototype now takes about 4 turns on a good economy and 5 on an average one (§4), and the Drive about 10 and 12.
- **A smoother opening** (§2; Tamsin asked for the discovery rate smoothed, counting knowledge from rivals). The Ancient era's many small breakthroughs had come at up to 1.25 a turn (the novice's). Its price is now 1.5× its even-pace price (22 per discovery, from 14), with its span to match (858 Insight, from 572), and the Primitive span is 1.3× longer at its old price (194, from 149), so that development still enters the Ancient era within 6–9 turns of civilisations' entry by count, rather than 10–14. Every era up to the Early Modern now runs at one turn per discovery or slower for all three benchmark players. The cost: civilisations get through their Ancient breakthroughs later, entering the Ancient and Classical eras by count about 4–7 turns later than before (the Medieval 3–4, the Early Modern 1–2; screened on three victory conditions). Development's eras now last about 33, 36, 28, 36, 49, 46, 36 and 56 turns on a good economy, and idea windows follow (§1.2).
- **Measured** (three worlds, all 15 victory conditions, three benchmark players, 2,700 games with rivals at the same prices, none unfinished): victory medians **304** (expert; middle half 298–313), **307** (novice) and **380** (average), against base Civ V's 305, 305 and 380.
