Civilisations enter every era by count within 9 turns of their development reaching it. Turns per discovery while development is in each era, counting rivals' knowledge (expert / novice / average): Primitive 1.1 / 1.1 / 1.4, Ancient 1.1 / 1.0 / 1.2, Classical 1.2 / 1.3 / 1.6, Medieval 1.1 / 1.1 / 1.3, Early Modern 1.2 / 1.2 / 1.5, Modern 0.9 / 0.8 / 1.0, Atomic 0.8 / 0.7 / 0.9, Space 0.7 / 0.8 / 1.1.
- **The late pace stays as it is, for now.** A game holds 280–308 discoveries by the race's finish whatever the prices or diffusion (measured 15 October), so with the game's length fixed, prices can only move the fast stretches around. The Modern to Space breakthroughs take 85% of the Insight spent on discoveries, so they cannot get dearer without lengthening the science victory, and slowing knowledge diffusion only turns free breakthroughs into the player's own. Fewer late discoveries need fewer late breakthroughs: merges, cuts and demotions to UNCOMMON are under review.
- **Still to do.** §7's building cost scaling, solved for v3.108's era timing, is to be re-solved for v3.122's late eras and v3.123's early ones. The FTL methods' balance (v3.122). The simulator's income follows the Insight accumulated, not the breakthroughs held, so it cannot price the later Ancient-era buildings: to be confirmed in play.

### v3.122 — Base Civ V's Game Lengths: The Late Eras Redrawn and Dearer

- **Back to base Civ V's game lengths** (Tamsin, 15 October): a science victory should take as long as in base Civ V at every skill level, about 320 turns for a good player and 400 for a casual one, because cultural and diplomatic victories are balanced against those lengths. The targets used since 13 October (about 295 for a good economy, 360–375 for an average one) came from v3.91's description of the era model ("the fork's eras run about a tenth shorter"), which v3.108's redraw had superseded; v3.112's 20% cut in Space-era prices and its 5× prototype were made to reach them.
- **The last three era boundaries at base Civ V's timing** (§2). Development now leaves the Modern era at 66,231 Insight, enters the Space era at 113,629 and leaves it at 209,676: on a good economy around turns 228, 264 and 320, base Civ V's own. Before, development entered the Space era around turn 285, 24–29 turns after civilisations entered it by count, so 81–88% of the Space-era breakthroughs a player held at the finish came before its development reached the Space era, and the ahead-of-time premium took 36–47% of the Insight spent studying them. Now civilisations enter every era by count within about ten turns of their development reaching it. Idea windows follow the new era lengths (§1.2).
- **Dearer late research** (§2). Prices per discovery ×1.25 in the Modern and Atomic eras (951 → 1,189; 1,369 → 1,712) and ×2 in the Space era (1,399 → 2,798, replacing v3.112's ×0.8). A Space-era discovery had cost half a turn of a good economy's Insight, since §2 divided the era's span by all its expected content, most of it rows no single game reaches; it now costs about 1.25 turns. A civilisation's own discoveries come at about 0.8 a turn or fewer in every late era, and about one counting knowledge from rivals, where they had run at up to 1.4 (2 in the expert's Space era). The prototype, at 5× the Space era's price, takes about 8 turns on a good economy and 10 on an average one (§4).
- **Measured** (three worlds, three benchmark players, 2,700 games with rivals at the same prices, none unfinished): victory medians 323 (expert; middle half 313–333), 328 (novice) and 407 (average). Rivals had been replayed from records made at v3.120's prices, which understated any change in price; they are now made under each setting's own. The simulated expert was retuned for the new prices: it never targets a row whose ahead-of-time premium exceeds 2, and commissions an Endeavour only for the last thing its race waits on (900 games: median 326 → 323).
- **Still to do.** The methods have drifted apart: Warp and Tachyonic win 62–78% of races against their world's field, Wormhole, Tunnelling and Plenum 22–44%, against a band of 40–60%; part of it predates v3.122. §7's building cost scaling, solved for v3.108's era timing, is to be re-solved. The simulator's income follows the Insight accumulated rather than the breakthroughs held, so dearer research does not delay its buildings and real games may run somewhat longer: to be confirmed in play.
- **Corrected:** v3.121's figures for counting the study already done after the premium were 15% and 22.5%; with every Endeavour taking at least a turn, they are 14% and 22%.

### v3.121 — Endeavours on Ideas Under Study

- **A Grand Endeavour may again target an idea already under study** (Tamsin, 15 October 2026), to have it now rather than when its focus gets round to it: bomb shelters, the turn word comes that a rival has the bomb. v3.120 had allowed only Revealed ideas that had not occurred, because reordering research at 2× cost the benchmark players 4–9 turns of victory (measured 14 October, before the safety net). Re-measured on v3.120 (familiar world, 900 games a setting), allowing it costs the novice and the average player 5–6 turns and the expert nothing measurable. That stays true when nothing presses, so the simulated players keep the restriction (`ENDUNOCC=1`): the needs that make such an Endeavour worth its price come from the game situation, which the simulator does not model. §1.5's rule is now *Under study or not*; §1.4's REVEALED state, §4's *Bad luck never blocks a science victory* and Guarantee Rule 12 say the same. Open: whether the Insight already spent studying the idea counts towards the Endeavour.
- **Measured** (the expert, familiar world, 300 games; `endeavour_oracle.py`): at moments sampled every ten turns, an Endeavour would have had an idea under study sooner than funding its focus for 12% of such ideas: 2% of those alone in their focus, 8% with one other, 26% with two or three others, 52% with four or more. Counting the Insight already spent would raise the 12% to 14% (in the funded focus, 13.5% to 22%).
- **§1.2's study rule corrected.** A breakthrough's progress, which with 0.1 sets its share of its focus, is the Insight it has received measured in its price, not the share of its hidden meter filled. This is what the simulator has always done (the workshop report: "progress as a fraction of the average cost").
- No row changed.

### v3.120 — The Discovery Model Written In; the Safety Net; W16 Dropped

- **§1.2 now describes the discovery model in use.** Every measurement since 1 October 2026 has run on it, while §1.2 still described the first design's Insight thresholds, hidden discovery rolls and excess Insight feeding the roll. It now sets out what the simulator does:
  - the active focus, and a spread that goes only to foci with something under study, weighted by the work waiting there and by §1.3's environment, with spill-over;
  - development and price: a breakthrough costs its era's price per discovery × 0.83, × 4 for each era it lies ahead of development;
  - ideas: a breakthrough is studied only once its idea occurs, at once for CORE, otherwise by chance through a window sized to the tier's odds (STANDARD 85% within half an era; UNCOMMON 40%, RARE 10%, UNIQUE 6.5% within a whole era), the window shrinking behind history;
  - study: hidden meters filled with momentum, every tier alike once its idea has occurred;
  - the tier table restated as each tier's odds and moment; accelerators as head starts (decided 14 October); knowledge diffusion as modelled, with the meaning of the old roll bonuses marked as proposed.
- **The safety net (adopted).** Rediscovery on demand: a lost STANDARD idea comes back when something waits on it, 10% a turn for each waiting thing. Grand Endeavours only for revealed ideas that have not occurred: at 2×, reordering work already under study cost the benchmark players 4–9 turns. §1.5 and Guarantee Rule 12 say so.
- **W16 dropped.** §4's rule that a Space-era row's window stays open while its focus is funded gives way to rediscovery: with rediscovery, W16 only bought speed (about 8 turns for the expert), and its written form did nothing measurable for the simulated players.
- **Measured** (three worlds, three benchmark players, 2,700 games; these are now the simulator's defaults): no game unfinished, against about 1% under the previous rules; victory medians 297–298 (expert), 301–302 (novice) and 374–375 (average), within the balance targets of about 295 for a good economy and 360–375 for an average one. The weaker players gain about ten turns because Endeavours are no longer spent reordering work.
- **Knock-on wording.** §1.3's necessity bonus is Insight alone. §1.4 and §1.5 lose their rolls and thresholds: proximity revelation is the glimpse (10% a turn for STANDARD ideas, 3% for UNCOMMON, provisional), serendipity is the spread, and an Endeavour costs 2× the breakthrough's price. Likewise §4's prototype paragraph, the Early Steam, Clockwork and Antikythera worlds, the elemental gateways' difficulty (prices, not thresholds), and §6's diffusion summary, *Almost Discovered* notice and dual paths. Mycological Knowledge's accelerators are head starts. §1.4's reveal rules themselves await the reveal decision.
- ~544 breakthroughs.

### v3.119 — Gearing Is Classical

- **Gearing (MC03) moves from the Ancient to the Classical era.** Toothed gears are Hellenistic and Han: Philo's geared water wheel (3rd century BC), the Antikythera mechanism (2nd century BC), Vitruvius's water mill and hodometer, and Han China's bronze gears. The row's own text already called its machines Hellenistic and Han-era; only the lever and pulley merged into it are older. It remains available from the Ancient era (the Wheel and Geometry), one era early at the era premium.
- **Nothing breaks:** no Ancient row requires Gearing, and the battery's results are unchanged. Eight rows that could be had from the Ancient era now wait for the Classical: the Water Mill, Hero's Aeolipile and the Antikythera-Style Mechanism (now available in their own era), the Windmill, the Printing Press and the Crossbow (one era early instead of two), and the Cotton Gin and the Ornithopter (two instead of three). Rows available two or more eras early fall from 20 to 17.
- **Measured** (familiar world, three benchmark players, 900 games, against v3.118 with the same seeds): victory medians unchanged; 8 unfinished games against 7, all STANDARD build-list ideas that never occurred.
- **Not simulated:** Gearing's bonuses (faster Mines and Farms, +10% Production on wonders) arrive later in a typical game; Gearing now counts toward entering the Classical era; and Energy Systems' early route, Canal Building with Gearing, opens later.
- ~544 breakthroughs.

### v3.118 — Glass Without a Shore

- **Glassmaking no longer needs a desert or coast tile.** It requires Kiln Construction and either Charcoal Production or a desert or coast tile: the two traditions of glass. Soda glass took its flux from natron and the ash of salt-loving plants of desert and shore (Egypt, Rome, Venice). Potash glass took it from wood ash: the forest glass of inland Europe (Theophilus, c. 1120: two parts beechwood ash to one of sand washed from a river), later Bohemian crystal. The silica was never the difficulty: river sand, crushed quartz pebbles and sandstone are found almost everywhere, and inland China made glass with lead and barium.
- **Why:** a terrain condition cannot be met by trade, and Glassmaking opens the Optics focus and stands on every route to 152 rows (27 CORE): the Telescope, the Vacuum Tube, the Programmable Computer, nuclear fission, the quantum chain, Quantum Gravity Theory and Orbital Assembly. A civilisation with neither a desert nor a coast could not win in an atomic world, and in an elemental one only by Warp or Wormhole.
- **In practice the gate is gone,** since Charcoal Production is a Primitive CORE row with no terrain condition; the shore keeps its story, not an advantage. Inland, the Lighthouse bonus does nothing, but opening the Optics focus is the row's benefit (v3.84). The tools read the row as Kiln Construction plus Charcoal Production, as they read every environmental alternative.
- **Guarantee Rule 1** now says what v3.117 and this change make true: no terrain condition stands on the CORE path to Interstellar. Its only environmental conditions are iron in territory, which trade or conquest can supply, and a city of five.
- ~544 breakthroughs.

### v3.117 — The Windmill, the Dry Country's Mill

- **The industrial chain no longer needs a river.** Irrigation requires a "River or Water source", and through Canal Building and the Water Mill it stood on every route to the Workshop, the Blast Furnace, the Steam Engine and everything after them: 170 rows, among them Orbital Mechanics, whose Apollo Program enables spaceship parts. A civilisation without water could never win; in a light simulator run with Irrigation removed it won none of 300 games.
- **The Windmill is the alternative.** It now requires Gearing and Weaving (the sails' cloth) instead of Sail Invention, which needed the Raft's coast or river, and it is CORE, like the Water Mill it stands in for, so the industrial chain keeps an all-CORE route without water. Crank and Connecting Rod (the Workshop), the Blast Furnace, Plate Armour and the Steam Engine each take the Water Mill or the Windmill. History is strongest for the crank (the Dutch wind-powered sawmill of the 1590s) and the Steam Engine (the Zaan's industrial windmills); bellows and hammers ran on water, or in dry country on animals, which have no row.
- **Irrigation now gates 21 rows**, nearly all bound to water by nature: the Water Management focus, the water UNIQUEs, Hydroponics and Vertical Farming, and Miasma Engineering, plus the optional Early Steam chain, whose Hero's Aeolipile needs Canal Building. Guarantee Rule 8 now names both mills.
- **The Windmill can be had from the Ancient era**, two eras early, because Gearing is labelled Ancient; as a CORE row it reveals itself then, at the era premium.
- **Measured** (familiar world): against v3.116, victory medians within one turn, with 5 unfinished games against 3 of 900, all STANDARD build-list ideas that never occurred. A civilisation without water (Irrigation removed) wins 299 of 300 expert games, at the same median as with water.
- **Not yet fixed:** Glassmaking's "Desert or Coast tile in territory" still bars a civilisation with neither from the Optics focus, electronics and most FTL methods (152 rows).
- ~544 breakthroughs (CORE ~142).

### v3.116 — The University Before the Press; Comparative Anatomy Merged

- **The University no longer requires the Printing Press.** Bologna (1088), Paris and Oxford came three centuries before Gutenberg, so the requirement ran history backwards. The University now needs Writing and Formal Logic, the grammar and logic its arts course was built on. It can therefore be had from the Classical era, one era early at the era premium, alongside the first schools of higher learning (Nalanda; Constantinople, 425; al-Qarawiyyin, 859; al-Azhar, 970). Its era stays Medieval.
- **Scientific Method requires the University and the Printing Press**, the two institutions it waited on: the universities, where natural philosophy was taught from about 1200 and most of the seventeenth century's founders trained, and the press, which made observations fixed, shared and checkable. On Formal Logic alone it was available from the Classical era, two eras early; it is now available from the Medieval era, and the Experimental Laboratory's University is certain to come first.
- **What depends on them.** The University is now on every route to 310 rows (3 before) and the Printing Press to 309 (5 before): Scientific Method and everything after it, and for the University also the Renaissance. All are Early Modern or later, while both rows are Medieval and CORE, so they arrive first and no timing moves. §1.4's example of a CORE row revealing itself now has the University waiting on Writing and Formal Logic.
- **Comparative Anatomy (O07) is merged into Anatomy Study (MD03).** Since its Anatomy Theatre was cut (v3.88), its only benefit was opening the Medicine focus, which Herbal Medicine and Writing also open, normally first. Its only dependent was Anatomy Study, which covers the same Classical tradition. Anatomy Study now requires Animal Behavior Study, Herbal Medicine and Natural Philosophy (which brings Writing), so it is still available from the Classical era and nothing downstream moves. The Medicine focus opens with Herbal Medicine and Writing.
- **Measured** (familiar world, three benchmark players, 900 games, against v3.115 with the same seeds): victory medians within one turn; 3 unfinished games against 10, all STANDARD build-list ideas that never occurred.
- ~544 breakthroughs (CORE ~141).

### v3.115 — Sixteen Missing Enablers

- **Sixteen rows gain the prerequisite history waited for.** A review of the 38 rows whose prerequisites can all be held two or more eras before their own era found nineteen whose missing enabler is already a row; these sixteen are adopted. Each row now arrives in its own era, or one era early with the rest of the gap left to demand:
  - Advanced Pyrotechnics (F07) after Saltpeter Mining (MI08), its oxidiser; and Saltpeter Mining after Alchemy (CH01), whose practitioners identified saltpetre (c. 492).
  - The Blast Furnace (ME08) after the Water Mill (WM05): water-driven bellows, in Han China (31 AD) and in 12th-century Europe.
  - Letter of Credit (TR05) and Probability Theory (MA06) after Banking (SO11), the bill of exchange and the insurer's risk.
  - Mongol Combined Arms (WA14) after the Stirrup (WA27).
  - Petroleum Discovery (MI07) after the Steam Engine (MC08): the steam-driven drilling rig (Drake, 1859), and an industry that wanted lamp oil.
  - The Industrial Canal Network (WM11) after the Joint Stock Company (TR06), the canal companies' capital.
  - Vaccination (MD04) and Calculus (MA05) after Scientific Method (PH04).
  - Existentialism (PH09) after The Enlightenment (PH14).
  - Information Theory (PH10) after the Vacuum Tube (EN01), the telephone and radio circuits it measured.
  - Fractal Mathematics (MA11) and Optimisation Theory (MA15) after the Programmable Computer (CP01).
  - Anaesthesia (MD07) after Oxygen Theory (CH05): nitrous oxide came out of the same pneumatic chemistry.
  - Vertical Farming (FA11) after Semiconductor Mastery (EN06), for LED lighting.
- **The rules hold:** no addition is from a later era than its row, a CORE row gains only CORE prerequisites, each exists in every world its row does, and none makes a cycle. The battery's results are unchanged.
- **Hyperspace:** Optimisation Theory's prerequisite puts the Programmable Computer on the build list, through Higher-Dimensional Geometry (MA19); the prove list already needed it.
- **Irrigation's terrain condition reaches further.** Through the Blast Furnace and Petroleum Discovery, 18 more rows, four of them CORE (the Blast Furnace, Iron-Frame Construction, the Bessemer Process and Petroleum Discovery), now cannot be had without Irrigation's "River or Water source": 170 in all. The gate predates this change (the Workshop and the Steam Engine already needed the Water Mill) and is an open question.
- **Measured** on two seed sets of 2,700 games each (three worlds, five methods, three benchmark players): victory medians move by three turns at most: the expert's one to three turns sooner, the other players' by a turn or less; 31 games are unfinished against 29 before, all of them STANDARD build-list ideas that never occurred, none a changed row.
- **Not adopted yet:** Scientific Method after the University, Comparative Anatomy after Natural Philosophy, and the Windmill after the Water Mill. ~545 breakthroughs.

### v3.114 — Dialectical Thinking Removed

- **PH08 Dialectical Thinking is removed**, by the rule that a row must justify its existence (v3.80). With Synthesis gone (v3.113) its only effect was ⟨Resting point for Influence with City-States is increased by [10]⟩, which Radio and Talking Drum Networks also grant, and no row needed it. Philosophy and the Arts keeps two UNCOMMON rows, Existentialism and Systems Thinking. ~545 breakthroughs.

### v3.113 — Experiment and Synthesis Removed

- **Experiment and Synthesis are removed** from Scientific Method (PH04) and Dialectical Thinking (PH08), and from §7.0's list of the fork's custom uniques. They were never part of the agreed design: two abilities written for the per-turn roll model ("spend Gold to boost one Revealed breakthrough's roll"; "once per era, re-roll one failed breakthrough") with no price, no size and no definition of a failed breakthrough, and no meaning in the discovery model's occurrence windows. §7.0 no longer counts its list: "one of these seven" had gone stale as the list grew.
- **The Experimental Laboratory's spec is restored** on Scientific Method: [190/2; requires a **University** in this city; +2 Science; Scientist ×1]. The v3.100 merge copied the building's name without its brackets (the merge script's spec reader stopped before the opening bracket), leaving the Early Modern era's Science landmark with no cost or statistics. The values are those the power pass recorded: +2 Science and a Scientist slot (v3.80 kept the +2 as the era's landmark), the price and upkeep set by value in v3.90, and the University requirement added in v3.88.

### v3.112 — The FTL Endgame Redesign Written In
- **§4 rewritten** from the direction document's W1–W19. A hypothesis for each candidate method, proposed by a CORE theory row and listed on the FTL screen from RC01 Special Relativity; the unified theory (QP08 / QM-E27) closes the list. **Three conditions per method**, each a Space-era result with a named host, a positive raising a method's standing and any failure ruling it out; **every report is true**; the screen shows facts, never odds or advice, and every hint points to where progress is possible now. **Generation rules**: the viable method uniformly, the other conditions at even odds, and a guarantee checked as a battery.
- **Instruments answer the measurement questions.** CP21 Macroscopic Quantum Control unlocks the Orbital Quantum Laboratory, the new **RC11 Gravitational Leakage Survey** the Space Interferometer, and RC05 Dark Matter Identification or RC06 Dark Energy Understanding the shared Survey Telescope — national wonders whose completion settles the question. RC05 and RC06 now sit alongside each other (RC02 + RK05 + the unified theory). Theoretical tests answer with their breakthrough. A test's evidence travels with it by diffusion, trade or theft; a measurement still needs the receiver's own instrument.
- **The theory phase opens promptly**: each unified theory needs its ontology's great instrument (QP08 needs QP04 Particle Accelerator; QM-E27 needs QM-E08 Boundary Furnace, and MA13 Topology so every proposal arrives first), and both instruments become **CORE**; QM-E08 needs EL07 Power Grid, as QP04 does. Neither instrument settles a condition any more.
- **The prototype** (a Grand Endeavour, one per method, on the FTL screen) costs **5×** the Space era's price, runs its full length and reports; the SS FTL Drive needs only the viable method's successful prototype. Committing before certainty is the **Hail Mary**. Completing the prototype and the Drive is announced to every civilisation; committing stays private.
- **The Space era widened** (W18): signature pools for Computing (CP16–CP22), Energy Systems (ES17–ES22), Materials Science (MS10–MS17), Mathematics (MA15–MA23), Nuclear Physics (NP12–NP17), Thermodynamics (TH15–TH22) and Aeronautics (AE14–AE20), each carrying its method's engineering; entry and pool rows at 2.5×, capstones at 4×, condition hosts and deciders at 1×. QM-E07 Aether Tension Engine moves to Energy Systems, QM-E23 Current Sail to Aeronautics (now STANDARD). Space Engineering settled: the Dyson Swarm becomes **SE09 Advanced Solar Power** and its Orbital Solar Array, and MS09 Dyson Sphere Materials is retired. Rocketry settled, with one new row, **RK17 Reconnaissance Satellites** (the Spy Satellite).
- **Balance**, measured on three benchmark players (expert, novice, average) at 60 games a cell: round two's changes — MS17 for Wormhole at 4×, ES18 without ES22, AE14 1×, atomic Tachyonic without NP11 (NP15 needs EL11 and a power plant), TH20 4×, MA19 1×, CP16 1× — and CP10 Quantum Computing at 2×. MS17 is **Transformation Media**: Shockproof Walls (siege resistance in their city) and Bomb Shelters made 50% more effective. Victory medians: expert about turn 294, novice 305, average player 380; methods within a few turns of each other.
- **Reliability**: MS02 Nanotechnology / Fine Structuring is CORE, and QM-E06 accepts MS02. **EL07 Power Grid becomes CORE**, since both CORE great instruments need it: with it STANDARD, a lost Power Grid idea left the unified theory unreachable (8 of 60 simulated Substance Plenum games).
- **Removed**: the three clauses speeding Tunnelling's "final research", a step from the older design; QP13 Hidden Variable Discovery's Determinist Institute instead reveals every breakthrough as soon as the civilisation becomes eligible for it.
- **The tachyonic-sector test moves to the Space era**, found by a traced game: it sat on QP06 Standard Model Completion and QM-E17 Complete Catalogue, Atomic-era rows, against W1. New rows **QP15 Beyond the Standard Model** and **QM-E28 Beyond the Catalogue** (Space, STANDARD, priced as deciders) host it as measurement tests; each unlocks a national wonder, the **Very Large Particle Collider** / **Very Large Boundary Furnace** (+6 Science, 2 Scientist slots), whose completion settles it. QP06 and QM-E17 return to plain prerequisites.
- **The Space era's price per discovery ×0.8** (§2).
- **Deferred**: the discovery model's write-up in §1.2 and §1.5 (Grand Endeavours at 2×, proximity revelation at 10%); the non-FTL Space-era fields; the fork engine work (the orbital unique, buildings that settle a condition, `Nuke.kt`'s context, the Determinist reveal, the announcements). New buildings' and units' costs and statistics are marked "set at spec".

### v3.111 — FTL Re-Audit, Part 2: The Evidence Map, a Scientific Register, the Aether Tension Engine

- **The Evidence Map (§4).** Every method's deciding rows in both ontologies, each with the report it gives where its method is the world's and the plainer one it gives everywhere else. Elemental worlds had no negative signals at all — nothing could be ruled out in 45% of games; now every method can be. The elemental negatives name only inversion: exotic matter is the medium driven *below its zero*, which is neither the thinning that is gravity (Model I, axiom 5) nor the opening of a cycle to the Great Cycle that every Generative Cycle Engine performs (Model II, axiom 6) — so the negative that rules out Warp and Wormhole cannot be read as forbidding either. Models I and II now say so in their own text.
  - **Wormhole is decided by RC04 Black Hole Thermodynamics and RC07 Closed Timelike Curves** — the rows it alone needs. The negatives are real physics: every throat pinches shut faster than light could cross it (Fuller and Wheeler), every closed curve proves unstable (chronology protection); in elemental worlds, every dark star closes smoothly, every closed circuit of the medium unwinds. RC04 is Atomic-era, so Wormhole can be confirmed or ruled out an era before exotic matter can be tested. FQ1 (FTL Method) gains the derived truth that makes this so — *Method B makes traversable geometry real* — which it had lacked.
  - **QP12 Casimir Effect Amplification and QM-E13 Aetheric Vacuum are no longer deciders.** FQ4 (Vacuum Energy) already says they always work as science; a report tied to the method would have contradicted it whenever the two disagreed. Wormhole still needs them, since a throat is held open by the Casimir effect's negative energy, real in every world.
- **QM-E07 Transcendent Engine becomes the Aether Tension Engine** (Substance) / **Still-Point Engine** (Process): power drawn from the medium's tension, or at the still point where the cycle turns — the vacuum tap at power scale. It needs QM-E13 Aetheric Vacuum and QM-E27 Plenum Gravitation, as ES12 Zero-Point Energy needs QP12 Casimir Effect Amplification and QP08 Quantum Gravity Theory, and like ES12 it validates or dead-ends with FQ4 (Vacuum Energy). It had been governed by nothing, so elemental worlds had their RARE endgame-energy shortcut five times as often as atomic ones. It keeps its Aluminium and Rare Earths, now grounded: with power this abundant, re-tempering's loss stops mattering (Substance), or Earth-patterns are driven forward into Metal faster than any mine (Process).
- **Plenum's sail is MS02 Nanotechnology / Fine Structuring**, not QM-E06 Exotic Matter (Elemental): the models keep the Plenum Drive apart from exotic Aether, the material of warps and throats. Elemental exotic matter now rules out exactly two methods, as its atomic mirror does, and Plenum completes in step with Tunnelling (about turn 287 in both).
- **Gravitic engineering no longer depends on exotic matter, in either kind of world.** RC09 Inertial Dampening needed MS08 Exotic Matter Engineering, which needs exotic matter — real only in Warp and Wormhole worlds — so gravitic engineering would have paid off in about 6% of games instead of 15%. It now takes MS02 Nanotechnology / Fine Structuring: gravity control is control of spacetime's coupling, of the Aether's rarefaction, or of the world-eddy's draw, and needs no negative density. MS08 itself now requires *validated* exotic matter — where none can be sustained there is nothing to fabricate — and declares that it exists only where the FTL method is Warp or Wormhole; battery check 20 accepts that form of declaration, which is not a World Truth.
- **Elemental names in a scientific register.** Elemental physics is science, so its late-era names are a scientist's, each grounded in the models: QM-E04 Unified Field Theory's Process name **Flow Field Theory**; QM-E05 **Re-Tempering Reactor / Self-Feeding Cycle Reactor**; QM-E08 **Boundary Furnace / Cycle-Limit Observatory**; QM-E10 **Temperament Medicine**, with its wonder the **Institute of Temperament**; QM-E11 **Full-Cycle Coupling** (the Great Wheel keeps its name); QM-E12 **Higher Strata Theory** — *Aether Strata Theory / Higher-Order Cycle Theory*; QM-E21 **Compact Re-Tempering Reactor / Compact Cycle Reactor**. §4's "higher realms", "ten thousand transformations" and the like are rewritten in the same register. CH21 Philosopher's Stone keeps its name: if science made something that worked like the fabled Stone, that is what it would be called.
- **Battery check 22:** every breakthrough row is a well-formed table row, closing pipe included. A row that had lost its closing pipe was still read by the battery but dropped by `reach.py` — and so by the generator, the harness and the viewer — while every check passed.
- **Measured** (30 harness runs a world): every elemental method completes within a few turns of its atomic mirror — Warp 294 against 296, Wormhole 294–295 against 301, Hyperspace 284–288 against 288, Tachyonic 278–285 against 282, Plenum 287 against Tunnelling's 287.
- **Still to come in this pass:** the spread between methods — Tachyonic is identified an era before the others and completes about 20 turns before Wormhole — and the Space era's length.

### v3.110 — FTL Re-Audit, Part 1: Structure and Era Parity

The FTL methods were audited in both ontologies — the rows each needs, whether each exists and is reachable in each world, when a civilisation completes each method's set in the harness, and the evidence each gives. Part 1 fixes structure and era parity; every change is grounded in *Elemental Physics, Models I and II*.

- **Four rows stood only in atomic worlds**, though Warp and Wormhole need them and v3.17 meant the Relativity chain to stand in both: Black Hole Thermodynamics, Dark Energy Understanding, Closed Timelike Curves and Exotic Matter Engineering. Their prerequisites named only atomic rows, so elemental worlds could not reach them, and nothing said what the SS FTL Drive should require in their place. Each now takes its elemental mirror and carries its elemental account:
  - *Black Hole Thermodynamics* — the **dark star**: rarefaction so deep that light cannot climb out, its rim glowing at the Aether's limiting fineness; or an eddy whose draw no Fire-movement escapes, its rim at the flow's fastest rhythm. Michell's 1783 dark star, read through Lorentzian aether-mechanics.
  - *Closed Timelike Curves* — **closed circuits of the medium**: the Aether's natural motion is the circle, and a fold lets a circuit close upon itself; where the medium gives a preferred frame these are closed paths in space, not in time.
  - *Dark Energy Understanding* — the same expansion, read as the Aether's tension at the scale of the heavens, or as yang's outward flow against the world-eddy's draw.
  - *Exotic Matter Engineering* — exotic Aether, or broken-cycle matter, made in quantity. Its text always said the elemental Exotic Matter satisfied it; its prerequisite now says so too.
- **Elemental worlds reached Warp, Wormhole and Hyperspace about 30 turns early.** Unified Field Theory did two jobs — the mirror of Quantum Field Theory (Atomic era) and of Quantum Gravity (Space era) — so the elemental FTL rows hung two steps closer to the start than their atomic mirrors. **Plenum Gravitation (QM-E27)**, a new CORE Space-era row, now takes Quantum Gravity's part: *Rarefaction Mechanics* in Substance worlds — bulk substance rarefies the Aether, and substance drifts toward rarer Aether (Model I, axiom 5) — and the *Mechanics of the Inward Draw* in Process worlds — the world-eddy's Earth-movement drawing every pattern inward (Model II, §2). It needs Unified Field Theory and General Relativity, as Quantum Gravity needs Quantum Field Theory and General Relativity, and like it maps strategic deposits — a dense body betrays itself by what it does to the medium. Exotic Matter, Supernal Spheres, the Celestial Current Survey and the Gravitational Coupling Experiment now follow it; the Aetheric Vacuum stays on Unified Field Theory, as its mirror, Casimir, follows Quantum Field Theory.
- **Elemental Gravitic worlds could never reach Inertial Dampening or Gravitic Lift** (about 7% of games), since both needed the atomic-only Exotic Matter Engineering. Now they can.
- **§4 states every method's prerequisites for both ontologies**, as the FTL Drive's derivation reads them. Elemental Tachyonic takes Unified Field Theory — which grants HIGH_ENERGY_PHYSICS — as Quantum Field Theory's mirror; the capability table had named a token from QM-E01, which does not grant it.
- **Measured in the harness** (30 runs a world), each elemental method now completes within a few turns of its atomic mirror — Warp about 295 against 296, Wormhole 296 against 301, Hyperspace 284 against 288, Tachyonic 278–284 against 282, Plenum 288–290 against Tunnelling's 287 — where they had run 15–35 turns early.
- The Quintessic Physics note no longer names the Medium Reservoir, removed in an earlier version.
- Tallies: 494 breakthroughs; CORE 138.
- **Still to come in this pass:** an evidence map — every method's deciding rows and both their reports, in both ontologies, since elemental worlds have no negative signals at all; the spread between methods (Wormhole completes about 20 turns after Tachyonic); and the Space era's length.

### v3.109 — Dual-Path Records Made Consistent

- **A dual path is recorded twice** — in the breakthrough's own text ("*Also discoverable through …*") and in the other focus's "*Also reachable in this focus*" line — and the two had drifted apart in eight rows. Each is now completed from whichever record had it, so every dual path either record stated is kept:
  - *Recorded only in the row's text:* Biomass Power Plant (ES03-W), through Farming, and Phase Exchange (TR16), through Chemistry — both added to those foci's lines.
  - *Recorded only in the focus's line:* Massively Parallel Bio-Processing (GN09), Organic Neural Architecture (GN11) and Animate Intelligence (GN14), through Computing (v3.2's text said so for the first two), and Room Temperature Superconductor (EL12), through Electronics as well as Materials Science — their texts now say so.
  - *Naming a focus that no longer exists:* the Telegraph (EL04) and the Printing Press (MC06) were "also discoverable" through a Communications focus; the sentences are gone.
- **One format for every dual-path line**: Computing's line now names each breakthrough's home as the others do, and Energy Systems' two lines are one.
- **22 breakthroughs** are reachable through more than one focus, with 26 extra focus entries in all.
- **Battery check 21:** both records of every dual path agree, and every focus they name exists.

### v3.108 — Era Boundaries Redrawn: An Even Pace, Base Civ V's Game Length

- **The fork's eras get their own boundaries on the Insight curve** (§2, *Era boundaries*, a new table). They had been base Civ V's tech-column costs, which left discoveries bunched — about 1.5 a turn in the Ancient era, 1.2 in the Modern and Atomic, 0.4 in the Space era. Redrawn, every era runs at about 0.8 a turn (0.79–0.83 measured), about 260 discoveries over 320 turns. The Ancient era grows from about 21 turns to 31, the Modern from 48 to 60 and the Atomic from 33 to 49; the Early Modern shrinks from 58 to 52 and the Space era from 50 to 29. Era prices follow — the span ÷ the expected discoveries — and were found by iterating against the harness, damped, since the Space era's realised count moves with its span.
- **Game length now matches base Civ V at every skill level.** The anchor is base Civ V's own: a strong player wins around turn 260, a good one around 320, a casual one around 400 (community records — Hall of Fame and CivFanatics benchmarks, r/civ). The fork had run about 5% faster at every level; redrawn, it takes about 262, 320 and 396. One Insight curve serves all three because the fork's extra Science scales with income.
- **The Space era is provisional.** It realises only about 24 discoveries, so an even pace makes it short; whether it should instead carry more content or run slower — it is where the FTL puzzle is solved — is decided with the FTL methods' re-audit (deferred).
- **Cost scaling is solved by turn, not by era.** §7's building column had matched each fork era against the same base era, which held while the two lined up in time; after the redraw it would have let fork cities spend 20–55% more of their Production on buildings than base cities at the same turn. Now, by the end of every fork era, the fork's buildings have taken about 1.1× the share of a city's Production that base Civ V's had by the same turn. Buildings: ×0.72 Primitive to Classical, ×0.86 Medieval, ×0.64 Early Modern, ×0.52 Modern, ×0.48 Atomic, ×0.45 Space and Interstellar. The unit column is unchanged — a city's Production at an era's end depends on its buildings, which arrive by era, not on where the boundaries sit.
- **The tools keep two sets of boundaries**: the fork's, redrawn, and base Civ V's own, so its calibration (the good player at turn 320) and its era timings are untouched.
- The calendar is unchanged: on Unciv's Standard schedule a game now ends about where base Civ V's does for the same player — the 1700s for a strong player, about 1900 for a good one, about 2000 for a casual one. Whether the fork should have an era-aligned calendar of its own is still open.

### v3.107 — DNA Structure in Every World

- **DNA Structure needs Germ Theory discovered, not validated.** When Mendelian Genetics merged into it (v3.101), its prerequisite was written as "Germ Theory *validated*" — Mendelian Genetics had needed only Germ Theory discovered. Germ Theory is attempted everywhere but validates only in Germ worlds, so as written DNA Structure, and the genetics line after it, would have been closed in Miasma and Humoral worlds: about 40% of games. Corrected.
- **Battery check 20:** a prerequisite on a validated result may appear only on a row that declares the matching world in its own text ("Only in Miasma worlds", "*Substance only.*", "Germ-exclusive"). Every such row did, except DNA Structure.

### v3.106 — Distillation Needs Alchemy

- **Distillation needs Alchemy again** (Alchemy + Controlled Fire, as before v3.105). The merge had carried over Acid/Base Discovery's second route, Charcoal with Iron Smelting, but metalworking leading to the Distillery and Whisky made little sense. With that route gone, the line Distillation opens — Oxygen Theory, Explosives, the Electricity focus, the Voltaic Pile, Refrigeration — needs Alchemy too; Alchemy is a Classical CORE row, so it is always reachable.
- **Acid/Base Discovery's Mine Production is dropped.** Without the metalworking route it no longer fitted the row, and it is not needed for balance: the Production ledgers never counted civilisation-wide tile bonuses (base Civ V's own Chemistry mine upgrade included), and fork mines still match or beat base mines at every stage — +1 from Deep Shaft Mining in the Ancient era against base's +1 from Chemistry in the Renaissance. What Distillation keeps of Acid/Base Discovery is its place as the gateway to the acid-dependent chemistry and electricity.

### v3.105 — Thinning, Batch 7: Middle-Era Skeleton Merges; A Notation Without Precedence

- **Eight Classical, Medieval and Early Modern merges:**
  - *Trigonometry → Basic Algebra*: the mathematics of Alexandria and Baghdad. Flight, the Flying Buttress and Geodesic Structures follow Basic Algebra.
  - *Epistemology → Formal Logic*: Aristotle's logic and his account of knowledge. The University, the Renaissance, Scientific Method, Dialectics and Existentialism follow Formal Logic.
  - *Acid/Base Discovery → Distillation*, which keeps the better-known name: the mineral acids were themselves products of distillation — vitriol into oil of vitriol, saltpetre and alum into *aqua fortis*. Distillation carries the Distillery, Whisky and the acid-leaching Mine Production, and is reached through **Alchemy, or Charcoal with Iron Smelting** — the reading Acid/Base Discovery's note always intended, which its prerequisite as written had not said. The Electricity focus, Oxygen Theory, Explosives, Systematic Element Analysis, the Voltaic Pile, Refrigeration and Caloric Storage follow Distillation.
  - *Sternpost Rudder → Caravel*: the rudder made the great hulls possible. The Caravel carries the Galleass and now also needs the Trireme and Iron Smelting.
  - *Double-Entry Bookkeeping → Banking*: the Medici kept double-entry books. Banking carries the National Treasury.
  - *Static Electricity → Voltaic Pile*: Galvani's frogs and Volta's pile were one line of experiment. The Pile takes over Static Electricity's prerequisites and its Museum Culture.
  - *Microscope → Telescope*: the same lens-makers made both, Janssen around 1590. DNA Structure, Germ Theory and Phage Therapy follow the Telescope.
  - *Nation-State → Public Education*: the nineteenth-century state schooled and conscripted together. Public Education carries the Constabulary, the Police Station and the extra Unit Supply, and keeps its own prerequisites, so CORE closure holds.
- **Prerequisites no longer rely on operator precedence.** "CH01 or F03 + ME04" and "(CH01 or F03 + ME04)" look alike but read differently — "+" bound more loosely than "or" outside a parenthesis, and "or" more loosely than "+" inside one. Now, whenever a cell uses both, every group of alternatives is in parentheses ("WA10 + (ME05 or ME08)") and so is every alternative that needs several rows ("G02 + (G07 or (FA01 + FA02))"). The 25 cells that leaned on precedence are rewritten; a comparison of every row's reading before and after shows that none changed except the merges and two intended fixes — Distillation's route, and **Electric Aviation**, which now reads as superconductors, *or* batteries with miniaturisation (it had required Miniaturization on both routes). §7's notation says so, and **battery check 19** holds every cell to it.
- **Effect:** the familiar world's realised discoveries fall from about 273 to about 264 — about 0.87 a turn.
- **The thinning phase is complete.** From v3.99 to here: about 307 realised discoveries a game to about 264 (1.01 to 0.87 a turn), and about 548 breakthroughs to about 493 — stepping stones first, then moderate skeleton merges, with every canonical object kept, every Competing Solution and World Truth row untouched, and UNCOMMON, RARE and UNIQUE rows left as the source of variety between games. Next: bring each World Truth axis level, then redraw the era boundaries.
- Tallies: 493 breakthroughs; CORE 137, STANDARD 204.

### v3.104 — Thinning, Batch 6: Atomic-Era Skeleton Merges

- **Five Atomic merges:**
  - *Operating System → Programmable Computer*: a computer and the system that makes it usable. Cryptography, Narrow AI, Quantum Computing's silicon route and the Bio-Computing gateway follow Programmable Computer.
  - *Networking → Internet*, which takes over Networking's prerequisites, its capital-connected Science and its Stock Exchange Gold.
  - *Database Systems → Internet*: the Database Centre — the networked age keeps its records in data centres.
  - *Genetic Crop Engineering → Genetic Engineering*: the two were one idea, and Genetic Engineering's own growth was already labelled "engineered crops". Genetic Engineering now also needs Selective Breeding.
  - *Kevlar/Armor Textiles → Carbon Fiber*: the Flak Jacket promotion; Carbon Fiber now also needs Polymer Science.
- **Kept:** Machine Learning, part of the tuned machine-intelligence Competing Solution (the Perceptron alternative is balanced against the rewards Machine Learning gives AI Labs) and a prerequisite of a Vitalist row.
- **Effect:** the Atomic era's realised discoveries fall from about 39 to about 37, but the Space era rises by about one — the late game is limited by game length, and Insight saved in the Atomic era reaches more of the Space era — so the game total moves only from about 274 to about 273.
- Tallies: 501 breakthroughs; CORE 142, STANDARD 207.

### v3.103 — Thinning, Batch 5: Modern-Era Skeleton Merges

- **Eight Modern merges:**
  - *Electric Motor → Electric Generator*: the motor is the generator run backwards. The Dirigible's alternative and the Ion Drive follow the Generator.
  - *Telephone → Wireless Transmission*: Bell and Marconi, telecommunication by wire and without. Wireless Transmission now also needs Precision Machining.
  - *Television → Radio*: broadcasting by sound and then by picture. Radio carries the Broadcast Tower Gold, the Sydney Opera House, the CN Tower and the City-State influence, and now also needs Lens Grinding.
  - *Heat Exchanger → Turbine Design*: the steam plant's two halves. Turbine Design now also needs Bessemer steel; the Fission and Fusion Plants and the elemental reactor plant (ES15) follow it, so both worlds keep their route.
  - *Cryogenics → Refrigeration*: Linde built the refrigerator and then liquefied air. Refrigeration now also needs the Periodic Table; Superconductivity and Cryogenic Preservation follow it, and Helium Extraction's accelerator applies to it.
  - *Set Theory / Formal Logic → Topology*: the new mathematics of the same decades. Topology takes over Set Theory's prerequisites, so it keeps its depth; Cryptography, Quantum Mechanics and Hidden Variables follow it.
  - *Solid Fuel Rocket → Rocket Propellant*: the first rockets were weapons, made from the propellant itself. Rocket Propellant carries the Rocket Artillery and Mobile SAM, and still opens the Rocketry focus, whose first row is now Liquid Fuel Rocket.
  - *Hydroelectric Dam → Dam Construction*: the dam and its turbines. Dam Construction carries the Hydro Plant and now also needs the Electric Generator.
- **Kept:** the Rigid Airship, part of the airship chain that the Airship Disaster acts on.
- **Effect:** the Modern era's realised discoveries fall from about 68 to about 60, and the game's from about 282 to about 274. The Modern price rises from 672 to 761.
- Tallies: 506 breakthroughs; CORE 144, STANDARD 210.

### v3.102 — Thinning, Batch 4: Ancient-Era Skeleton Merges

- **The thinning pass moves to moderate skeleton merges**, aiming at about 260 discoveries a game (about 0.85 a turn). Stepping stones alone would have left it near 290. A skeleton merge combines two adjacent foundational steps of the same theme and era into one larger breakthrough; every canonical object keeps a home, CORE closure holds, and nothing moves across eras. The merges fall mostly in the Ancient, Modern and Atomic eras — the densest, at about 1.9, 1.5 and 1.3 discoveries a turn — so that era lengths stay sensible when the boundaries are redrawn.
- **Eight Ancient merges:**
  - *Lever and Pulley → Gearing*: lever, pulley and gear together, as in Hellenistic mechanics. Gearing now needs the Wheel and Geometry.
  - *Zero and Place Value → Geometry*: arithmetic and geometry together, as in Babylon and Alexandria. Geometry keeps its own prerequisites, so roads and arches are not delayed; Basic Algebra, Binary Mathematics, Mayan Vigesimal Mathematics and TR18 follow Geometry.
  - *Flood Control → Aqueduct*: the two shared their prerequisites. The Aqueduct now carries Remove Marsh, the Polder gate and the Flood-plain Food; the Dam follows it.
  - *Postal System → Arch Construction*: the Post Office rides the roads.
  - *Selective Breeding (Animals) → Selective Breeding*: one row for plants and herds, now needing Herd Animal Domestication as well as Seed Saving, and discoverable through **Farming or Animal Husbandry** (dual-path). Carrier Pigeons and Veterinary Medicine follow it.
  - *Sulfur Mining → Geological Observation*: sulphur is gathered where vents and springs bring it up. Gunpowder, Pyrotechnics and Greek Fire follow Geological Observation.
  - *Megalithic Construction → Monumental Architecture*: Stonehenge, the Pyramids and the Mausoleum, with the border-culture discount; the Megalithic Observatory follows it.
  - *Maritime Trade → Sail Invention*: the Port — the Phoenicians traded as soon as they sailed.
- Four CORE rows gain content that only some civilisations used to reach: the Post Office, the Port, Remove Marsh, and Stonehenge and the Pyramids.
- **Effect:** the Ancient era's realised discoveries fall from about 39 to about 32, and the game's from about 288 to about 282. The Ancient price rises from 8.2 to 9.6.
- Tallies: 514 breakthroughs; CORE 147, STANDARD 215; about 19 dual-path rows.

### v3.101 — Thinning, Batch 3: The Remaining Stepping Stones

- **Apiculture is cut outright.** Its one effect, +1 Gold from Citrus, fitted no neighbouring row naturally, nothing depended on it, and it did not matter to the balance.
- **Ten stepping stones merge into their natural hosts**, each effect copied verbatim and every dependent re-pointed:
  - *Wool Processing → Weaving*: Sheep Production and tundra Happiness, an era earlier.
  - *Pitch and Tar → Charcoal Production*: tarred roads and caulked hulls — wood tar is the kiln's by-product. Greek Fire now needs Charcoal Production and Sulphur.
  - *Mechanical Seed Drill → Gearing*: −25% Farm build time; the seed drill is a geared machine, Han-era in China.
  - *Camera Obscura → Lens Grinding*: +1 Culture per Artist; the two had the same prerequisites in the same era.
  - *Insurance → Probability Theory*: +10% Gold. Insurance was found by about 40% of civilisations and Probability Theory by about 83%, so Early Modern Gold rises; the Gold budget sat below base and has room. Futures Market now needs TR05 + Probability Theory + Statistics.
  - *Mendelian Genetics → DNA Structure*, which takes over its prerequisites and its +1 Food from Pasture.
  - *Synthetic Fibers → Polymer Science*: +15% towards Land units — nylon is a polymer. Kevlar now follows Polymer Science.
  - *Supersonic Flight → Jet Engine*: Fighter range and strength. Scramjet now needs the Jet Engine, Composite Materials and Carnot Efficiency.
  - *Graphene Production → Nanotechnology*: +5% Production. Dyson Sphere Materials now follows Nanotechnology.
  - *Fiber Optics → Networking*: +1 Gold from Stock Exchanges.
- **Kept, though the scan flagged them:** Satellite Communications, the atomic twin of Aether Telegraphy (merging it into the world-neutral Satellite Technology would give elemental worlds both twins); Carnot Efficiency, whose natural host, Laws of Thermodynamics, dead-ends in Caloric worlds — both wait for the World Truth phase.
- **Effect:** the familiar world's realised discoveries fall from about 299 to about 288 (307 when the pass began), about 0.95 a turn. With this batch the tree has essentially no stepping stones left.
- Tallies: 522 breakthroughs; STANDARD 220.

### v3.100 — Thinning, Batch 2: Warfare, Construction, Philosophy and the Arts, Metallurgy, Observation

- **Twelve rows merged**, their objects moving with them and every dependent re-pointed:
  - *Phalanx Tactics* — cut: its fork Phalanx duplicated the canonical Spearman of the row before it.
  - *Pike and Shot → Drill and Discipline*, which gains the Lancer and the gunpowder-versus-mounted bonus.
  - *Road Building → Arch Construction*: roads and their bridges together. Roads now need Geometry rather than Specialisation of Labour, both CORE rows of the same era.
  - *Prefabrication → Assembly Line*, which gains +15% towards all buildings and +25% towards Military Bases.
  - *Translation Movement → University*, which gains Oxford University.
  - *Opera → Renaissance*, which gains the Opera House, Ceilidh Hall, Theatre and Sistine Chapel — opera began in Renaissance Florence, and the Sistine ceiling dates from 1512.
  - *Empiricism → Scientific Method*, which gains the Experimental Laboratory; every civilisation now has it rather than about 85% of them, about +0.3 Science per Early Modern city, inside the Science budget.
  - *Mass Media → Television*, which gains the Sydney Opera House, the CN Tower and the City-State influence.
  - *Cast Iron → Steel Production*, which gains +1 Gold from Iron; Cannons and the Atmospheric Engine now accept Steel Production where they accepted Cast Iron.
  - *Tinplate/Canning → Nation-State*, which gains +2 Unit Supply per city — the nation in arms, fed from cans (Napoleon offered the prize Appert won in 1810).
  - *Magnetism Discovery → Geological Observation*, which gains the iron sorting and the lodestone's Culture, and becomes the Compass's prerequisite.
  - *Newtonian Mechanics* — cut: its +1 Range for Siege units duplicated Calculus's, and the two had stacked.
- §7's canonical-object table follows the moves: the Lancer to WA28; the Road to CO03; Oxford University to CU06; the Opera House, Ceilidh Hall, Theatre and Sistine Chapel to CU07; the Sydney Opera House and CN Tower to EN05.
- **Effect:** the familiar world's realised discoveries fall from about 305 to about 299 (from about 307 when the pass began). Era prices rise most in the Early Modern era, from 412 to 453. The Space era fills as earlier eras thin (about 17 to 20), since freed Insight reaches the late tree sooner.
- Tallies: 533 breakthroughs; CORE 150, STANDARD 231.

### v3.99 — Thinning, Batch 1: Chemistry

- **The thinning pass begins.** Target: a bit under one discovery a turn — about 0.8, some 240 a game against about 305 now — fairly even across the game, with bursts and two- or three-turn gaps both common. It runs in three phases: thin focus by focus, merging before cutting; bring each World Truth axis level; then redraw the era boundaries on the Insight curve so the remainder runs evenly. UNCOMMON, RARE and UNIQUE rows stay, because they are what makes games differ and together are worth only about ten discoveries a game; the cuts fall on CORE and STANDARD stepping stones.
- **How thinning slows the game.** An era's price is its Insight ÷ its expected discoveries, so fewer breakthroughs mean each one costs more, and the pace falls with the content; thinning is what keeps arrivals on time at the slower pace. Era prices are recomputed after every batch — this one raises the Medieval price from 110 to 112, the Early Modern from 404 to 412 and the Modern from 614 to 636.
- **Three merges:**
  - *Electrochemistry (CH10) → the Voltaic Pile (EL02).* Both were electroplating Gold; Davy's electrolysis was the pile put to work. The Pile gains +1 Gold from luxury tiles, and becomes the prerequisite of Cold Fusion and Electric Aviation. Direct-Current Stations' bonus to Generating Stations now waits on Aluminium Extraction instead — aluminium smelting is what DC power ran on — so it stays a later, earned step rather than coming free.
  - *Portland Cement (CH16) → Reinforced Concrete (CO11)*, which now requires Roman Concrete *or* Oxygen Theory, plus Bessemer steel, and carries the road-speed unique for cement roads. Reinforced concrete was Portland cement and steel.
  - *Crystallography (CH17) → the Periodic Table (CH06)*, which gains +1 Gold from Gems and becomes the prerequisite of Piezoelectricity and Semiconductor Mastery.
- **Effect:** Chemistry's realised content falls from about 17 to 14.5; the familiar world's total from about 307 to 305.
- Tallies: 545 breakthroughs; STANDARD 241.

### v3.98 — Closing the Power Pass; One Prerequisite Parser

- **Mycological Knowledge (G06) becomes a path less travelled.** Nothing waits on it any more: Antibiotics now needs Germ Theory + Organic Chemistry (penicillin was made usable by chemists, Florey and Chain, and deep-tank fermentation), Nitrogen Fixation Awareness only Crop Rotation (legume nodules hold bacteria, not fungi), Biomass/Biogas Distillation + Organic Chemistry (anaerobic digestion is bacterial), and Vaccination MD03 + Herd Animal Domestication (the milkmaid's observation). It stays UNCOMMON, and a civilisation that finds it is quicker down a whole biological lane — Antibiotics, Bio-Organic Synthesis and Biomass/Biogas each cost ×0.5. That wording is *provisional*: how accelerators are stated awaits the discovery-model decision. Its Compost Yard becomes the **Mushroom Cellar** [30/0; +1 Food from Forest and from Jungle tiles in its city] — mushrooms from woods left standing. With the gate gone, a Germ-world civilisation reaches the Medical Lab about as often as any STANDARD, rather than about a third of the time.
- **The Biogas Plant is weaker, not cheaper**, as the Biomass Plant became in v3.96: +1 Production at 440/2. Its +2 at 310 dominated the Coal Plant.
- **Selective Breeding's prerequisite now says what it meant:** Seed Saving + (Botanical Classification, or Irrigation with Crop Rotation). As written it read as requiring Crop Rotation outright; Crop Rotation leaves the list of non-CORE rows a CORE must wait on.
- **The tools had two prerequisite parsers, and each misread rows.** The battery's safety and world checks stripped every parenthesised group, IDs included, so they lost the alternatives in 40 rows — Star Fort's Cannons-or-Trebuchet, nearly every atomic-or-elemental split. The generator and harness kept groups but ANDed comma-separated alternatives (Gene Therapy's three medicine routes) and lost IDs in nested descriptions (the Blast Furnace's Coal Discovery since v3.94; Quantum Computing's Entanglement Mastery). All of them now read prerequisites through **one shared parser** (`prereq.py`), whose rules are written into §7 as the Prerequisites-column notation. Re-reading every row through it, the safety check still finds all 76 requirement links safe. The three cells that had been rewritten to suit the old parsers (the Blast Furnace, Quantum Computing, Selective Breeding) keep their clearer forms.
- **Battery check 18:** every ID left in a prerequisite cell once descriptions are set aside is one the parser reads.

### v3.97 — Era Entry: Small Counts, Provisional

- **Era entry now needs a small count of breakthroughs from the era or later** — 5 (Ancient), 3 (Classical), 3 (Medieval), 5 (Early Modern), 7 (Modern), 6 (Atomic), 4 (Space) — about a tenth of each era's expected discoveries. The previous counts (8, 12, 15, 18, 20, 18, 15) dated from the first draft and left a civilisation up to an era behind its own development.
- **Why not base Civ V's rule.** Unciv enters an era with the first technology from it (`TechManager.updateEra`: the era of the most advanced researched tech). That suits a dense chain of techs; in the fork a single breakthrough can arrive well ahead, and a Mathematics specialist would enter the Early Modern to Atomic eras 0.8–1.1 eras early, firing game-wide triggers early. A breadth requirement (breakthroughs from two or three foci) was tested and added nothing beyond the count.
- **What entry affects** is now stated beside the era table: policy branches, the era's uniques, Faith purchase prices, embarked defence, research agreements and the AI's city valuation. None of the fork's own pricing depends on it.
- **Not settled.** The counts were measured on the discovery workshop's rate-model candidate, so they depend on the discovery model's pace; they are marked provisional in §2 and recorded as a lever for playtesting.

### v3.96 — One Engine; Plants for Every Fuel

- **The Alcohol Combustion Engine (MC09-W) is merged into the Internal Combustion Engine (MC09),** exactly as the blast furnaces were in v3.94. Its Distillery Fuel Depot already made Oil, at a real cost — three worked Farms and −20% growth — and the Landship and Tank already followed either engine. The engine now requires MC08 + (Petroleum Discovery + Petrochemistry *or* Distillation + Organic Chemistry), becomes CORE (the alcohol option is all CORE), and grants the Landship, Tank and Anti-Tank Gun with the Depot. Every civilisation can raise Depots: an expensive way to get Oil, worth it to a civilisation short of wells, or to one pushing to field more tanks and aircraft than its wells could fuel. Heavier-Than-Air Flight no longer waits on a roll; the battery's count of CORE rows whose OR-group offers no CORE option falls from 6 to 5. The Landship and Tank take the engine as a plain `requiredTech`; the transmutation row's Oil conditional names it; the engine keeps the alcohol route's dual path through Chemistry.
- **The chemistry pair stays separate** — Petrochemistry and Bio-Organic Synthesis are competing technologies, not two routes to one resource.
- **The Coal and Oil Power Plants exist in every world.** They required Coal Discovery and Petroleum Discovery, so fossil-free worlds could not build them even though their Kilns and Depots now make Coal and Oil. They now require knowing the fuel by either route: the Blast Furnace for Coal, Petroleum Discovery or the engine for Oil. In fossil-free worlds the Biomass and Bio-Fuel Plants therefore become ordinary Competing Solutions, as they are elsewhere: a civilisation that discovers the Coal Power Plant first is locked out of the Biomass Plant.
- **The Biomass Plant is weaker, not cheaper.** It had the Coal Plant's +2 Production and +5% for 310 against 440, with no Coal and no smoke, which dominated the Coal Plant despite its row calling it "weaker". It now gives +1 Production and +5% for 440/2, what its peers cost: a power station costs what it costs, whatever it burns. (The value model had priced the Coal Plant's smoke, −1 Happiness, as a point of value, since it counts any unique that is not a positive yield as one point — the crudeness noted in v3.90.)
- **Knock-on edits:** the Airship and Heavier-Than-Air Flight name MC09 alone, as does Aeronautics' availability; the fossil-free world's description and Guarantee Rule 9 are restated; the Competing Solutions table loses its Motorisation row (16 groups: 10 persist, 6 are superseded).
- Tallies: 548 breakthroughs; CORE 152, STANDARD 244.

### v3.95 — A Stale Name in a Conditional

- **A regression from v3.94.** The Substance world's transmutation row (CH20) opens its fuel improvements once a civilisation knows the fuel by either route, written as a De Morgan OR: ⟨Only available <after discovering [non-[{non-[Coal Discovery]} {non-[Charcoal Blast Furnace]}]]>⟩. v3.94 removed the Charcoal Blast Furnace, but its leftover check searched for the row's ID, not its name, and no battery check read names nested inside conditionals. The conditional now names the Blast Furnace, which makes Coal through its Charcoal Kiln, so the OR still means "discovering the seams, or making the fuel".
- **Battery check 17:** every breakthrough named in a tech conditional — `<after discovering […]>` or `<before discovering […]>` inside a unique, including names nested in De Morgan ORs — must be a real row or a canonical tech. Text in backticks is excluded, since it describes patterns with placeholders. Run against v3.94 it finds exactly this reference; the document holds 17 such conditionals.

### v3.94 — One Blast Furnace

- **The Charcoal Blast Furnace (ME08-W) is merged into the Blast Furnace (ME08).** Its Charcoal Kiln already makes Coal, so a separate breakthrough had only two jobs left: giving coal-less worlds a route that does not need Coal Discovery, and making civilisations diverge by discovery order. One row does the first with an OR — the Blast Furnace now requires ME04 + CO02 + (Coal Discovery *or* Charcoal Production) — and the Kiln's own economics do the second better than a lock-out: it needs three worked fuel tiles, and its −10% Production cancels a Factory's scaling.
- **The Blast Furnace becomes CORE.** All its prerequisites are CORE, and CORE rows depend on it. The Steam Engine, Iron-Frame Construction (the Factory's row), the Railway Network and the Bessemer Process no longer wait on a roll. The battery's count of CORE rows whose OR-group offers no CORE option falls from 10 to 6.
- **The Charcoal Kiln becomes a general way to get Coal — expensive, and therefore a choice.** A coal-rich civilisation will rarely bother; a coal-poor one has a way forward; and an aggressive one can pay the premium to raise a coal-powered army larger than its seams could supply. The Blast Furnace grants it alongside the canonical Ironworks, which needs Coal from pits or kilns alike.
- **Two objects go.** The *Charcoal Ironworks*: once charcoal is made at scale, a normal Ironworks will do, so the Ironworks slot tag and its derivation clause go with it. The *Managed Forest*: it had no yield, nothing read it, and building one cost a Lumber Mill's place; the Kiln already stops when its city stops working its woods.
- **Knock-on edits:** six dependents now name ME08 alone (Bessemer, Iron-Frame Construction, the Railway, the Steam Engine, the Ironclad, and Cannons already did); the Peat Cutting and Distillery rows point at ME08; the fossil-free world's description no longer lists the Blast Furnace among the rows removed with Coal Discovery, and no longer calls the -W alternatives UNCOMMON (they have been STANDARD Competing Solutions since v3.61); Guarantee Rule 8 is restated; the Competing Solutions table loses its blast-furnace row (17 groups: 11 persist, 6 are superseded).
- Tallies: 549 breakthroughs; CORE 151, STANDARD 246.

### v3.93 — Fossil-Free Guarantees

- **Found by the threshold generator**, which places every non-CORE a CORE must wait on as though it were CORE: two chains broke Guarantee Rules 8 and 10 in FOSSIL_FUELS_ABSENT worlds.
- **Industrialisation needed three forest tiles.** The Steam Engine, Iron-Frame Construction (the Factory's row) and the Railway Network, all CORE, can only come through the Charcoal Blast Furnace (ME08-W) there, and its row required ≥3 forest tiles in territory — a terrain condition trade cannot meet. It duplicated, more strictly, a condition its building already has: the Charcoal Kiln yields Coal only while its city works more than two fuel tiles (Forest, Jungle or Peat Cutting). The row's condition goes. The knowledge is open to every civilisation, and the Coal still needs fuel tiles or trade, as §1.2 intends for strategic resources.
- **The space race waited on an UNCOMMON roll.** Polymer Science and Liquid-Fuel Rocket, both CORE, can only come through Bio-Organic Synthesis (CH09-W) there, which required Mycological Knowledge (G06, UNCOMMON). G06 now *speeds* it instead: it makes Bio-Organic Synthesis cost ×0.5 (a multiplier, §1.2), on the pattern of the Atmospheric Engine's discount to the Steam Engine. Bio-Organic Synthesis requires Organic Chemistry and Selective Breeding only.
- **Vaccination's prerequisite said something the tools could not read.** "MD03 + G06 or empirical observation" names no row for its second option, so every check treated G06 as required. The observation is the milkmaid's, which needs herds: Vaccination now requires MD03 + (G06 or AH02, Herd Animal Domestication — CORE, Primitive).
- Guarantee Rules 8 and 10 now state how they hold.
- **Still open:** ten STANDARD rows remain that a CORE must wait on (the combustion and alcohol engines, the two blast furnaces, petrochemistry and bio-organic synthesis, the two telegraph rows, crop rotation and selective breeding) — reported by the battery since v3.73. Each fires about 85% of the time over a game.

### v3.92 — The Space Era Consolidated

- **The problem.** At value price the Space era's new buildings loaded a fork city about five times as much as base Civ V's, which adds almost nothing in its last era. A price could absorb that only at about ×0.22 — a sign of too many buildings, not of a wrong price.
- **The Planetary Defense Network becomes a national wonder** (SE13): one network for the whole planet, not a building in every city. [national wonder; 750/0; cityStrength +10; cityHealth +100; ⟨[+15]% Strength for cities⟩; ⟨Cost increases by [180] per owned city⟩], on vanilla's pattern — the National College costs 125 and 30 more per city. With the row's own +15%, every city gains +30% Strength, roughly what the old +15% and +10 Strength a city gave; the host city keeps the old +10 Strength and +100 HP, as the network's hardened centre. It has no building requirement: the Bomb Shelter vanishes when nuclear weapons are disabled, and the Military Base and Radar Station sit on STANDARD rows that are not ancestors of SE13, so none is a safe predecessor.
- **The Orbital Depot goes; Orbital Resource Delivery (SE06) upgrades the Asteroid Mining Station instead**, adding ⟨Provides [2] [Aluminum]⟩ and ⟨Provides [2] [Rare Earths]⟩ to each station `<after discovering [Orbital Resource Delivery]>`. SE06 always follows Asteroid Prospecting, and three upgraded stations provide exactly what three stations and three depots did.
- **The Quantum Network goes; Quantum Communication (QP14) carries ⟨[+2 Gold] from each Trade Route⟩ itself.** Nine buildings already gave Gold per trade route. QP14 also loses a stale sentence naming it the prerequisite of Interstellar Communication, a row removed in v3.80.
- **The Orbital Platform stays a building:** per-city orbital industry is a choice a city makes, unlike a planet-wide network.
- **Result.** The Space era's load at value price falls to about 3.4× base, and the Space row is solved on it at ×0.32 (Interstellar follows). Every era from the Ancient to the Space now loads a fork city 1.09–1.10× as much as base. Net Gold by the Space era improves from −87 to −79, against base's −66. Most of what remains in the Space era is the Fusion Plant upgrade; base Civ V has no Space-era plant.

### v3.91 — Cost Scaling Corrected: Whole-City Production and Calibrated Eras

- **What v3.90 got wrong.** Its multipliers were solved against the power ledger's Production ratio, about 1.3× base, but the ledger counts only buildings' Production. A real city's Production is mostly worked tiles, which both games share, and percentage bonuses multiply those as well. Counting them, a fork city is only about 1.05–1.2× as productive as a base one. So v3.90's unit and wonder multipliers (up to ×1.35) made units take 3–17% *longer* than base, and its building column left each era's new buildings loading a fork city 1.2–2.3× as much as base, and far more in the Space era, not the 1.1× it reported. The build-time ledger also had two faults of its own: it counted only the first power plant a city ever builds, never the upgrades, and it counted capital-only, spaceship-stack and per-civilisation-limited buildings as though every city built them. Both are fixed, and the ledger can now list what it counts.
- **The era model.** Era lengths come from an empire's Insight per turn: 1 Science per citizen, building Science and the Palace's +3, with percentage bonuses applied to the whole city (the first version applied them to buildings alone and left out the Palace, which put base Civ V at 795 turns). The Science the typical city does not model — specialists, trade routes, city-states, policies, wonders, Great Scientists — enters as a factor that ramps to about ×2.5 by the Space era, calibrated so base Civ V's science victory falls around turn 320: good players win by 300, and after about 375 someone else usually has. The calibrated base game enters the Space era around turn 264; the fork's eras run about a tenth shorter from the Medieval era.
- **The corrected table** (§7, Cost scaling). *Units, wonders and spaceship parts:* the whole-city Production ratio — ×1.00 in the Primitive and Classical eras, ×1.05 Ancient, ×1.10 Medieval and Early Modern, ×1.20 Modern, ×1.15 Atomic, ×1.20 Space. *Buildings:* solved era by era on the share of a city's Production each era's new buildings take — ×0.79 in the Primitive to Classical eras, ×0.60 Medieval, ×0.84 Early Modern, ×0.48 Modern, ×0.48 Atomic. Cheaper buildings do not add power, since yields are budgeted per city whatever a building costs; and because the full set now takes about base Civ V's time, a city spends about the same share of its Production on buildings as in vanilla, so the balance between buildings and units holds.
- **Result** (from the document's table): each era's new buildings load a fork city 1.09–1.10× as much as base from the Medieval to the Atomic era, and 1.10× across the Ancient and Classical eras together. Units match the whole-city ratio in every era.
- **The Space era is left provisional at ×0.50.** At value price it adds about five times base Civ V's building load, which a price could absorb only at about ×0.22 — a sign of too many late buildings, not of a wrong price. Its buildings will be consolidated first and the value re-solved.
- **Assumptions to verify in playtesting:** 1.5 Production per citizen from worked tiles, plus 2 from the city centre; 1, 3, 5, 6, 7 and then 8 cities; the ramp's shape; and the 0.05 per-city factor, Civ V's standard-map value, which the local Unciv source does not show.

### v3.90 — Cost Tuning (Power Rebalance, Stage 5)

- **The invariant.** Build time: a typical city's full building set should take about as long as base Civ V's, with a tenth more for the fork's breadth. Before this pass it took 1.48–2.05× base time from the Medieval era on and 2.2–2.7× earlier. From the Medieval era, canonical buildings alone took only 0.74–0.88×: the extra Production was already there to fund breadth, but fork buildings were priced by era, not by what they give.
- **The Water Mill moves to the Classical era** (WM05, CORE; both prerequisites are Ancient CORE rows, and every row that needs it is Medieval or later). Base Civ V has it early, with The Wheel, and so does history: Vitruvius describes one, and the Perachora wheel dates from the 3rd century BCE. Classical Production had been the one era below base (0.84×); it is now 1.07×.
- **Fork buildings are priced by value on vanilla's scale** (§7, Cost scaling): the era's typical canonical cost × (value points ÷ 4), clamped between a quarter and one and a half times that cost, with upkeep at vanilla's rate for the era. 137 rows' specs change, in cost and upkeep only. UNIQUE replacements cost what their base costs (most already did), and the v3.77 spaceship stack keeps its tuned costs. Nine grid plants that had no cost, and so took their tech column's default, now carry explicit ones.
- **A cost-scaling table in §7**, applied by the ruleset derivation to canonical and fork objects alike. Buildings: ×0.80 in the Primitive, Ancient and Classical eras, ×0.94 Medieval, ×0.90 Early Modern, ×0.77 Modern, ×0.64 Atomic, ×0.50 Space and Interstellar — solved era by era, and shared by every building, so none is a bargain per point. Units, wonders and spaceship parts: the era's Production ratio (×1.00 to ×1.35), so they take base time. Spaceship parts taking the unit multiplier restores v3.77's intended ship timing, which compared costs with the spaceship bonuses alone, not the fork's higher general Production.
- **Result** (measured from the document): a full building set takes 1.07–1.08× base time at the end of each era from the Classical to the Atomic, and 1.17× in the Space era. The Ancient era stays at 1.74×, the accepted timing shift of seven Classical buildings onto historically Ancient rows, gone by the end of the Classical era. Net Gold after upkeep is now within a few Gold of base through the Early Modern era (−42 against −42) and closer after (−87 against −66 by Space, from −102), so gross Gold needs no trimming.
- **Judgement calls to revisit:**
  - *The Space-era building value is a floor.* How much a city can build there depends on how many turns the era lasts, which the Insight-threshold simulation will measure; the table makes it one number to retune.
  - *The value model is deliberately crude:* it counts every non-yield unique as one point, whatever its strength. A building whose unique is unusually strong or weak may need its price set by hand.
- **Battery check 16:** every fork building carries an explicit cost, and the cost-scaling table covers every era. Run against v3.89 it finds the nine costless plants and the missing table.

### v3.89 — Wonder-Only Rows (Power Rebalance, Stage 4)

- **The problem.** A world wonder is first-come: one civilisation builds it, and every other civilisation's copy of the breakthrough gave nothing. Eleven rows granted only a wonder, against the rule that every breakthrough carries a tangible benefit (v3.84). Each now also carries one that every civilisation receives, chosen outside the budgeted yields where possible, or in Gold, which still runs below base net of upkeep:
  - *Megalithic Construction:* −25% Culture cost of border growth — the stones mark whose land this is (archaeologists read megaliths as territorial markers).
  - *Flying Buttress:* +15% Production towards Universities — the masons who raised the Gothic cathedrals raised the colleges at Paris, Oxford and Cambridge. A cheaper Faith purchase of Cathedrals was the first thought, but only a religion holding the Cathedral belief can buy them.
  - *Philosopher's Stone* and its mirror *Great Harmony:* Great Merchants and Great Sages 15% faster — each echoes its wonder's own Great Person points.
  - *Elixir of Life:* +10% Great Person generation — long-lived masters.
  - *Zero-Point Energy* and its mirror the *Transcendent Engine:* +1 Production from every Power Plant, a tenth of what each wonder gives. Unciv's building filter matches tag uniques at runtime (`Building.matchesFilter` → `hasTagUnique`), so the slot tag works as a filter.
  - *Space Station:* +2 Gold from every Spaceship Factory — orbit pays. (The ruleset has no Airport for the tourism the wonder describes.)
  - *Space Elevator:* +25% Production towards launch-complex buildings — infrastructure, not spaceship parts, so v3.77's one-bonus-per-job rule holds.
