  - *O'Neill Cylinder:* Great Engineers 10% faster.
  - *Dyson Swarm:* +1 Production from Solar Plants.
- **Budgets.** Space-era Production moves from 1.32× to 1.34× base; the new flat bonuses sit on RARE and UNCOMMON rows, which count at a tenth and four tenths in a typical city. Science and Culture are unchanged, and net Gold stays below base in every era.
- **Battery check 15:** no row's only grant is a world wonder. Run against v3.88 it names exactly these eleven rows.

### v3.88 — Safe Predecessors: the Requirement Audit

- **The rule.** A required building must be a *safe predecessor*: one whose row is certain to come first (every OR branch of the dependent's prerequisites leads through it), or a CORE row of the same or an earlier era with no terrain condition the dependent lacks. CORE rows reveal themselves once eligible (v3.58), so they cannot hold a building back for long. Resource conditions do not count against safety, since trade satisfies them (§1.2). Anything else is a soft gate: a building whose breakthrough is known but which cannot be built until some unrelated discovery happens, or never. New battery check 14 applies the rule to every requirement, the fork's own and the canonical ones as the fork sets them. Run against v3.87, it fails and names exactly the eight broken chains below.
- **Eight canonical chains were broken.** The v3.83 middle-era rows moved canonical buildings onto STANDARD rows that are not ancestors of the rows needing them. The Opera House's row could be discovered without the Amphitheater's, leaving the Opera House unbuildable, and the Military Base's CORE row depended on the Arsenal's STANDARD one.
  - *The military chain keeps vanilla's order.* Concentric Fortification (the Castle) and Plate Armour (the Armory) become CORE, on the precedent v3.83 set for Public Education and Crank and Connecting Rod; both already had only CORE prerequisites. The Military Base moves off Precision Machining, where it was early anyway, onto a new row: **CO26 Ring Fortress** (Modern, STANDARD; Star Fort + Reinforced Concrete, neither of which needs gunpowder). Rifled shells made the bastion obsolete, and high explosive then did the same to masonry, so the forts were rebuilt as rings of detached works in concrete and steel — Liège, Verdun. The Bunker moves there with it and requires the Military Base.
  - *The culture and happiness lines take a first-rung rule.* Vanilla chains these rung by rung for pacing, not realism: a museum does not need an opera house. Uncoupling the rungs entirely would let a city buy only the most efficient building — the Stadium gives +4 Happiness for 2 upkeep, against +5 for 3 from the Colosseum and Theatre below it. That matters most for wide and conquering play, the case happiness exists to brake. So a top rung requires its line's *first* rung, which is cheap and always reachable: the Opera House, Ceilidh Hall and Museum require a Monument, and the Stadium a Colosseum. The Broadcast Tower needs nothing, since its +33% Culture multiplies what the city already has. These are derivation overrides of canonical data, recorded in a new table in §7, Buildings.
- **Nineteen new requirements,** each a building further along the same institution: Surgery ← Herbalist; Medical School ← Library; Experimental Laboratory ← University; Nuclear Physics Lab, its elemental mirror the Deep Matter Lab, the AI Lab and its alternative the Perceptron Laboratory ← Research Lab; Trading Company and Commodity Exchange ← Bank; Drydock ← Harbor; Automated Workshop ← Workshop; Textile Mill ← Weaver; Fab ← Microchip Foundry; Distillery Fuel Depot ← Distillery; the four Temperament Halls ← Balancing Clinic. *Rejected:* Medical School ← Surgery, Commodity Exchange ← Stock Exchange, Telegraph Office ← Post Office and Telephone Exchange ← Telegraph Office (unsafe — predecessors on STANDARD rows that are not ancestors); Veterinary College ← Stable (the Stable needs grassland); Resonant Crystal Works and Neuromorphic Foundry ← Microchip Foundry (both exist to bypass silicon).
- **Two Stage 3a misses:** the Anatomy Theatre and Logic School, bare Scientist-slot shells, are cut. Comparative Anatomy keeps its Medicine unlock and Formal Logic its City-State influence.
- **The Melancholic Hall was empty.** Stage 1 took its Science, and Stage 3 skipped slot members. It now gives +25% Great Person generation in its city: the melancholic was the temperament of genius (the Aristotelian *Problemata* asks why all who became eminent in philosophy, politics or the arts were melancholics). A scan of every fork object found no other empty building.
- Tallies: 550 breakthroughs; CORE 150, STANDARD 248.

### v3.87 — Required Buildings

- **The mechanic.** Unciv's `requiredBuilding` field makes a building need another in the same city, and the check accepts the named building or any building that replaces it — so a Qanat satisfies a requirement for an Aqueduct. The field had gone unused only because the notation had no token for it. It now has one: `requires a **X** in this city` inside a spec (§7, Notation). X must name a real building, never a slot tag: the engine's rejection message looks the name up, and fails if it is not a building.
- **The rule.** A fork building that takes a canonical building's place carries that building's requirement — which is how vanilla builds its own chains (Library → University, Aqueduct → Hospital → Medical Lab, Factory → power plants). Thirty requirements follow:
  - *The Power Plant slot:* all nineteen fork plants require a **Factory**, as the canonical Nuclear and Solar Plants do. Before this a city could raise a Coal Plant without a Factory, but not a Nuclear Plant.
  - *Replacements copy their base's requirement,* as the Wat copies the University's Library: the Bard Council and Royal Court (replacing the Amphitheater) require a **Monument**; the Swordsmith (the Armory) requires **Barracks**; the Bronze Foundry Temple and River Temple (the Temple) require a **Shrine**.
  - *Early versions copy their successor's:* the Engine House and Steam Works, which convert on towards the Factory, require a **Workshop**; the Tide Computer, which converts into the Seaport, requires a **Harbor**.
  - *An alternative that removes a building copies the removed building's:* the Phage Institute, which takes the Medical Lab's place, requires a **Hospital**.
  - *A paradigm's stand-in copies the one it stands in for:* Sanitation Works (the Miasma world's Hospital) requires an **Aqueduct** — replacing the v3.86 stopgap, under which it needed only the Aqueduct breakthrough — and the Pharmacopoeia (the Humoral answer to the Medical Lab) requires a **Balancing Clinic**.
- **Battery check 13.** Every required building must be a real building granted somewhere, arrive no later than the building that needs it, and exist in the same worlds. A row's world is stated in its text or inherited from its hard prerequisites. Every replacement must carry its base's requirement, and every Power Plant slot member a Factory. All thirty pass. Tested against a copy with three injected faults — a misspelt building, a replacement stripped of its requirement, and a Miasma building made to need a Humoral one — it catches all three.
- The requirements do not move the power ledger, which measures a typical city that already holds every building; they change what a city must build first, which Stage 5's build-time tuning inherits.

### v3.86 — Growth and Healing by Disease Paradigm (Power Rebalance, Stage 3b)

- **The measure.** Growth percentages, food carried over after growth and flat Food all set how fast a city grows: in proportion to (surplus + Food) × (1 + growth) ÷ (1 − carry-over). Unciv sums carry-over (capped at 95%) and applies growth to the surplus. Converting each effect into the flat Food that would give the same speed puts it on the Food ledger. The typical surplus is an estimate — 3 Food per city in the Ancient era rising to 8 by the Space era — and belongs with the dials to verify in playtesting.
- **The aim.** Only one disease paradigm is true in any game, so the paradigms need not match each other exactly; each must sit near base Civ V on its own, and each should feel different to play. Before this pass Germ worlds ran about +15 growth-equivalent Food over base from the Atomic era (+55% growth, and the Kiln's carry-over stacking on the canonical Medical Lab's), while Miasma and Humoral worlds ran about −5 from the Modern era (no Hospital or Medical Lab, and nothing in their place).
- **Shared by every world.** The Kiln goes: its 10% carry-over stacked with the Aqueduct and Medical Lab for up to +40% growth speed. Gene Therapy keeps +25% Great Person generation but not its +10% growth. The Quarantine Station and Vaccination stay — empirical medicine works whichever theory is true.
- **Germ — the universal cure:** late, empire-wide, carried by the army. The canonical Hospital and Medical Lab; Germ Theory's growth falls from 15% to 10%; Antibiotics keeps the Medic promotion for every new unit but not its +10% growth. Behind base at the Modern era (−2.7), ahead from the Atomic (+3.6, +3.9): germ theory, then the antibiotic age. *Phage route:* the Phage Institute gains 15% carry-over. It replaces the Medical Lab, whose carry-over the Institute's +15% growth could not match, so the route was not a real trade; Engineered Phages now bring it close by the Space era (+1.5, +3.2).
- **Miasma — public works:** early, built and dug. Sanitation Works becomes the Hospital and Medical Lab in one (+3 Food, 25% carry-over — stores kept sweet in clean air), an era before the Germ world's Medical Lab; drained fens and miasma bombardment stay. Level with base from the Modern era on (0.0, +0.2, +0.4). Unciv expresses a required building as a field, not a unique, so Sanitation Works requires the Aqueduct breakthrough rather than an Aqueduct in the city.
- **Humoral — the physician's art:** personal, healing, choice. A little less Food (−1.6, −1.1, −0.8), paid for in healing and the Temperament Halls: the Balancing Clinic adds +2 Food and keeps the strongest city healing of any world; the Pharmacopoeia adds 20% carry-over.
- **Healing.** Unciv sums every city-healing bonus on top of its base 25 HP in a city, so the old stack of +55 next to a Germ city healed 80 of a unit's 100 HP each turn. Now one shared ladder: Herbalist +5, Surgery +10, Medical School +5 (+20 by the Classical era). The Surgical Theatre goes; Anaesthesia instead gives every unit +5 HP whenever it heals, anywhere. The Balancing Clinic adds +15 in Humoral worlds; the Essence Clinic's healing falls from +20 to +10 in Vitalist worlds.
- **Kiln Construction** now carries ⟨[+25]% City Strength from defensive buildings⟩ — walls of fired brick — and says what kiln heat leads to: Glassmaking, Cast Iron, Portland Cement and Geopolymer Concrete.
- **Mud Brick is sun-dried.** Mud Brick Construction (CO01) now follows Seed Saving (G02), not Kiln Construction: the first farmers build the first houses, with straw from the first harvests, and a Granary for their seed. The prerequisite on six rows now reads "F02 (Kiln Construction)", since no Kiln building remains.
- **Anaesthesia** (MD07), formerly Anesthesia: British spelling.

### v3.85 — Food, Culture and Happiness (Power Rebalance, Stage 3a)

- **The budget.** Canonical buildings already supply base Civ V's Food, Culture and Happiness in every era; fork-made buildings added another 1 to 2.5 times base on top, for totals of 2 to 4.8 times. Generic flat Food, Culture and Happiness now come off fork-made buildings. Situational effects stay: a bonus tied to a resource, a terrain or the coast, unit healing, espionage, trade routes. Landmarks kept: the Forager's Hut (+1 Food, Primitive), the Fireworks Ground (+1 Culture, +1 Happiness, Medieval; also a Saltpeter consumer), the Pharmaceutical Works (+2 Happiness, Modern), the Photography Studio (+2 Culture, Modern) and the Hydroponics Lab (+2 Food, Modern).
- **Consolidation.** A building left with only a little Gold, a specialist slot or nothing is removed — 35 in all: the Music Hall, Insect Farm, Vision Lodge, Stonecutter, Tavern, Apiary, Gallery, Counting House, Paper Mill, Glassworks, Calendar Stone, Laboratory, Cast Iron Works, Clock Tower, Printing House, Seed Drill Works, Leyden Hall, Electromagnetics Institute, Actuary's Office, Cannery, Gasworks, Tramway, TV Station, Garment Factory, Weather Bureau, Radiology Laboratory, Radiotherapy Ward and its elemental mirror the Boundary Laboratory, Computer Centre, Telecom Exchange, Biome Dome, Biosphere, Bio-Recycler, Vertical Farm and Climate Control. A row left without a benefit now carries one that adds no entry to the construction list:
  - *Great Person rates:* Musical Instruments (Prophet), Artistic Tradition (Artist), Clockwork (Engineer), Electromagnetism (Scientist), each 10% faster.
  - *Gold:* from Silk (Insect Cultivation), Wine (Fermentation), Citrus (Apiculture), Iron (Cast Iron), Salt (Alchemy) and Uranium tiles (Radioactivity, and its elemental mirror Boundary Survey); from Markets (Zero and Place Value), Banks (Probability Theory), Harbors (Atmospheric Science), Broadcast Towers (Television, +2), Stock Exchanges (Fiber Optics) and Hydroponics Labs (Vertical Farming).
  - *Faster improvements (25%):* Quarries (Grinding & Polishing), Farms (Mechanical Seed Drill), Railroads (Electric Motor), Lumber Mills (Wood Gas Production), all improvements (Planetary Engineering Theory).
  - *Construction (+15%):* Libraries (Paper Making), Lighthouses (Glassmaking), Stadiums (Geodesic Structures), Land units (Synthetic Fibers).
  - *Other:* Faith from Incense (Psychoactive Plant Knowledge), Culture from Museums (Static Electricity), Submarine strength (Life Support Systems), 10% lower building maintenance (Closed-Loop Ecology).
- **Families of the same effect.** *Food carried over after growth:* only the Kiln keeps it (10%). *Farm Food:* canonical Civil Service and Fertilizer carry it alone. *Enclosed agriculture:* the Hydroponics Lab is the one building; the Greenhouse and Desalination Plant keep only their terrain and coast bonuses.
- **Kept although thin:** the Weaver, the base building that two UNIQUE buildings replace (Silk Weavers, Tapa Workshop).
- **Direct-Current Stations** no longer fold +1 Happiness onto the Tramway, which is gone; the "trams ran on DC" point moves to the Electric Motor as faster Railroads.
- **Result** (typical city, fork ÷ base): Food 1.05–1.38× from the Classical era on; Culture 1.0–1.33× (Medieval 1.59×, mostly canonical); Happiness 1.02–1.37×. The Primitive and Ancient eras run higher on very small bases, and the Ancient surplus is canonical Classical buildings sitting on Ancient rows. Science and Production are unchanged. Gross Gold is 3.8–5.6× base, but net of upkeep a fork city still runs below base (−109 against −66 by the Space era).
- **Left for Stage 3b.** The ledger does not count growth, carry-over or healing percentages, and across the four disease paradigms they do not add up to the same: Germ worlds reach about +35% growth (Quarantine Station, Germ Theory, Antibiotics), Miasma worlds +20% and Humoral worlds +25%. The buildings whose only remaining effect is growth or unit healing stay until the ladders are decided.
- **For Stage 5.** When upkeep falls with building cost, net Gold rises; gross Gold must be re-checked then.

### v3.84 — Every Breakthrough Carries a Benefit

- **The rule.** Every breakthrough carries a tangible benefit. Frontier breakthroughs may grant only an event and knowledge; opening a new focus counts as tangible, since the focus is visible and aimable. A breakthrough that only unlocks breakthroughs the player cannot see is not enough.
- **The audit** found 17 rows that failed it, most left empty by the power pass. Each now carries a benefit drawn from sources that do not break the power curve: Gold (net of upkeep, the fork runs below base Civ V), unit and military effects, map reveals, Great Person rates, knowledge events on the FTL path, and — in the Early Modern era, which has room — two folds restored at +1.
  - *Unit production:* Charcoal Production (Melee +25%), Turbine Design (Water +15%), Vacuum Tube (Air +15%), Sealed Environments (Submarines +25%).
  - *Folds:* Oxygen Theory (+1 Production from every Forge), Carnot Efficiency (+1 Production from every Engineer).
  - *Gold:* Voltaic Pile (+1 from Silver and Gold Ore tiles), Cryogenics (+1 from every Factory).
  - *Other:* Dam Construction (+1 Food from River tiles), Prosthetics (new Land units receive Medic), Nuclear Fission and its elemental mirror Bound-Energy Release (reveal Uranium within 20 tiles), Information Theory and Organic Neural Architecture (Great Scientists 10% faster), Quantum Mechanics (a report, and Great Engineers 10% faster).
  - *Reports on the FTL path:* Antimatter Production, Dark Matter Identification.
- **Production after the restored folds** runs at 1.32–1.33× base from the Modern era on — accepted, on the principle (now in §7.0) that production and costs are tuned together, with build time as the invariant.
- **Where costs stand.** A city's full building set still costs 2.4–5× base Civ V's, so building everything takes 2–3.8× as long. The remaining stages address it: stage 3 (Food, Culture and Happiness) consolidates the many remnant buildings the first two stages left behind; stage 4 handles the rows whose only grants are world wonders; stage 5 tunes building and unit costs to the production ratio, so build times match base.

### v3.83 — The Middle Eras Filled

- **21 new breakthroughs** for the thin Classical-to-Early-Modern stretch, each grounded by taking a canonical object that sat on a row that fitted it poorly — often an era early — so the whole set is power-neutral while moving power into its base-game era. Several reunite Civ V's own tech bundles.
  - *Classical:* Professional Army (Terracotta Army, Heroic Epic), Long-Distance Trade (Petra, Trading post), Historiography (National Epic), Assaying (Mint); and Siege Warfare moves here from the Ancient era, where its Catapult is canonically Classical.
  - *Medieval:* Stirrup (Knight), Concentric Fortification (Castle, Mughal Fort, Alhambra), Plate Armour (Longswordsman, Armory), Sternpost Rudder (Galleass), Double-Entry Bookkeeping (National Treasury), Scholastic Theology (Hagia Sophia, Great Mosque of Djenne, Grand Temple), Translation Movement (Oxford University), Tidal Mill (UNCOMMON: a mill for coastal cities without a river, never stacking with the Water Mill), Crank and Connecting Rod (Workshop, Longhouse).
  - *Early Modern:* Newtonian Mechanics (+1 Range for Siege units), Longitude (Seaport), Nation-State (Constabulary, Police Station), Public Education (Public School), The Enlightenment (Museum, The Louvre), Opera (Opera House, Ceilidh Hall, Theatre, Sistine Chapel), Star Fort (Arsenal, Kremlin), Drill and Discipline (Military Academy, Brandenburg Gate, Cavalry).
- **Two are CORE because Unciv's building chains require them:** Crank and Connecting Rod (the Factory requires the Workshop, and every power plant the Factory) and Public Education (the Research Lab requires the Public School).
- **No-gunpowder worlds:** Drill and Discipline needs no gunpowder, so the Cavalry — previously behind Rifling — is now available there, where §5 says cavalry stays relevant longest; Star Fort accepts the Trebuchet in place of Cannons.
- **Gearing** gives up the Workshop and gains ⟨[-25]% construction time for [Mine] improvements⟩ (geared hoists), so it carries a benefit of its own; the Pyramids' general speed-up stays unique to the Pyramids.
- **Row text and §7 reconciled.** The Knight, Pikeman and Theatre were assigned in §7 but named in no row; the Cavalry sat in Rifling's row while §7 placed it at Cavalry Tactics. Every moved object is now named in its row and reassigned in §7.
- **Power:** Ancient Production falls from 1.83× base to 1.17×, and Medieval from 1.40× to 1.29×; the Workshop and Seaport now arrive in their base-game eras. ~549 breakthroughs.

### v3.82 — The Late Plants Trade

- **The Nuclear Plant is the reference.** ES06 grants Unciv's own (+5 Production, +15%, upkeep 3, Uranium). Stage 2 had set every late plant to its output without checking their other terms, so three beat it outright: the Thorium Reactor (no Uranium, lower upkeep), the Micro-Reactor (half the cost, upkeep 1) and the elemental Quintessic Plant (no fuel, plus +2 with Uranium).
- **Atomic-era alternatives now trade.** Thorium Reactor +4 and +10% (no Uranium, lower upkeep); Micro-Reactor +3 and +10% (small, cheap); Quintessic Plant +3 and +10% on common matter, rising to exactly the Nuclear Plant's +5 and +15% when Uranium is held.
- **Fusion is a Space-era plant.** The Fusion Reactor (ES08) moves from Atomic to Space — commercial fusion is a 2000-onwards technology, and the Fusion Reactor was always the primary of a Competing Solution whose alternative, Advanced Fission, is Space-era. The physics (NP04, Nuclear Fusion) stays Atomic. Fusion Plant: +8 and +15%, no fuel, cost 1000, upkeep 4. Advanced Fission Plant: +6 and +15%, cost 750, upkeep 3, Uranium — cheaper to build and run, and the only such plant where fusion proves impossible. The elemental Great Work Reactor mirrors the Fusion Plant, and now shares its era.
- **The RARE Space-era upgrades match fusion's output** (+8, +15%): the Compact Fusion Plant and its elemental mirror the Compact Athanor keep their lower upkeep; the Cascade Reactor keeps its Uranium.

### v3.81 — Power Pass, Stage 2: Production (and Science, completed)

- **The ledger, corrected.** It now counts folds ("[+N Production] from all [X] buildings", once per city), reads specifications bracket-balanced (the grid plants' ⟨Remove [{Power Plant} {non-[…]}]⟩ clauses had truncated them), models conversions (a building stops counting once its successor is available), and weights RARE-chain follow-ups by their gateway's rarity. Measured this way, Production ran at 3–4.9× base Civ V, and Science after stage 1 at 1.33–1.69×.
- **Grid plants calibrated to Unciv's own.** Early Modern: +1. Modern fuel plants (coal, oil, the biofuels, geothermal, tidal, caloric): +2 and +5%. Late plants (fusion, thorium, micro-fission, and the elemental reactors): +5 and +15%, the Nuclear Plant's own figures. Space-era plants: +6 and +15%. One plant per city, so the slot never runs more than a step past base. Every plant's percentage is now a real unique rather than prose.
- **The Steam Works converts into the Factory** (the Factory's derivation clause removes it; the Steam Works speeds its own replacement), completing the chain Engine House → Steam Works → Factory.
- **Pairs trade evenly.** Perceptron Laboratory +2 Production against the AI Lab's +2 Science; Refinery +2 and +1 from Oil tiles; the Bio-Refinery +1, weaker than its primary but needing no Oil.
- **Generic industry stripped.** Production removed from 31 buildings; 15 left with nothing were removed (among them the Turbine Hall, Dam, Nanofab, Composites Factory, Casimir Generator and its elemental mirror the Vacuum Tap). About one fold per era survives at +1 (Laws of Thermodynamics and Caloric Theory on the Steam Works, the Calorimetry Hall on the Cycle Forge, Lens Grinding on Scientists — now the Medieval Science landmark in place of the Printing House); the rest are stripped.
- **Rows removed** for want of dependents or mechanism: Rubber Vulcanization, Supercritical Steam and the Medium Reservoir. The Dam no longer duplicates the Hydro Plant: Dam Construction is now a prerequisite only.
- **Result, per city from buildings:** Production 1.06–1.25× base from the Early Modern era on (percentage bonuses 1.0–1.26×); Science 1.0–1.26× in every era. The Ancient (1.83×) and Medieval (1.40×) Production excess is canonical placement — the Workshop and Seaport sit an era early — and is addressed by the middle-era rows. ~528 breakthroughs.

### v3.80 — Power Pass, Stage 1: Science

- **The measurement.** A per-city ledger compared every building a typical city could build, era by era, with base Civ V: flat and percentage yields, per-population and tile uniques, upkeep, exclusive groups counted once. The fork's canonical science matches base exactly; the excess — 3.8× base by the Medieval era, 6.5× by the Space era — came entirely from fork-made buildings, many of them a laboratory per field. Because Insight thresholds are calibrated to base science, this was the most consequential excess.
- **The rule.** Fork-made buildings no longer yield flat or percentage Science, except one small landmark per era: the Printing House (+1), Experimental Laboratory (+2), Nuclear Physics Lab and its elemental mirror the Deep Matter Lab (+2), and the AI Lab (+2, the science half of its Competing Solution with the Perceptron Laboratory) with the Computer Centre (+1). UNIQUE buildings and wonders are untouched for now.
- **What changed.** Science stripped from 45 buildings that keep other content; 30 buildings with nothing left removed. Two rows with no dependents and no content left are removed — Photonics (EN08) and Interstellar Communication (SE14) — by the rule that a row must justify its existence. Twelve rows now rest on their structural role alone: gateways for large chains (Quantum Mechanics, Nuclear Fission), FTL prerequisites, and Science Advisor reports.
- **Result.** Per-city Science from buildings measured 1.0–1.22× base Civ V — *but that measurement omitted folds* ("[+N Science] from all [X] buildings"); with folds it was 1.33–1.69×, corrected in v3.81. ~531 breakthroughs.
- **Stages to come:** Production; Food, Culture and Happiness; the rows whose only grants are world wonders.
- **Checkpoint.** v3.79 is the last version before the pass, preserved as `checkpoint_v3.79_pre-power-rebalance.md` with its starting measurements in `CHECKPOINTS.md`.

### v3.79 — Spin Gravity Removed

- **SE04 (Spin Gravity) is removed.** Centrifugal force was understood centuries before anyone needed spin gravity; making a habitat spin is engineering, part of what the Generation Ship and the O'Neill Cylinder cost, not a discovery. The principle: *a breakthrough is a discovery or a new capability, not the engineering of something already understood.*
- The Generation Ship now requires Closed-Loop Ecology (SE05) and Orbital Assembly (SE02), which SE04 had carried; the O'Neill Cylinder, Orbital Assembly and Closed-Loop Ecology. The Arcology stays in the gravitic chain (RC09). ~533 breakthroughs.

### v3.78 — Era Dates, and Spin Gravity

- **Non-overlapping eras.** The Modern era was defined as "Industrial — Information Age", roughly 1830–1990, overlapping the Atomic era's Nuclear Age. The eras now have dates: Early Modern to about 1840, Modern 1840–1945, Atomic 1945–2000, Space thereafter.
- **22 rows moved by date.** To Early Modern: Portland Cement, Carnot Efficiency, Electromagnetism, the Railway Network, Non-Euclidean Geometry, the Telegraph and Rubber Vulcanisation. To Atomic: Information Theory, Antibiotics, Titanium Working, Geodesic Structures, Supercritical Steam, Desalination, Systems Thinking, the Ekranoplan, Kevlar, Tidal Power, Fibre Optics, Fractal Mathematics, and Composite Materials and the Grand Aerostat (which require Atomic breakthroughs). To Space: Vertical Farming. Radar, the jet engine, the helicopter and television stay Modern, as pre-1945 inventions.
- **Result.** The Modern era falls from 102 to 80 familiar-world rows, and per-discovery prices now rise through the eras (Medieval 135, Early Modern 464, Modern 606, Atomic 776, Space 2,355) instead of dipping from Early Modern to Modern.
- **Three ordering inconsistencies fixed.** Composite Materials was labelled earlier than its prerequisite Carbon Fiber; Megalithic Construction (now Ancient) earlier than Quarrying; and Surgical Techniques earlier than Comparative Anatomy — it now rests on Herbal Medicine instead, since surgery predates anatomy by millennia.
- **Spin Gravity.** SE04 had become the Arcology, "structures engineered under controlled gravity" — which only Gravitic worlds have — yet it gated the Generation Ship in every world. It is now Spin Gravity (Centrifuge Laboratory), which works in any universe and still gates the Generation Ship and the O'Neill Cylinder; the Arcology moves to Inertial Dampening (RC09), in the gravitic chain.

### v3.77 — Spaceship Production Balance

- **The problem.** Twenty-five bonuses to spaceship parts all stacked, and Unciv adds percentage bonuses into one sum. A fully developed capital reached +368% (+493% with the RAREs and a gravitic world), building the 4,500-production ship for 760–960 (counting the spaceship bonuses alone), against base Civ V's +75% and 2,570.
- **One bonus per job.** *Fabrication* stays canonical (Spaceship Factory, Hubble). *Lift* is one launch complex in the capital — Space Port +25%, Mass Driver +33%, Gravitic Platform +50%, converting like power plants — or the Space Elevator +50%, which retires it and suspends every complex while held. *Assembly* is the Orbital Shipyard +25%. *Exotic components* (+15% each) speed only the FTL Drive. *Propulsion* is one capped bonus to Boosters: +10% per ⟨Propulsion⟩ breakthrough, up to three, so every drive contributes and the atomic and elemental worlds reach the same cap.
- **Capital-only launch infrastructure.** Every part must reach the capital to be launched, so lift and assembly are built only there, destroyed on capture, and help every city. The capital builds infrastructure while other cities build parts. As ordinary buildings they can be bought with Gold, which parts cannot — the lever for one-city play.
- **Removed:** the part bonuses of Carbon Fiber, Inertial Dampening, the Antimatter Lab and the Medium Cell. The Orbital Platform becomes orbital industry (500/2; +3 Production, +10% Production).
- **Result:** the best city reaches +125% (+150% with the Elevator or a gravitic world); the ship costs about 1,650–1,900 there.
- **Corrected:** §4 gave the ship's total as 5,250; it is 4,500, exactly base Civ V's.

### v3.76 — Exclusion by Replacement

- **The problem.** Shared slots were enforced by *blocking*: ⟨Only available <in cities without a [X]>⟩. Once a city built any grid plant it could never build another — a Generating Station barred the Fusion Plant for good — and the same lock-in held for every persisting Competing Solution pair, the Regimen Halls and the Ironworks.
- **The fix.** Each member of a shared slot now carries ⟨Remove [the others] [in this city]⟩, which fires on completion, so building the new one retires the old. A city still holds one at a time, keeps the old one working while the new one is built, and is never stuck. Applied to all 19 grid plants (plus the canonical Nuclear and Solar Plants), the five persisting pairs, the four Regimen Halls, the two Ironworks, and the reservoirs' bank-or-draw rule.
- **A latent bug removed.** The groups were built on `replaces` declarations pointing at placeholder buildings. Unciv's capture swap follows `replaces`, so capturing a city would have turned its power plant into the stat-less placeholder (and a Solar Reservoir into a Caloric Reservoir). Shared slots now use tag uniques, which Unciv matches as filters without any `replaces`; the Power Plant and Regimen Hall placeholders are gone.
- **A loophole closed.** The Caloric Reservoir had no exclusion of its own, so a desert city could build a Solar Reservoir and then a Caloric one, banking 10 heat a turn. Either now retires the other.
- **UNIQUE replacements convert.** A UNIQUE discovered mid-game now converts the holder's existing base buildings into its replacement, as Unciv does when a city changes hands, instead of letting the two stack.
- **Early versions convert too.** An audit of every new building against the canonical ones found one true early version: the Tide Computer carries the Seaport's own core unique, so the two stacked. It now speeds its city's Seaport and is retired by it, as the Engine House is by the Steam Works. The other overlaps are the ordinary stacking of shared effect types, as base Civ V's Barracks, Armory and Military Academy stack.
- **The principle** is stated in §7.0: exclusion by replacement, not by blocking; early versions convert; tags for shared slots; `replaces` only for true equivalents.

### v3.75 — Engine Houses Convert

- **The gap.** Once the Steam Engine arrived, existing Engine Houses kept running beside any Steam Works built in the same city, so the superseded alternative *added* to its primary; the only exit was selling it.
- **The fix, native to Unciv.** The Engine House carries ⟨[+100]% Production when constructing [Steam Works] buildings [in this city]⟩, and the Steam Works carries ⟨Remove [Engine House] [in this city]⟩, which fires on completion (`CityConstructions.addBuilding` → `triggerNewBuildingUniques`). Conversion reuses the house, beam and pit, as Newcomen engines were refitted with Watt's condenser.
- **The result.** Each city runs the old engine or the new, never both. Converting frees the Coal and gains the Steam Works' power but gives up the mine and mill yields — a real choice, not an automatic upgrade.

### v3.74 — An Early Steam Path

- **The problem.** The Aeolipile (MC07) opened the Thermodynamics focus centuries early, but every Thermodynamics breakthrough waited on the Steam Engine — and even with its prerequisites met, a Classical civilisation could never afford its Early Modern threshold. The focus opened onto an empty shelf.
- **The frame.** Steam does work three ways: as a jet, as a vacuum, and as pressure. Pressure steam needs precision machining, strong boilers and standardisation, so it cannot exist before the industrial era. Vacuum steam needs none of these — Newcomen's engine never exceeded about one atmosphere — so it can.
- **The Weight of the Air (TH13, STANDARD, Classical):** the Condensing Pump raises water for houses and fountains, and every Thermodynamics breakthrough costs ×0.9 for its holder.
- **Atmospheric Engine (TH14, STANDARD, Medieval):** the Engine House burns Coal to drain mines and, where no Water Mill stands, to run a mill away from the river. It is a superseded Competing Solution to the Steam Engine, and halves the Steam Engine's cost — a discount that moves here from the Aeolipile.
- **Deliberately excluded:** vehicles, ships, factory drive, turbines and the steam cannon — each needs precision or power the period cannot supply.
- MC07 → TH13, TH14 becomes the fourth RARE gateway chain; 18 Competing Solutions groups.

### v3.73 — Section 5 Brought Up to Date

- **Phlogiston remnants removed** from eight places — the Truth examples, the Foundational Questions' opening, the tokens, the exotic-matter route, Deep Reconciliation and the Frontier Questions — and replaced, where an example was needed, with caloric (Carnot built the heat engine's theory on it).
- **Stale content corrected:** the Vitalist world (Vital Essence is a building, and Organic Chemistry still stands, short of abiotic synthesis); TH11's name and caloric's non-tradeability; an unsupported claim that caloric opens fusion containment; the No-Fusion example rewritten from obsolescence into Competing Solutions and dead-ends, with fusion noted as moot in elemental worlds; the No-Gunpowder example notes Saltpeter's use for Nitrate Fertiliser.
- **Contradictions resolved:** "forks, not dead ends" now agrees with §1.2 (the losing theory dead-ends, the civilisation forks; only frontiers leave holes); the atomic spine is Nuclear and Quantum Physics with the silicon age, not all of Electronics and Computing.
- **The Enabling worlds restored.** Early Steam, Enhanced Springs and the Antikythera tradition were promised "below" but had been lost in an earlier restructure; they return as worked examples written against the current rows.
- **The Airship Disaster moves to §1.2**, beside Competing Solutions: it is a per-civilisation contingency, not a World Truth. Stale counts ("tens of thousands", "five permanent parallel games", "40 fork breakthroughs … 115 outcomes") corrected or removed.

### v3.72 — The Airship Disaster

- **A STANDARD airship chain** — Dirigible (AE02), the new **Rigid Airship** (AE13, the Zeppelin, on aluminium frames) and the Grand Aerostat (AE12, RARE → STANDARD) — with no obsolescence by aeroplanes: the two lines trade (speed and strength against sight, endurance and no oil).
- **The Airship Disaster (§5)**, a second kind of road not taken: lost confidence. Every gas airship carries a 1% per-turn chance of triggering an event that destroys it — native, since Unciv passes the triggering unit into the event (`Event.triggerChoice`, verified). A **confidence ladder** follows: the sufferer's undiscovered airship breakthroughs drop to RARE; each contacted major civilisation receives a report and drops one step (a first report to UNCOMMON, a second to RARE); confidence never recovers. The ladder and the broadcast are fork code.
- **Helium Extraction** (CH28, STANDARD, Modern) ends the risk and makes its holder deaf to reports, without restoring lost confidence; it also makes Cryogenics and Superconductivity ×0.8. Hot-air balloons never burn.

### v3.71 — Phlogiston Is a Model, Not a World

- **The Combustion axis (FQ8) is cut.** Working it through (Combustion Physics, Model III) showed that free phlogiston cannot be consistent — negative mass in contact with ordinary matter runs away — and that the consistent version is our own world described through missing oxygen: phlogiston is a hole in oxygen, as positive charge-carriers are holes in a semiconductor's electrons. Every weighing agrees, and nothing a layman, craftsman or early laboratory can do tells the two apart. **Principle recorded (§5):** a World Truth must be a fact that eventually makes different predictions, never a choice of description.
- **Removed:** Phlogiston Theory (F08), Phlogiston Extraction (F12), Levity Gas (F13), Levity Condensate (F14), the Phlogiston Furnace Plant (ES17), and Quantitative Combustion (CH18), whose only role was the fork; the COMBUSTION_THEORY token; the Grand Aerostat's tier override; the Levity routes into the airships. Oxygen Theory always validates. The exotic-matter frontier question remains, so FTL loses nothing.
- **What historically predates oxygen chemistry no longer waits for it:** the Steam Engine (Newcomen, 1712) and the Hot Air Balloon (Montgolfier, 1783) require the Scientific Method directly instead of Oxygen Theory. An audit of everything gated behind the Scientific Method in or before the Early Modern era found no other anachronism.
- World Truth Axes: 11 (4 Foundational + 7 Frontier). 530 breakthroughs.

### v3.70 — The Geology Is Data; Fuel Lodes by Either Route

- **The Removal Truth is expressed as geology:** Coal and Oil get `Doesn't generate naturally`, which the map generator honours, and Coal Discovery and Petroleum Discovery are absent because there is nothing to discover — the ordinary absence of §1.2, not a rule that works differently. Nothing needs revealing: the resource bar shows a strategic resource as soon as it is held, and neither building requirements nor trade consult revealing (verified in `WorldScreenTopBarResources`, `Building`, `TradeLogic`, `TradeEvaluation`).
- **Fuel lodes follow a universal rule:** a Transmuted Coal or Oil Lode is available once the civilisation knows the fuel by either route — Coal Discovery or the Charcoal Blast Furnace; Petroleum Discovery or the Alcohol Combustion Engine — written as `Only available <after discovering [...]>` with a De Morgan OR. Nothing changes in normal worlds, and in fossil-free worlds a Substance civilisation can re-temper matter into the seams its world never laid down.

### v3.69 — Turbary

- **Peat Cutting moves to its own breakthrough, Turbary (F16, STANDARD, Ancient), which needs a Marsh in the civilisation's territory** — a people with no bogs never learns to burn them. Flood Control (WM04) keeps Remove Marsh, the Polder and its flood-plains food, so bog-less civilisations lose nothing unrelated to peat. Named after the old common right to cut peat, and apart from its improvement, because the Charcoal Kiln and the Distillery both filter on `[Peat Cutting]`.

### v3.68 — Whisky Spends Its Peat

- A Peat Cutting that smokes malt cannot also feed a kiln: in a city with a Distillery, the Charcoal Kiln counts its fuel tiles minus `min(worked Peat Cuttings, 1)`, so the Distillery claims one bog, and only if there is one to claim. Expressed with the kiln's uniques split by `<in cities with a [Distillery]>` / `<in cities without a [Distillery]>`, since no city-local building count exists to subtract.

### v3.67 — Whisky

- **Whisky**, a new luxury made rather than mined (`Doesn't generate naturally`, as Jewelry and Porcelain are). The existing Distillery (CH04) provides one wherever its city works a Peat Cutting — malt dried over peat smoke — so the only new object is the resource itself. A bog now has three uses — drain it, cut it for Production and kiln fuel, or cut it for Whisky — and a reason to be kept.

### v3.66 — Fossil-Free Worlds Follow the Same Rules

- **No technology works differently in a fossil-free world.** v3.65's special cases are retired — the mill-powered Factory remap, the Ironclad on Iron, the Biofuel display name and fossil-free-only peat. The Factory and Ironclad need Coal, and tanks, battleships and aircraft need Oil, in every world; only the source of the fuel differs.
- **Grown fuel, priced to be outclassed:** the **Charcoal Kiln** (charcoal road) provides 1 Coal while its city works three fuel tiles — forest, jungle or Peat Cutting — at −10% Production, which cancels a Factory's percentage so that charcoal never scales; the **Distillery Fuel Depot** (alcohol road) provides 1 Oil while its city works three Farms, at −20% growth. The condition carries the consumption (unworked woods feed nothing; a cold kiln costs nothing) and the percentage carries the scale, so the costs stay meaningful across eras. Flat costs were rejected because they would have made charcoal relatively *cheaper* as industry grew — the reverse of why coal won.
- **Peat Cutting** moves to Flood Control (WM04), beside Remove Marsh — a bog can be drained or cut — as a +2 Production improvement in every world, never locked out; worked, it also fuels a kiln. Jungle and Marsh yield no Production, and nothing floors tile yields, so the kiln's cost sits on the building rather than on its fuel tiles.
- **The Landship and Tank follow either engine** (`<after discovering [...]>` with a De Morgan OR, verified to match against every researched technology); the Ethanol Tank is removed.

### v3.65 — The Fossil-Fuel-Absent World, Completed

- **A geological model (§5):** lignin-digesting fungi evolved before the great forests, so no coal formed; no plankton was buried, so no oil. Everything else a layman sees is unchanged, and peat still forms in waterlogged bogs.
- **Every canonical Coal and Oil gate now has an answer.** The two Coal consumers get apt replacements: the **Factory** runs on a Water Mill, Windmill or grid plant (a De Morgan OR in one conditional), the mill-powered factory of our own 1770s; the **Ironclad** needs Iron, burning wood. The seven Oil consumers need no substitutes, because **Oil is grown**: the Distillery Fuel Depot provides it (shown as Biofuel), so tanks, battleships and aircraft keep working, paid for in farm food. The Landship and Tank answer to the Alcohol Combustion Engine; the Ethanol Tank is removed as redundant.
- **Peat Cutting** (Marsh, +2 Production) arrives with the Charcoal Blast Furnace in these worlds.
- **No start-position lottery:** every city has a mill or plant route to the Factory, fuel comes from farms, and the only forest-bound object is one national wonder. The §5 text is rewritten in the language of Competing Solutions.

### v3.64 — Alternatives Trade, Never Simply Add; Phages and Perceptrons

- **Principle (§1.2):** a persisting alternative carries real disadvantages as well as advantages, and competes for its primary's slot — a mutually exclusive building, the same token, a bonus that does not stack — so holding both gives a choice in each city, not an accumulation. The order a civilisation found things changes its options, never its total.
- **Six existing alternatives were adding rather than trading, and now trade:** the Charcoal Ironworks replaces the Ironworks (one of either kind); the Bio-Refinery and Refinery are mutually exclusive; the Stirling Works and the DC Generating Station become cheap, weak, fuel-free grid plants — so a connected city with a DC station gives up AC's free grid power; the Distillery Fuel Depot's farm production now costs farm food; and Analog Computing, whose siege bonus stacked with the digital computer's, is *superseded* — its own bonuses end when the Programmable Computer arrives, and what persists is the road it opens.
- **Phage therapy (MD20–21, germ worlds):** the alternative to Antibiotics — no empire-wide growth, only a per-city Phage Institute competing with the Medical Lab; Engineered Phages later double the Institutes' worth. **The perceptron road (CP14–15):** the alternative to Narrow AI — Perceptron Laboratories give production and cheap upkeep for less Science, competing with AI Labs; Neuromorphic Processors, competing with Fabs, meet the computing threshold for general intelligence with no Rare Earths.
- 535 breakthroughs; 17 Competing Solutions groups (12 persist, 5 are superseded).

### v3.63 — Edison and Tesla Are Engineers

- The War of the Currents turns on Great **Engineers**, not Scientists. Both names are already in Unciv's canonical Engineer name group, so the easter egg needs no new data — only the record of received names and the check in the discovery roll.

### v3.62 — The War of the Currents

- **Electrification becomes a Competing Solution.** The primary, the Power Grid (EL07), is AC: central generation and transmission, now also giving +3 Production to connected cities that have no plant of their own — and fragile, since a cut road or blockaded harbour drops every city behind it off the grid. The alternative, **Direct-Current Stations** (EL14), is DC: a cheap Generating Station in any city, connected or not, stronger once Electrochemistry is known, with better trams; it persists if AC arrives later. A continental power wants AC and an archipelago wants DC, and the order a civilisation finds them can commit it to a style of industry.
- **Easter egg:** Thomas Edison and Nikola Tesla join the Scientist names. Unciv never reuses a name within a game, so at most one civilisation receives each. Having had Edison makes DC win the race (the grid waits for it); having had Tesla locks DC out. The fork records received Great Person names — one line where Unciv assigns them — and the discovery roll reads the record.
- **Filter validator tightened:** its city-filter vocabulary is now exactly the documented list. One hand-added phrase ("in non-capital cities") was not a real filter; the document never used it, so nothing was hidden.

### v3.61 — Competing Solutions Replace UNCOMMON Obsolescence

- **The road less travelled is pre-empted, not hard.** Obsolescence is no longer tied to UNCOMMON. A *Competing Solutions* group (§1.2) has one goal, a primary and one or more alternatives: alternatives carry a normal tier (STANDARD), a ×1.5 detour margin tunes the race so the primary usually comes first, and lock-out runs one way. If the primary arrives after the alternative, the alternative is either **superseded** (native `Unavailable <after discovering [X]>`, `obsoleteTech` and `<before discovering [X]>`) or **persists**, when it keeps a lasting advantage — the deep fission chain is the model case. Where the primary cannot exist in a world, the alternative loses its margin and takes the primary's tier. UNCOMMON now means only "moderately hard".
- **14 groups** (§6): the ten former pairs, all now STANDARD — the fossil-fuel alternatives persist, since independence from Coal or Oil is a lasting advantage, so the industrial era of a fossil-free world no longer sits behind UNCOMMON rolls; Corbel Vault, Chariots and the Canal Network are superseded. Reframed as alternatives: the **Thorium Reactor** (to the uranium plant), the **Stirling Engine** (to internal combustion) and **Analog Computing** (to the digital computer), all persisting. New: **Nitrate Fertiliser** (FA16), the alternative to the Haber process — the same farm bonus while you hold Saltpeter, which the musket also wants — superseded when Catalysis arrives.
- **Advanced Fuel Cycles now requires the Breeder Reactor plus the Thorium Reactor *or* the uranium plant.** With thorium usually pre-empted, the old prerequisite would have closed the deep fission chain to most civilisations — including in the fusion-impossible worlds where it is meant to be the main road.
- Tiers now: {'CORE': 147, 'STANDARD': 227, 'UNCOMMON': 61, 'RARE': 41, 'UNIQUE': 54}; 530 breakthroughs.

### v3.60 — Filter Validation; World-Truth Language Made Accurate

- **World-Truth language.** §1.2 and §6 claimed that only Removal Truths make breakthroughs absent. They now give the true account: a breakthrough is absent in two ways, both fixed by the ruleset derivation — a Removal Truth deletes it, or a foundational axis leaves no room for it (an elemental world has no Nuclear Physics, a germ world no Humoral Medicine) — and a third group is present but may dead-end (rival theories a decisive experiment settles; frontier breakthroughs such as Fusion in an FQ2-Impossible world). A dead-ended breakthrough obsoletes nothing. The obsolescence note and two rows that still used the retired FUSION_ABSENT and "World Flag" vocabulary were brought into line.
- **Filter-value validation added to the battery.** Every bracketed value is matched to its parameter type and checked against the ruleset's names, the parameter docs' keywords and the document's own objects. It found four real problems, all fixed: Supercritical Steam boosted `[Coal Power Plant]` (the breakthrough's name — it would only have worked through the filter's tech-name match) rather than the Coal Plant; the four humoral halls were excluded with `[Regimen Hall]`, which matched no building (now four named halls replacing a Regimen Hall placeholder, as grid plants replace the Power Plant); the eight Transmuted Lodes and the Five Turnings were used by name but never declared as objects.

### v3.59 — Tier Review: CORE Means Visible and Aimable

- **CORE reassigned by seven rules** (§1.2), now that CORE breakthroughs reveal themselves: focus unlocks, strategic-resource reveals, the buildings and improvements every Civ V city builds, victory paths and the seas, the defensive backbone, the decisive experiments — and closure, so that no CORE ever waits behind a roll that may not fire. CORE 117 → 147 (47 promoted, 17 demoted); STANDARD → 213.
- **Promoted (47):** Horse Domestication, Alchemy, Distillation, Organic Chemistry, Polymer Science, Quantitative Combustion, Systematic Element Analysis, Organic Synthesis Attempt, Iron-Frame Construction, Monumental Architecture, Charcoal Production, Advanced Pyrotechnics, Herbal Medicine, Botanical Classification, Trigonometry, Non-Euclidean Geometry, Set Theory / Formal Logic, Topology, Clockwork, Precision Machining, Contagion Experiments, Bessemer Process, Aluminium Extraction, Sulfur Mining, Petroleum Discovery, Saltpeter Mining, Uranium Discovery, Rare Earth Extraction, Celestial Mathematics, Comparative Anatomy, Geological Observation, Laser, Spectroscopy, Epistemology, Information Theory, Resonant-State Computing, Boundary Survey, Quantum Electrodynamics, Satellite Technology, Banking, Heat Exchanger, Mechanical Equivalent of Heat, Carbon Fiber, Gunpowder, Rifling, Drone Warfare, Aqueduct. These include every chokepoint the design review flagged — Charcoal Production, Celestial Mathematics, Comparative Anatomy, Epistemology — and all five decisive experiments.
- **Demoted (17):** Jet Engine, Dog Domestication, Reinforced Concrete, Artificial Intelligence (Narrow), Electric Motor, Power Grid, Radio, Selective Breeding (Plants), Mendelian Genetics, DNA Structure, Genetic Engineering, Bronze Alloy, Life Support Systems, Orbital Assembly, Radiation Shielding, Orbital Resource Delivery, Barter System. Important, but nothing the familiar game needs in order to function; their high STANDARD ceilings keep them near-certain, but not visible in advance.
- The Stock Exchange was left off the essential list, since promoting it would have pulled the whole finance chain into CORE by closure. Existing CORE nodes on the FTL path were left as they were.

### v3.58 — Philosophy and the Arts; CORE Breakthroughs Reveal Themselves

- **Philosophy and Religion & Culture merged into Philosophy and the Arts** — the institutions of mind and spirit: faith, learning and art. Separately they were the two thinnest early foci (one breakthrough per era, and nothing at all before the Classical or in the Medieval era respectively), and the Temple and University sat in the one a player was least likely to fund for them. Merged, the focus offers non-UNIQUE breakthroughs in every era from Primitive to Modern (1, 2, 3, 1, 4, 4), opens in the Primitive era, and keeps its PH and CU IDs, so no prerequisite changes. Natural Philosophy no longer unlocks a focus; the Isolated-start modifier and the Vision Lodge's Insight point at the merged focus.
- **CORE breakthroughs are Revealed automatically once eligible** (§1.4) — the furthest reach the One-Step Horizon allows. Players can aim for what they know they need, while the roll still decides when it arrives. Because CORE now means visible and aimable, a tier review follows.

### v3.57 — Mechanical Grounding, Batch 8 (Materials, Genetics, Energy, Culture) — Grounding Complete

- **50 breakthroughs grounded; the grounding pass is complete.** Every Unlocks cell now names Unciv objects, real unique templates, or one of the fork's enumerated extensions.
- **One grid plant per city.** Fifteen grid plants join a placeholder **Power Plant** through `replaces`, so one clause, `<in cities without a [Power Plant]>`, makes them mutually exclusive — the canonical Nuclear/Solar exclusion generalised. The Fusion Plant takes the Great Work Reactor's stats; the Quintessic Plant mirrors the Nuclear Plant, fuel-free, with a bonus when Uranium is held. Earlier-grounded reactors (Thorium, Micro, Cascade, Compact Fusion, Compact Athanor, Great Work) join the rule.
- **§7 era-stacking fixed for culture:** Hagia Sophia, the Great Mosque and the Grand Temple move from Ancient to Medieval (University); the Opera House, Sistine Chapel, Ceilidh Hall, Museum, Louvre and Hermitage from Ancient/Classical to the Renaissance.
- **Vital Essence follows the resource discipline** (as Phlogiston did): its consumers require an Essence Reservoir in the city. A stockpile treatment, as Caloric received, is a redesign-phase candidate.
- **Found while grounding — UNIQUE replacements did not replace.** Nation unique buildings block their base through the civilisation's `uniqueBuildings` cache, built from `uniqueTo`; breakthrough-granted replacements have no `uniqueTo`, so the base building would have stayed buildable beside them and stacked. The fork adds UNIQUE-granted replacements to the holder's cache on discovery (an eighth fork extension, §7.0). The double-replacement case (a nation UB and a UNIQUE UB on the same base) is left for redesign.

### v3.56 — Grand Aerostat in Levity Worlds; Arcologies

- **The Grand Aerostat (AE12) is STANDARD in Phlogiston worlds** — the first World-Truth tier override (§7.0, derivation step 5). Levity gas is the gateway there, so its apex follows the RARE-gateway rule; elsewhere it stays RARE.
- **Artificial Gravity (SE04) grants the Arcology** (+25% growth in its city) in place of the Medical Centrifuge's happiness: structures engineered under controlled gravity climb taller and hold more people on the same ground.

### v3.55 — FTL Is the Science Victory; the Generation Ship Is the Challenge

- **The Scientific Victory is the FTL ship**, on the canonical space race: the Apollo Program, SS Booster ×3, SS Cockpit and a new **SS FTL Drive** in place of the SS Engine, its requirements written by the ruleset derivation for the one viable method. About 5,250 production, as in base Civ V, so the Spaceship Factory and Hubble keep their meaning. The SS Engine and SS Stasis Chamber are removed; the FTL Probe wonder of v3.54 is superseded.
- **Sub-light cut to one challenge victory.** The Cryogenic Ship (SE12) and Von Neumann Probe (SE10) are removed (529 breakthroughs). The **Generation Ship** (SE11) is a national wonder that pauses growth like a Settler, costs a citizen on completion (`[-1] population [in this city]`), and provides one **Voyage** a turn; the victory is `Have at least [150] [Voyage]`. Correction to v3.54: "launch, then survive" *is* expressible — the stockpile is the clock, since the resource countable returns a stockpile's amount, and capture destroys the national wonder and stops it. Verified in `Victory.kt`, `Countables.kt`, `Civilization.getResourceAmount`, `CityConquestFunctions` and `UniqueTriggerActivation`.
- Note on the brief: in Civ V and Unciv the Settler does not cost population — it pauses growth and needs size 2. The Generation Ship takes both of those and adds the population cost separately.

### v3.54 — Mechanical Grounding, Batch 7 (Rocketry, Aeronautics, Space Engineering); Victories Made Buildable

- **42 breakthroughs grounded.** Canonical objects land on their canonical eras (Rocket Artillery and Mobile SAM at Solid Fuel Rocket, Apollo at Orbital Mechanics, Hubble and the SS Cockpit with Satellites' own `Reveals the entire map`; Fighter, Bomber and Paratrooper moved to Radar as in the canonical tree). Probes, probe range, launch costs, asteroid events and aerial bases — none of which Unciv has — are converted or dropped.
- **§4's victories rebuilt on `VictoryTypes.json`.** "Launch and survive N turns" has no mechanism, so every space victory is a construction race: the canonical Scientific Victory is the Cryogenic Ship, unchanged; the Generation Ship (two more parts, no cryogenics) and the Von Neumann Probe (the cheapest ship, behind a RARE design) are alternative part sets; the Interstellar Victory builds an **FTL Probe** whose requirements the ruleset derivation writes as `Researched [X] Technologies` conditionals for the one viable method. The SS Stasis Chamber moves to the Cryogenic Ship breakthrough, now reached through Cryogenics rather than the UNCOMMON Cryogenic Preservation, so the familiar victory never waits on an uncommon roll.
- **Tuning watch-item:** spaceship-part production bonuses now stack widely — the canonical Spaceship Factory (+50%) and Hubble (+25%), four more one-city buildings of +25–50%, and some seven civilisation-wide +10–25% breakthroughs. A fully stacked capital could build a 750-production part for under 200. Most of these should become non-production effects in the balance pass.

### v3.53 — Nothing Renamed for Flavour

- **Naming principle (§7.0):** things are named for what they observably are or do, or for historical reasons that hold in any world; a world-dependent name is used only where the familiar name asserts a theory false in that world (*atomic*, *nuclear*, *semiconductor*), and then it is the name that world's own science would give. Elemental science is as rigorous as atomic science.
- **Applied:** Uranium stays Uranium in every world (it is named for the planet Uranus) — "Heavy Ore" is gone. The Vacuum Tube, Programmable Computer, Integrated Circuit, Radiation Shielding and Solar Panel lose their elemental renames. Semiconductor Mastery gets its familiar name back in atomic worlds (it had become the neutral "Substrate Mastery"); in elemental worlds it is Resonant Crystal Mastery — one name, since there it is a different craft. The Nuclear Submarine and Atomic Bomb keep their elemental names, which replace a false assertion.

### v3.52 — Mechanical Grounding, Batch 6 (Quintessic, Quantum, Relativity)

- **49 breakthroughs grounded**, the elemental frontier alongside its atomic mirrors so that each pair carries identical stats: Deep Matter Lab ↔ Nuclear Physics Lab, Fineness Laboratory ↔ Electron Microscope, Grand Athanor ↔ Particle Accelerator, Codex Hall ↔ Standard Model Institute, Dimensional Loom ↔ Dimensional Scanner, Vacuum Tap ↔ Casimir Generator, Exotic Matter Forge ↔ Exotic Matter Lab, Compact Athanor ↔ Compact Fusion, Great Work Reactor ↔ the Fusion Plant to come. Elemental power stays fuel-free where the physics says so (Bound-Energy Laboratory, Great Work Reactor), and the warhead keeps the Atomic Bomb's Uranium requirement, read as Heavy Ore.
- **The Particle Accelerator and Grand Athanor are national wonders**, so that the FTL evidence they carry is never first-come-first-served.
- **World-Truth ruleset derivation stated in §7.0** — removal, multipliers, remapping base-game objects to their mirrors, world-dependent display names, and tier overrides — so that the Quintessic Warhead can use the real Manhattan Project and Atomic Bomb, and Uranium can be revealed as Heavy Ore by the Boundary Survey.
- **Nonexistent mechanics removed:** probe movement (Celestial Current Survey, Current Sail), trade goods, MAD, "cities that fly", forest regrowth. **Vague prerequisites grounded:** the Particle Accelerator's "large energy supply" (now the Power Grid), Entanglement's "experimental validation" (now the Laser), Gravitic Lift's "large energy supply" (now Superconductivity). The "+X% Insight toward FTL" accelerators are now cost multipliers, as §1.2 defines them.

### v3.51 — Lodes Gated by Knowledge; a Lost Token Source Restored

- **Each Transmuted Lode requires the breakthrough that reveals its resource** (`techRequired`): Iron, Gold and Gems at Ore Identification, Silver at Deep Shaft Mining, Coal at Coal Discovery, Oil at Petroleum Discovery, Aluminum at Aluminium Extraction, Rare Earths at Rare Earth Extraction. Instant construction honours it — the action is enabled only when `canBuildImprovement` reports no problem, and `MissingTech` is one. In practice Oil, Aluminum and Rare Earths are real gates; the rest are known before transmutation.
- **Bug fixed (introduced in v3.26):** Substance worlds had no source of MATTER_THEORY. The grant lived on Systematic Element Analysis and was dropped when that row became the Analytical Laboratory, so every breakthrough naming the Periodic Table — Organic Chemistry, Aluminium and Rare Earth Extraction, Cryogenics, Spectroscopy and nine more, and through Spectroscopy the whole elemental computing path — was unreachable in Substance worlds. Transmutation Practice now grants it ("the Elemental Codex"), mirroring Five-Phase Theory in Process worlds. The downstream sweep missed this because it assumed every token has a source in every world; a per-world token-source audit now runs alongside it.

### v3.50 — Transmutation Re-locks into the Land

- **The seven Transmuted buildings are replaced by lodes.** Since the Hall became a national wonder, each building could be built exactly once, capping lifetime transmutation at about 2,000 matter against a ~2,800–3,700 budget. Now a matter-bought **Mineral Crucible** (240) or **Noble Crucible** (400) — buy-only civilians on the Bullion pattern, sold in the Hall's city — walks to any land tile in your territory and consumes itself to build a **Transmuted Lode**: +2 Iron, Coal, Oil, Aluminum or Rare Earths, or one Gold Ore, Silver or Gems.
- **Verified in code:** lodes have no `turnsToBuild`, so Workers cannot build them (only instant construction places improvements without build time) but can repair them; they stand in owned territory (`OutsideBorders`); pillaged lodes stop providing. Transmutation is now repeatable and limited by land and matter, not by city count, and every lode costs a tile.
- **Noble lodes are Gold, Silver and Gems** in place of the made luxuries Jewelry and Porcelain: a lode is mineral, and lead into gold is the alchemist's defining act.

### v3.49 — One New Mechanic per Elemental World; Matter Budget Reset

- **The Great Alchemist, the Athanor and the Alchemist specialist are removed.** Each elemental world now has one new mechanic: Substance a new *resource* (Prima Materia), Process a new *Great Person* (the Great Sage and its Turnings). The Alchemist's slots become ordinary ones — Scientist in the Hall, Merchant in the Vats, one of each in the Philosopher's Stone (which gives Great Merchant points).
- **Matter sources reset to absorb the loss of the Athanors** (which were the largest late-game source): the Hall provides 6 a turn (was 4), and the Philosopher's Stone provides a flat 6 a turn — replacing its "half of every Market's gold", which the converter reads as half of the Market's own 2 Gold, i.e. one matter a turn. A whole game's matter is now ~2,800 for an eight-city empire (~3,700 with the Stone), with a three-city tall civilisation within a few per cent: the Vats lean wide, Dispersal in the Hall's city leans tall.
- With no gold → matter route left, Bullion's exchange is one-way and pump-safe by construction.

### v3.48 — One-to-One Feeding; the Great Wheel Feeds Two

- **Turnings rebalanced for the counts players reach.** At five to eight Great Sages a game, "+1 per feeder and +3% empire-wide" was an ordinary Great Improvement with an invisible percentage, and the cycle itself was almost inert. Each Turning is now +3 of its stat on its tile, and each *fed* Turning grants one stacking, policy-sized effect of its phase: Wood +15% growth, Fire +15% Production on buildings, Earth −20% culture cost of border growth, Metal +10% Strength for military units, Water +15% Great Person generation.
- **Feeding is one-to-one**, counted as `min(beneficiaries, feeders)`: one Water no longer feeds every Wood, so piling one element behind a single feeder earns nothing, and progress beyond the first wheel is incremental — each extra Turning placed behind an extra of its feeder adds one effect.
- **The Great Wheel changes the rule rather than adding a percentage: each Turning feeds two.** Folded into the same counts as `(1 + [[The Great Wheel] Buildings])`, so no second set of uniques is needed; its value scales with the Great Sage investment it rewards. It keeps +5% of every yield per complete wheel as a baseline for one-wheel civilisations.

### v3.47 — Water Turnings on the Coast

- The Turning of Water may now stand on coastal land as well as beside fresh water. Unciv filters offer AND (`{A} {B}`) and NOT (`non-[X]`) but no OR, and several placement uniques are combined with AND (`TileImprovementFunctions`, line 125), so the rule is written `non-[{non-[Fresh Water]} {non-[Coastal]}]` — legal under the parameter docs' nesting rule (no AND inside an AND).

### v3.46 — Qi Removed; the Five Turnings Become Great Sage Improvements

- **Qi cut as a resource.** Its only sinks were the Five Turnings and the Wandering Sage; it was not earning its place. The Cycle Forge, Cycle Garden, Wandering Sage and every Qi cost and source are gone; the Forge is now +4 Production with a Sage slot and Great Sage points.
- **The Five Turnings are Great Improvements**, each built by consuming a Great Sage and each only on its element's land (Wood: Forest/Jungle; Fire: Desert/Plains/Volcanic; Earth: Grassland/Flood plains; Metal: Hill/Tundra/Snow; Water: land beside fresh water — and, from v3.47, the coast). Each yields +2 of its stat on its tile, +1 more for every owned Turning of the element that feeds it (Water→Wood→Fire→Earth→Metal→Water), and +3% of its stat empire-wide. All of it is counted natively — `Owned [tileFilter] Tiles` with `<for every [countable]>`, verified in `Tile.kt`, `UniqueMap.kt` and `TileStatFunctions.kt` — with no marker resource. Terrain is enforced by Unciv's own instant-construction check. Pillaging a Turning removes it from every count.
- **The Great Wheel** now pays +5% of every yield *per complete wheel*, counted with the countable language's `min()`; the Great Sage may alternatively be gifted to a City-State for 40 Influence (the gifting code pays before it destroys a Great Person).
- **Latent bug fixed:** Irrigation and Nitrogen Fixation used `Fresh water` (river-adjacent only) where the canonical ruleset uses `Fresh Water` (next to any fresh water, lakes included) in compound tile filters.

### v3.45 — The Flow Cannot Be Forced; Quantitative Audit Recorded

- **Turning the Wheel removed.** A process that pours production into Qi contradicts Model II's sixth axiom — only a pattern *opened* to the Great Cycle draws on it — and it was the main reason the Process gains outran the atomic losses. Qi now comes only from opened patterns (Forge 4/turn, Cycle Garden 2/turn, Great Sage 150, The Great Wheel 150), so a city's wheel takes ~75 turns passively and the +15 yields arrive as a Modern-era ramp. Substance keeps Dispersal (matter can be forced from work, at a loss); Caloric keeps Banking Heat (work into heat is Joule's own experiment). The fork's process extension now serves two stockpiles, not three.
- **Quantitative audit recorded in §7.0:** what elemental worlds genuinely lose (~+30 Science, +16–56 Production, +25% spaceship production, one missile tier, mostly late) against what Process gains (+120 yields across eight cities, paced), with the Turnings' yield and cost named as the next levers.

### v3.44 — Multipliers Are Situational

- The ×1.5 on Transmutation Practice and Five-Phase Theory is removed: both exist only in their own worlds, so there is no baseline cost for a multiplier to act on — their difficulty belongs in their thresholds, set when thresholds are assigned. §1.2 now states the rule: a multiplier expresses how much harder *the same* breakthrough is in a given world; world-exclusive breakthroughs are priced directly. Miniaturisation's ×2.5 stands — it is the same breakthrough in both worlds.

### v3.43 — Cost Multipliers as a Balance Lever

- **New rule in §1.2:** a breakthrough's Insight threshold may carry multipliers keyed to World-Truth values. Impossible physics removes a breakthrough; difficult engineering multiplies its cost. Several existing effects were already this lever informally (the Antikythera/Clockwork halving of Babbage, the +50% Insight toward Quantum Tunnelling from Loop Quantum Gravity and Bio-Quantum Coherence, Phlogiston Theory's Chemistry bonus) and are now named as such.
- **Miniaturisation restored to Elemental worlds at ×2.5** — Model I says resonance computing is *slower* to miniaturise, not unable — so pocket electronics and Powered Armour arrive late rather than never. Photonics and Quantum Computing remain removed: no photons to count, no discreteness to compute with.
- **The elemental gateways cost ×1.5** (Transmutation Practice, Five-Phase Theory) — the gentler alternative to moving them an era later, and now first in the list of levers in §7.0.

### v3.42 — The Familiar World Is the Baseline; the Silicon Age Is Atomic

- **Fissile Material withdrawn.** The atomic world should feel like base Civ V, not acquire a new — and almost wholly military — stockpile economy. The Nuclear focus is re-grounded on familiar objects: Uranium-gated units and reactors, the Breeder and Advanced Fuel Cycles as Uranium resource-production bonuses (the Arabian-Oil pattern), Thorium and Micro-Reactors as plant-less power.
- **What the physics says elemental worlds lack — taken seriously instead of mirrored.** Model I calls resonance computing "slower to miniaturise than silicon" and forbids photon-counting; so Miniaturisation (EN10) and Photonics (EN08) now require the Transistor, the elemental face of Substrate Mastery is a Resonant Crystal Works that does not shrink (+2 Gold, no Moore's law), Powered Armour follows Miniaturisation into the atomic world, and the Compact Athanor is reached through Robotics rather than Miniaturisation. An elemental world builds computers; it never gets the silicon age that base Civ V hands every player.
- **Elemental engines brought down:** the Transmutation Hall is a national wonder — one per civilisation, so matter scales with land and people through the Vats, Athanors and Dispersal, never with city count — and the Cycle Forge yields 4 Qi rather than 6 (a passive wheel in seventy-five turns, faster only by spending production).
- §7.0's parity paragraph rewritten around the principle: the familiar world is the baseline; elemental worlds are brought to it, never the reverse; the remaining levers are named.

### v3.41 — Process Parity and the Atomic Engine

- **Process brought level with Substance** along Unciv's own depth vectors, in its idiom: the **Sage** specialist (+1 Food, +1 Culture; Great Sage points), the **Great Sage** (150 Qi, or plants a **Cycle Garden** — a Great Improvement providing 2 Qi with food and culture), the **Wandering Sage** (bought with 40 Qi; 30 Culture on consumption, or 6 Influence gifted to a City-State), and Great Harmony grounded as **The Great Wheel** — a world wonder whose reward is *completed wheels*: every city that has turned all five Turnings works a tenth harder in every yield, and each luxury type gives a point of happiness. Balance, made a payoff.
- **The atomic world given its own exclusive economy.** The audit of what elemental worlds lack found only late theory and second-tier energy — nothing to weigh against alchemy. The ontology supplies the answer: discreteness alone makes fuel *countable*. **Fissile Material** is a stockpile enriched from Uranium (Enrichment Facility; the Enrichment process at 4 → 1), bred and extended by the fuel-cycle chain that was unmirrored theory (Breeder +3, Thorium +2 with no Uranium, Advanced Fuel Cycles +50%), and spent on the Atomic Bomb (60), Nuclear Missile (100), Nuclear Plant (40), nuclear hulls (30), Micro-Reactor (20), Cascade Reactor (60) and Giant Death Robot (80). No Bullion: fissile wealth buys power and nothing else. The Nuclear Physics focus is grounded in the process; NP08's nonexistent Thorium resource is dropped in favour of the breakthrough as gate.
- **World-type parity stated in §7.0**, with the one remaining asymmetry named — the elemental engines open three eras earlier — and the lever to pull if that snowballs.

### v3.40 — Matter for Tall Civilisations

- One Hall per city meant matter scaled with city count while its uses (strategic resources, luxuries) are civilisation-wide — a one-city Venice could never compete. Three native sources now scale with population and land instead: the **Alchemist** specialist (new `Specialists.json` entry: +1 Science, +1 Gold, Great Alchemist points 3; slots in the Hall, Reserve Vats and Philosopher's Stone); the **Great Alchemist** (a new Great Person by the standard recipe) who yields 120 Prima Materia or builds an **Athanor**, a Great Improvement providing 3 Prima Materia a turn from a tile with no upkeep; and the Reserve Vats' mine-head dispersal (50% of the city's worked-mine production, summed before truncation). With Dispersal scaling on production, a tall civilisation has four matter levers that scale with what it has most of. Qi and Caloric need no such fix: their sinks (Turnings, Heat Exchanges) are per-city, so their demand scales with cities as their supply does.

### v3.39 — Filter Discipline

- `Building.matchesFilter` matches a building by name, by `replaces`, and by the name of any technology it requires (`Building.kt` line 529). The TH11 breakthrough shared its name with the Caloric Reservoir building, so `<in cities with a [Caloric Reservoir]>` would have matched the Heat Exchange as well. TH11 is renamed **Caloric Storage**; a scan found the other three name-shares (Windmill, Water Mill, Coal Power Plant) harmless because only their own buildings require them. Rule added to §7.0.
- The `replaces`-match is put to use: the Solar Reservoir declares `replaces: Caloric Reservoir` with no `uniqueTo`, so every `[Caloric Reservoir]` filter — Banking Heat's availability, the Heat Exchange's exclusion — covers both Reservoirs with one clause. The only `replaces` buildability check concerns `uniqueTo` buildings (line 374), so both remain buildable.

### v3.38 — The Hall Makes Matter, Not Goods; Bullion Buy-Only and Immediate

- **Transmutation Hall** now `Provides [4] [Prima Materia]` with no production and 2 Gold upkeep — the alchemists who are not at the forge. Verified in `TurnManager.kt`: a civilisation gains its supply of each stockpiled resource every turn, and `Provides` is supply, so no stat needs converting. (Negative production would also work — the engine sums building stats without a floor — but upkeep is more legible; specialists cannot yield stockpiles natively, so an Alchemist slot would be a further extension.) The Cycle Forge keeps its production *and* `Provides [6] [Qi]` — the Process gain stated plainly; the Reservoirs become pure banks (`Provides [4]` / `[6]`).
- **Transmuted Bullion is buy-only** (`Unbuildable`; `Can be purchased for [1] [Gold]` + `Costs [40] [Prima Materia]` — the Faith-bought-Missionary pattern with matter as the price) and carries `Can move immediately once bought`, without which a purchased unit has no movement that turn (`BaseUnit.construct` line 376) and the consume action, gated on movement (`UnitActionsFromUniques` line 267), would wait a turn. Stockpile costs are charged on purchase, there is no per-turn purchase limit, and placement uses the nearest free tile — several carts a turn, cashed as bought, without touching the queue.
- **City-State gift raised to 6 Influence.** Gold gifts exist only in 250/500/1000, so a 30-Gold cart already buys a denomination gold cannot at a better rate per coin; the sixth point is the premium for the journey, so cashing the cart and gifting coin is never simply better. Higher would let Dispersal-fed carts hold alliances from one city.

### v3.37 — Banking Processes: Dispersal, Turning the Wheel, Banking Heat

- Three Wealth-style perpetual processes let a city bank its surplus into a stockpile for as long as it chooses, each available only where the producing building stands: **Dispersal** (2 Production → 1 Prima Materia, in Transmutation Hall cities), **Turning the Wheel** (1 → 1 Qi, in Cycle Forge cities — no loss on the way in, the physics' gain), **Banking Heat** (2 → 1 Caloric, in Reservoir cities). Rates are anchored to Wealth's 4 → 1: banked matter cashed as Bullion turns production into Gold at ⅜ (half again better than Wealth, plus resource optionality), while matter rush-bought back returns about a tenth — a reserve, never a substitute for building. This is the seventh fork extension recorded in §7.0: `PerpetualStatConversion` extended to target a stockpiled resource, a parameter type Unciv already has.

### v3.36 — The Matter Economy Balanced Against Unciv's Formulas

- Read from source: rush-buying costs `(30 × production)^0.75` Gold (2.5–4.6 per point), the Wealth process converts at ¼, and City-State gold gifts pay `gift^1.01/9.8` Influence decaying to a minimum of 5. Against these, two corrections: Transmuted Bullion's production cost drops from 10 to a token 1 (at 10, cashing 600 matter would have cost the city 150 Production — more than the unit it bought), and its City-State gift falls from 15 to the base 5 Influence (at 15, five Halls could have allied every City-State on the map continuously; at 5 it is a mild, widening edge).
